In a code session, `/mcp` and the start of a name lists the running servers; Tab completes; sending it gives the session that server through `setMcpServers` (with the Companion given again; docs/sdk-notes.md), again after a restart. **Why a bridge:** this laptop's policy loads no stdio or http server in a session, so each session gets an in-process server (`apps/host/src/mcp-builder/bridge.ts`) that passes the list and each call to the server's process; calls reach canUseTool as `mcp__built-<name>__<tool>`, so the floor, the rules and the audit decide them, an unknown server's first calls ask, and Remember can allow them. Each change on the page and each /mcp is recorded first (`mcp_server_*`). No new dependency (the MCP SDK was already the Host's). **Decided by me under the standing instruction, for the owner to confirm:** a server starts off by default, a stopped server cannot be given to a session, and Remove keeps its folder. Threat T129. Tests: mcp-builder (a real server process: starter, bridge, credentials, failure, caps, the list, and a real session), mcp-servers (the page and /mcp completion). On the Mac, where no job object is made, a server runs without caps and the page says so.
- **E-094, the escalation ladder (the owner's priority 3, remote notifications):** Settings, Phone and notifications, Who is told, and when: how many minutes the phone, and separately ntfy and Slack, wait before they are told of a request (0 to 60; at once by default, saved as no setting). The desktop is always told at once. The broker holds each channel's notice until its wait is over (`ladder` in `broker.ts`, a `push_due` event the push channels now listen for), and a request answered first never reaches them. No deadline moves, and an answer from any channel counts at any time. Read for each request (`escalationLadder`, a live setting). Tests: broker (the ladder, a request answered first, and no ladder as before), settings-ladder, ntfy, slack.
- **E-095, pausing a project from the phone (the owner's priority 3, remote notifications):** a session on the passkey phone app has **Pause all in <project>…**: one signed envelope (`pause_project`, a fresh nonce and a short life, naming only the project) pauses every open session in that project on the computer, with the reason `phone`; sessions in other projects keep running. It is recorded first (`project_paused_from_phone`), and as a stopping power it works with the kill switch on, and pauses even if the record cannot be written (the phone is told). Resuming is only Start now on the desktop: there is no envelope for it (one that tries is refused as not the expected shape), and no limit reset or bypass starts a phone pause again. **Decided by me under the standing instruction, for the owner to confirm:** pausing goes ahead when it cannot be recorded, as the kill switch does. Threat T130. Tests: relay-e2e, limit-pause.
- **O37, the wing on Windows (owner, 16 Nov: "Where a feature pushed to github appears to be mac only I always want you to take the code and make a windows version of the feature as well"):** David's wing beside the Mac's notch now has a Windows version. While something waits on you, a small black pill sits at the top centre of the screen the pointer is on, with how many and a dot for the highest risk; a click opens the list with each request's project, what it is and its risk (never the command) and a row's own Deny and Allow once; a Severe request, a question or a plan opens the main window on Approvals. It is the same page as the Mac's (`?view=notch`, `NotchMac.tsx`), told `notch-place:free`, in a window of its own (`packaging/Wing.cs`) that never takes the focus or a taskbar button (WS_EX_NOACTIVATE, a tool window), follows the pointer from screen to screen each second, and loads only Codehouse's own page (the main window's guard). The tray's **Show requests at the top of the screen** turns it off, remembered as `wing-off` in the data folder. Built with the C# 5 compiler like the rest of the window (checked to compile). Tests: wing-win (the page's messages and the window's handling stay in step), notch-mac. Not yet seen on screen: the owner's first look will say whether the pill sits well.
- **O42, a Timeline of ten-minute summaries (owner, 16 Nov: "For every session I want AI to produce a summary every 10 mins. The user doesnt see this unless they want to. To see the summary there is a timeline button on the bar at the top. It opens timeline that can be scrolled left and right. When a user presses one of the nodes (that shows a date and time stamp) it shows the summary of what happened in the 10 mins prior to that"):** the Host notes what each session does (your messages, Claude's words, and each tool it ran with a short look at its target) and every ten minutes Claude sums up each session that did something since its last summary (`apps/host/src/session-timeline.ts`, `timeline-writer.ts`: one Haiku turn, no tools, the activity marked as text to sum up). Kept in `timelines/<session>.json`, the last 500 a session, read tolerantly. **Timeline** on a session's bar opens a strip scrolled to the newest; each node shows its date and time, and pressing one shows its summary and how much happened. The session menu's earlier Timeline, every call from the audit log (C-010), is now **Audit trail...**, so the two are not confused. Like the recap: redacted first, never for a model on this computer or an evaluation, only for an account that passed the organisation check, audited by size (`timeline_summary_written`). On unless turned off in Settings, Sessions, Timeline (`timelineSummaries: false`). **Decided by me under the standing instruction:** on by default as asked, though it uses a little of the plan every ten minutes for each working session; summaries are written in the Host, so they are there whether or not the window was open. Threat T131. Tests: session-timeline, summary-timeline, settings-ladder.
- **T-002, open a project from a terminal:** `Codehouse.exe <folder>` (or `--open <folder>`) opens Codehouse on the start-session form with that folder filled in, in the copy already running too (the folder is left as a full path in `open-folder.txt` in the data folder, read once and removed, and the running window is asked to show). Only a folder that exists is taken; nothing starts until you say what to build, so a command run in a terminal cannot start work by itself. `--help` lists it. Tests: open-folder (the page), open-folder-win (the window's side, checked to compile). The installer does not add Codehouse to PATH, so run it by its full path or add the folder yourself. Also corrected in docs/plan/t3code-status.md: T-025 (snooze) was already built, and T-006, T-108 and T-109 are built in part.
- **T-020, a suggested next prompt:** sessions start with the SDK's `promptSuggestions` on (docs/sdk-notes.md): after a turn, Claude Code's own suggestion of what to ask next shows in the empty prompt box with "(Tab to use it)"; Tab fills it in, and it is never sent by itself. It goes as soon as the session works again. Off in Settings, Sessions, Suggested next prompt (`promptSuggestions: false`, for sessions started from then). Tests: prompt-suggestion (web), prompt-suggestions (Host). **T-005** (drop a folder on the sidebar) waits: a page is never given a dropped folder's path, so it needs the window to take the drop; noted in docs/plan/t3code-status.md.
- **T-017, remembered choices for a new session:** the start-session form starts with the model, effort and mode of your last session, and a folder you started in before with what you chose there (its project default), kept in the window (`lib/newSessionChoices.ts`). **Decided by me under the standing instruction:** an Extreme approval mode is never remembered, so a new session never answers everything by itself unless you choose that each time; it starts in Manual. Tests: new-session-choices.
- **T-016, your own models:** Settings, Sessions, Your own models: up to 20 lines of `id = Display name`, for a newer model before Codehouse lists it or one your gateway serves. Each shows in every model picker (start a session, start a chat, a session's own picker, compare) after the built-in ones, and one with a built-in id renames it (`apps/web/src/lib/models.ts`). The id is checked against a strict pattern in the settings schema (`customModels`), and the list is saved only when every line is right. Tests: custom-models.
- **T-024, finished sessions settle:** a session that has ended and not been touched for three days is archived by the Host, the way Archive does it (Earlier sessions, Show archived, still has it; Unarchive brings it back), looked at every hour whether or not the window is open (`apps/host/src/session-settle.ts`). Settings, Sessions, Settle finished sessions: Never, 1, 3, 7 or 30 days (`settleAfterDays`, absent for 3). **Decided by me under the standing instruction:** a pinned session never settles, which is the per-session Never, rather than a new flag of its own; settling after a merged pull request is left for later. Tests: session-settle, settings-ladder.
- **T-053, the send key:** Settings, General, Sending a prompt: Enter sends (the default, as before: Shift+Enter adds a line), or Ctrl+Enter (Command on a Mac) sends and Enter adds a line, for long prompts. Kept in the window like the appearance (`lib/sendKey.ts`); never sends while an input method is composing; the prompt box's tooltip says which. Tests: send-key.
- **T-031, copy a session's id:** a session's menu in the sidebar has Copy session id, the id Claude Code knows it by (for `claude --resume`), once it has one. No link: Codehouse registers no address scheme with Windows. Tests: session-id.
- **T-116, open a file in your editor:** the file viewer has Open in VS Code. The Host opens VS Code's own link (`vscode://file/<path>:<line>`, or Cursor's `cursor://`) with what Windows has for it, as one argument, with no shell (`openLink` in `files-api.ts`); only a file of the session's, as Show in Explorer allows, and never one the policy blocks, since a secret would open in plain view. JetBrains is left for later. Tests: files.
- **T-128, prices of your own:** Settings, Usage, Prices of your own: `model = input, output` (dollars a million tokens, then cache read and write if you like) works that model's cost out from its tokens in the Usage report, for a model through a gateway that reports none or the wrong one; `model as other-model` counts one model's figures as another's. Applied to the report as it is read (`apps/host/src/model-pricing.ts`), never to what was recorded, so taking a line away brings back what Claude Code reported. Saved only when every line is right (`modelPricing`, at most 50). Tests: model-pricing, custom-models.
- **T-027, a Working section:** the sidebar lists the coding sessions working on their own under Working, after Pinned (`groupByFolder`, and `railOrder` so Ctrl+1 to 9 count as shown). One that waits on you, a pinned one or a chat stays where it was. Fold it to keep busy sessions out of the way; the fold is kept like a folder's. **Decided by me under the standing instruction:** it starts open rather than folded, so a session that is working is never out of sight until you choose (the existing sidebar tests rely on seeing it). Tests: rail-working, rail.
- **T-030, search from the palette:** Ctrl+K knows each session by its title, project, folder, worktree branch and the pull requests it opened (`lib/sessionSearch.ts`), and shows its title and branch; and a Said in sessions group finds your words and Claude's in the sessions open here, from three letters, newest first, with a snippet, opening the session. Older sessions are still searched on Sessions, Earlier sessions (Search what was said). Tests: session-search, palette.
- **T-045, compact an idle session:** beside a session's context meter, after 50 minutes idle with a conversation worth compacting (60% full or more, the meter's own mark), Codehouse offers Compact, so the next prompt reads a summary rather than the whole conversation again, or Keep, which holds until the next turn (`shouldOfferCompact`). /compact and the meter's Compact were already there. **Decided by me:** 50 minutes, as the cache Claude Code keeps on a plan may last up to an hour; it is offered, never done by itself. Tests: idle-compact.
- **E-016, remote database guard:** a database client (psql, pg_dump, pg_restore, mysql, mysqldump, sqlcmd, mongosh, mongo, mongodump, mongoexport, redis-cli) pointed at a host that is not this computer escalates under `remote-database` at risk 75, with the host shown; a host that looks like production (a separate prod, production, prd or live in its name) is 90, Severe. The host is read from flags, URIs, libpq connection strings and PGHOST, MYSQL_HOST and SQLCMDSERVER set before the command (`packages/policy/src/database.ts`, in the engine, so the floor hook in Auto and Accept edits sees it too). A client with no host, or localhost, 127.x, ::1 or a local SQL Server instance, is decided by the rules as before. Passwords are never in the reason, and the redactor already takes them out of what is recorded. **Following the owner's decisions of 14 and 15 Nov over the spec's "Extreme never approves":** Rules-based Extreme approval waits on it at Severe (its limit is 80); Extreme max approval approves it. Production patterns of your own are left for later; the built-in ones serve E-021 too. Threat T132. Tests: database, golden 24 (policy lines 97%).
- **E-021, production context for cloud command lines:** a command that changes things with kubectl (or oc), helm, az, aws or gcloud, aimed at a context, profile, subscription, resource group, project, configuration or namespace that looks like production (the same names as E-016), escalates as Severe under `production-context` (90), naming it (`packages/policy/src/cloud-context.ts`, in the engine). Only the name written in the command or set before it (`AWS_PROFILE`, `CLOUDSDK_CORE_PROJECT`) is read: no kubeconfig, credentials or config file is opened, so a context chosen elsewhere is not seen and the cloud rules judge the change as before (a known limit). Looking (get, logs, list, show, ls) is unchanged. As E-016: Rules-based Extreme approval waits on it, Extreme max approval approves it. Threat T133. Tests: cloud-context, golden 25.
- **E-017, bulk data export guard:** a bulk export escalates under `bulk-export`, naming what it takes and where it writes: pg_dump and pg_dumpall, mysqldump, mongoexport and mongodump, bcp ... out and queryout, sqlcmd -o, COPY ... TO and SELECT ... INTO OUTFILE in a query on the command line, and Export-Csv when the same pipeline runs a database query (`Get-Process | Export-Csv` is not one). Risk 60 inside the project, 75 outside it, as the spec asks (`packages/policy/src/data-export.ts`, in the engine). The target is the tool's own output flag, or a redirect read from the whole command line, as the parsed segments leave redirects out. Threat T134. Tests: data-export, golden 26.
- **E-030, toolchain guard:** a change to the tools every project here uses escalates under `toolchain-change` at High (80), naming it, whatever pack is on: nvm and fnm install, use, default, alias and uninstall; rustup (other than show, which, check, doc); pyenv install, global and uninstall; the dotnet-install scripts; and global installs with npm, pnpm, yarn and dotnet tool (`packages/policy/src/toolchain.ts`, in the engine). **Decided by me:** pip install and Set-ExecutionPolicy are left to the rules that already hold them (dependency-new, package-install and the refusal of an invented package name; pwsh-execution-policy), which say more about them; the guard's first version outranked those and was taken back. As E-016, Rules-based Extreme approval's limit (80) holds it; Extreme max approval approves it. Threat T135. Tests: toolchain, golden 27.
- **E-023, Kubernetes manifest guard:** an Edit, Write or MultiEdit of a Kubernetes manifest (a YAML file with apiVersion and kind) or Helm values (`values*.yaml`) that adds a privileged container, a hostPath volume, hostNetwork, hostPID, runAsUser 0, runAsNonRoot false, allowPrivilegeEscalation true or a Secret with stringData escalates under `kubernetes-manifest` at Severe (85), naming each (`packages/policy/src/k8s-manifest.ts`, in the engine). Only what the edit adds counts: a Write is compared with the file as it is, an Edit with the text it replaces. **Decided by me:** a latest tag and a container without limits are noted in the reason but do not hold an edit on their own (the spec holds only Severe findings); the check reads the text, not a YAML parser, so a setting written in flow style is not seen (a known limit, recorded). Extreme max approval approves it; Rules-based Extreme waits (85 is over its limit of 80). Threat T136. Tests: k8s-manifest, golden 28.
- **E-011, destructive migration guard:** an Edit, Write or MultiEdit of a database migration that adds DROP TABLE, DROP COLUMN, TRUNCATE, DELETE with no WHERE, a column type changed to a bounded type, NOT NULL with no default (or SET NOT NULL), or a rename with no view kept for the old name escalates under `destructive-migration` at Severe (85), showing up to five statements (`packages/policy/src/migration.ts`, in the engine). Migrations are found by place and words: SQL under a migrations, db/migrate, flyway or changelog folder, Prisma's migration.sql, Flyway's `V1__name.sql`; Liquibase XML and YAML changelogs; EF Core's migrationBuilder (and its Sql); Alembic's op (and execute); Knex's schema builder (and raw). **Decided by me:** no SQL parser was added: the spec asks for tree-sitter or a pinned pure JS parser "with a stated reason", and the reason against is that a grammar for every dialect is a large dependency to say the same about these few statements, so the guard strips comments, cuts at semicolons and matches the ORM calls by name (a semicolon inside a string can split a statement, a known limit). "Type narrowing" is a change to a bounded type (varchar(n), char(n), int, smallint, numeric(p), date and the like), since the old type is not in the edit; widening to bigint or text passes. A Knex notNullable counts only inside alterTable or table, not createTable. The spec's "Extreme never approves these" is overridden by the owner's 14 and 15 Nov decisions: Extreme max approval approves it; Rules-based Extreme waits (85 is over its limit of 80). Threat T137. Tests: migration, golden 29 (15 destructive, 10 safe).
- **E-028, WSL command coverage:** the Linux command a `wsl` call runs (after -d, -u, --cd and the other options; `-e`/`--exec` words quoted back as they were, `--` or a plain command line passed as it stands) is parsed by the Bash parser and decided by the same rules and floor, from the Bash and the PowerShell tool alike; so is a `bash -c` (sh, zsh and the like) run from PowerShell, which nothing unwrapped before (`packages/policy/src/wsl.ts`, `analyse.ts`). An unparseable inner command escalates (parse error), a dynamic one is refused (obfuscated-shell), and nesting past three levels is refused as dynamic. WSL's management (--list, --install, --shutdown) and a bare `wsl` run no command and are left to the rules. The floor's secrets-files rule adds `.kube` and `.config/gcloud` under `\\wsl$` and `\\wsl.localhost` (`.ssh` and `.aws` were already denied anywhere). **Decided by me:** the `wsl` segment itself matches no allow rule, so `wsl -- git status` asks even though `git status` alone is allowed: a command in another system's environment never runs unasked (stricter, for the owner to confirm). `.kube` and gcloud are added for WSL homes only, not everywhere, so a Windows kubectl workflow is not newly refused. Threat T138. Tests: wsl, golden 30.
- **E-093, card data guard:** a number that could be a real payment card (13 to 19 digits, together or in groups of four, an issuer's start (Visa, Mastercard, Amex, Discover, Diners, JCB, UnionPay) with a length that issuer uses, Luhn-valid, not a documented test card or Stripe's 400000 range) is a new kind in the commit scan, so a session's commit or push that adds one is refused at the floor like a secret, naming the file and line (`apps/host/src/commit-scan.ts`). An Edit, Write or MultiEdit that adds one, logs a card field (pan, cardNumber, cvv and the like, in a console, logger, print or Console.Write call) or stores a CVV escalates under `card-data` at 80, naming the file and line, never the number (`packages/policy/src/card-data.ts`, in the engine). Redaction now takes out any number that could be a card, test cards included, before the audit, the Relay or a log sees it (`redact.ts`). **Decided by me:** the issuer and length check, beyond the spec's Luhn, keeps millisecond times, order numbers and other long numbers from being refused (one in ten would pass Luhn alone); Maestro's 12 to 19 digit ranges are left out for the same reason (a known limit). Logging is matched on one line, so a card field logged across lines is not seen. Threat T139. Tests: card-data, commit-scan.
- **Checkpoints and a glob from a root (fix, 17 Nov):** the WSL globs above made every checkpoint fail, because each secrets glob becomes a git pathspec and git refuses one outside the repository. A checkpoint now leaves out a glob that starts at a root or a drive, which can never name a file in a repository (`packages/shared/src/node/checkpoint.ts`). Found by the gate before release.
- **Ask about code names (owner, 17 Nov):** a question is named first by the code session it asks about ("Gatehouse build: How do I set up CNW..."), once, whichever way it was started (`aboutTitle` in `apps/host/src/sessions.ts`).
- **E-051, merge conflict pre-check:** every five minutes while sessions work in Codehouse's worktrees, `git merge-tree --write-tree --name-only` tries each worktree's branch against its base branch (the checked-out branch for one made from HEAD) and against every other worktree's branch from the same repository (`apps/host/src/conflict-check.ts`). It merges in git's object store only, so no working tree, index or branch changes (the test compares every tree's status, HEAD and refs before and after). A conflict is told once in each session working on either side ("Merge conflict ahead: a and b would conflict in app.ts") and shown on the Worktrees page. **Decided by me:** only committed work is compared, since uncommitted changes are not in a branch yet; a tag or commit base never moves and is not compared; a git older than 2.38 reports nothing rather than guessing. The spec's dashboard warning is the Worktrees page plus each session's transcript; a dashboard tile is left for E-099's health view. Tests: conflict-check, worktrees.
- **Pasted output starting with a path (owner, 17 Nov):** text that starts with /dev/mapper/... or another path was refused as an unknown slash command. A command is now a slash and a name followed by a space or the end; anything else is sent as written (`apps/web/src/lib/isCommand.ts`).
- **E-086, writing rules:** one list in Settings, Privacy (UK spelling and no em or en dashes on by default, the house style; terms always written one way; banned terms) plus a project's `.codehouse/writing-rules.json`, added together, so a project can add rules but not switch one off (`apps/host/src/writing-rules.ts`). A conversation export's prose is corrected after redaction, and the preview lists each change and any banned term, which is never replaced; code (fenced and inline) and web addresses are never touched. An agent's commit message is checked after each session commit and the problems are shown in the session ("Writing rules: the commit message uses the banned term..."); it is never rewritten. **Decided by me:** a dash between words becomes a comma and one between numbers a hyphen; a dash starting a line (a bullet) is left; "program" and "license" are not changed (both have UK uses); summaries, change records and the evidence pack are not yet passed through the rules (the export is the one place Codehouse writes prose a person keeps), for a later step. Tests: writing-rules, export, settings-ladder.
- **CNW installs natively for every kind of address, and the Docker build is fixed (owner, 17 Nov).** A real install on a Debian 13 VM failed. Fixed:
  - the Dockerfile did not copy `scripts/workspace-aliases.mjs`, which the interface's Vite config imports;
  - it also lacked the other workspaces' package.json files, which `npm ci` checks the lockfile against;
  - the updater service had no build section and was pulled from Docker Hub (now `pull_policy: never`);
  - the updater ran `compose -f compose.yml`, which would ignore a `COMPOSE_FILE` in `.env`;
  - the updater's build context, `../..` from `/stack`, was `/` inside its container (now `CNW_REPO_DIR`, its clone).

  The owner now sets only `CNW_HOST` in `.env`. A one-shot `cnw-config` service, from the CNW image, writes caddy's configuration from it (`apps/cnw/src/tls.ts`):
  - an IP address (IPv4 or IPv6) or a home-network name (.home.arpa, .lan, .internal, .local, .localhost, one word) gets caddy's own authority; an IP also gets `default_sni`, because browsers send no server name for one;
  - a public name gets Let's Encrypt;
  - `CNW_TLS` overrides the choice, and `CNW_PORT` and `CNW_HTTP_PORT` publish on other ports, with the port in CNW's public address;
  - `compose.own-proxy.yml`, chosen by `COMPOSE_FILE` in `.env`, switches the bundled caddy off and publishes CNW in plain HTTP; X-Forwarded-For is believed only from `CNW_TRUSTED_PROXY` (a comma list is allowed) and ignored without it.

  Passkeys are refused at an IP address, with the reason, and password sign-in stays. Codehouse pairs to `https://<ip>` and `https://<ip>:<port>` without sending the IP as the TLS server name (`tlsTarget` in `apps/host/src/cnw/pairing.ts`; no DEP0123 warning). The fixed Caddyfile is gone, so no tracked file is edited for setup.

  **Not done here:** the image was not built. This PC has no container tool, and its WSL distribution cannot start ("the user has not been granted the requested logon type", 0x80070569). The owner is to run `docker compose up -d --build` on the Debian VM. **Decided by me:** caddy's configuration is written by a Node service rather than a shell script, so the choice is tested in TypeScript; the own-proxy mode is a second compose file chosen in `.env` rather than a profile, because a profile cannot switch off a service that runs by default. Threats T140, T141. Tests: tls, dockerfile, server (CNW), cnw-pairing.
- **CNW on the owner's Debian 13 VM (17 Nov).** The first real build worked. Then:
  - **Updater health:** the updater was always "unhealthy", because it inherited cnw's health check on 8080. It now answers GET /health on 8090, and compose checks that.
  - **Updater and the clone:** the updater runs as the clone's owner (`CNW_UID` and `CNW_GID`, default 1000), with git told the clone is safe, so it can check out a signed release.
  - **Stuck on "Connecting" (owner found it):** pairing worked, but Codehouse stayed on Connecting. Its socket left certificate checking on, so Node refused caddy's own certificate before the pin was looked at, and the connector retried forever.
  - **The fix:** with a pin, the socket accepts any authority and checks the pin itself on ws's upgrade, before open. On a different certificate it ends there, sends nothing (no hello; nothing is sent until the pin is checked), and stays refused with the reason, which Settings shows ("the certificate changed since you paired").
  - **Pairing:** it now writes its code and public key only after TLS is up and the pin matches, where before it wrote them first and checked the reply. With no label, a pairing is named after the computer (`os.hostname()`) rather than listed as "A computer".
  - **Tests:** they run against a real TLS server with a self-signed certificate made by openssl (Git for Windows' or the Mac's), and skip with a reason where there is none (`cnw-tls.test.ts`). Threat T142.
- **The interface in CNW stayed on its start-up picture (owner, 17 Nov).** CNW serves the interface under /app/, but the build named its script `/assets/...`, which 404s there, so the page never started.
  - **Decided by me:** `base` is `/app/` only when `VITE_CODEHOUSE_REMOTE=cnw` (the CNW image's build), rather than `./` everywhere. The desktop's build is exactly as before (base `/`), so the Host at the root, `?view=quick`, `?view=pane`, the notch view and deep links cannot be affected.
  - **What resolves under /app/ (checked on a real CNW build):** scripts, preloads and the stylesheet are named `/app/assets/...`; lazy chunks are imported relative to the script; the pictures, icon sprite and favicons were already relative (`./codehouse/...`); the socket (`/ws/<computer>`) and the sign-in page (`/`) are CNW's own root paths.
  - **The start-up picture is safe:** if the page's script or stylesheet fails to load (or nothing has started after 20 s), it goes and says Codehouse could not start, instead of hiding the failure.
  - **Tests:** cnw-base (web: the base for each build; the fallback), server (CNW: /app/ and every file it names, 200; root /assets 404).
- **E-100, invariant canaries:** `apps/host/src/canaries.ts` runs eight canaries with no model and no session, at every start of the Host (which an update always restarts, so "after each update" needs no hook of its own) and once a day:
  - the floor (the live policy) denies a Read of a canary .env;
  - redaction masks a canary API key;
  - the Approver client answers escalate, never allow, with a wrong token against the running Approver and against a server that never answers;
  - the listener guard refuses no token, a wrong Host header and a foreign Origin;
  - a canary audit chain verifies with the Node and the PowerShell verifier, and the Node verifier refuses it once one byte is changed;
  - the organisation guard refuses a fake account;
  - the in-process check names a stdio MCP server;
  - the loader refuses a managed policy changed after signing.

  A failure refuses new sessions (`CanaryFailedError` in `SessionManager.start`, after the managed policy check), names the canary, and Doctor shows it first after Claude Code with what to do. Each run is audited as `canary_run`, with the pass and fail counts. A run takes well under 5 s apart from PowerShell's own start (the test times the canaries with that verifier stubbed).

  **Decided by me:**
  - On a Mac without PowerShell 7 the PowerShell verifier is noted, not failed, so that Mac is not refused every session.
  - "Offers safe mode": a failing canary puts Codehouse in safe mode (E-096, below), and the message points to Doctor.

  Each canary has a break check that makes only it fail. Threat T144. Tests: canaries.
- **E-052, structured task results:** `apps/host/src/task-results.ts`.
  - **The schema:** the Companion's `start_session` (hand off a side task) takes an optional `resultSchema`, a JSON Schema as JSON text. It is checked at once (`z.fromJSONSchema`, in the pinned zod 4.6.7, so no new dependency), and the side session is asked to end its last message with one JSON block in that shape.
  - **Checking it:** when the side session finishes its turn, `wait_for_session` takes the last JSON block of the turn's final words and checks it. A valid result is handed over, saying what the side session read (untrusted content, something that looked like prompt injection, or nothing untrusted); `wait_for_session`'s output was already read as untrusted by the session that asked. An invalid one is refused with its errors and sent back to the side session once; invalid again, the task is marked failed.
  - **Decided by me:** the "Hand off a side task" of the spec is `start_session` (T-121), the Companion's way for a session to hand work to another, since there is no other hand-off.
- **E-089, battery-aware scheduling:** `apps/host/src/power.ts`.
  - **Reading:** every two minutes the Host reads the power state itself (Windows: `Win32_Battery`'s BatteryStatus through PowerShell; a Mac: `pmset -g batt`). A reading that fails changes nothing. Each change is audited as `power_state_changed`.
  - **On battery:** a routine that falls due (by time, a commit or files changing) waits, one of each, and runs when mains power is back; the timeline's summaries wait with what happened kept; a new session you did not give a level starts at `low`.
  - **Extreme approval** keeps running on battery unless Settings, Sessions, On battery, "Pause Extreme approval sessions on battery" is on (off by default). Then each working Extreme session pauses (reason `battery`), a limit reset or a bypass never starts it again, and mains power does, where it stopped; you can also start it from its pane.
  - **Deviation:** the spec put the reading in the desktop shell (C#). The Host reads it instead, so the Mac and Windows share one path and no packaging changes.
  - **Decided by me:** routines started by triggers wait too, since they are heavy work Codehouse starts by itself.
  - Tests: task-results, with orchestrate and companion handlers as regressions.
- **E-080, shared signed redaction lists:** `apps/host/src/redaction-lists.ts`.
  - **The list:** a team's list is a JSON file (name, source, date, terms) and an Ed25519 signature over its exact bytes. It is used only when the signature checks out with a key in Settings, Privacy, "Team redaction lists" (trusted keys, as PEM).
  - **Add only:** a list that says to remove words, or a newer copy of the same list that leaves out a word the one before had, is refused, and says so.
  - **Where its words apply:** everywhere redaction runs (the audit log, the phone, exports, logs), because `config.redactTerms` is now your own words and every imported list's together (a getter on the running config). Saving Settings changes only your own words, and the Settings page is sent only yours.
  - **Shown and recorded:** each list is shown with its source, its date and how many words it hides. Each import is audited as `redaction_list_imported`, with the name, source, date and size, never the words.
  - Threat T146. Tests: redaction-lists, settings-ladder.
- **E-099, health score:** `apps/host/src/health-score.ts`, at GET /api/health-score (worked out at most once a minute). It is shown on the dashboard, listing only what needs looking at, and in full in Doctor. Seven factors, each with its last 30 days by day as small bars (described in words for a screen reader) and a button to where it comes from:
  - the safety checks' runs (`canary_run`);
  - unexpected stops (`host_crash_restart`, now written by safe mode's start record);
  - memory refusals (`memory_refused`);
  - flaky test files and gate results (on a developer's computer: `scripts/dev-checks.mjs`, which the test runner and the gate now write to dev-checks.json in the data folder);
  - whether the update check works;
  - whether the audit chain verifies.

  The overall state is the worst factor's, never a weighted score. A factor with nothing to go on (no test runs on an ordinary computer) shows "No data" and does not count. Tests: health-score (host and web), safe-mode.
- **Link clicks no longer break the window (owner, 18 Nov):** a plain link in a chat opened in the browser, but the guard's cancelled navigation then finished as OperationCanceled and the window showed "could not load the Codehouse interface".
  - **The window:** `packaging/CodehouseApp.cs` notes the ids of its own navigations to Codehouse's page as they start (`OwnLoads`), and each finished navigation goes through `NavGuard.Completed` (`packaging/NavGuard.cs`). Only the window's own load (its first or a reload) failing is fatal; a link the guard cancelled is ignored, as is a reload of the page that is up stopped part way. The quick prompt (`Overlay.cs`) uses the same rule, so a cancelled link no longer marks it not ready. Pop-outs and the wing had no such handler; the Mac already ignored a cancelled navigation (`packaging/mac/Page.swift`).
  - **The page:** links that leave Codehouse (web pages and email addresses), in a chat, an artifact, a file shown as Markdown, the Guide and Help, open with `target="_blank"` and `rel="noopener noreferrer"` (`apps/web/src/lib/links.tsx`). They go through the window's NewWindowRequested, which opens your browser and keeps the audit, and in CNW's /app they open a new tab without leaving the session. A link inside the interface is left as it is. NavGuard stays the safety net.
  - **Tests:** `apps/host/test/navguard-exe.test.ts` (the compiled decision and the handlers' wiring) and `apps/web/test/links.test.tsx`; both fail with the fix taken out.
- **CNW updates on its server in one command (owner, 18 Nov): `scripts/update.sh`.** From any folder it works on the clone it sits in (`/opt/gatehouse` on the owner's VM 105): notes the commit, `git pull --ff-only`, then `docker compose --progress plain up -d --build` in `docker/cnw` with the whole build shown (with `compose.own-proxy.yml` too when `.env` sets `COMPOSE_FILE` or `CNW_MODE` for your own proxy), waits up to four minutes for CNW's own health check, and if it fails goes back to the noted commit (`git reset --hard`, then the same build), says ROLLED BACK and exits 1. It prints the old and new commit and CNW's protocol number, which `GET /api/version` now reports. `set -euo pipefail`; it refuses to run over tracked files changed by hand; `sudo` only for docker, only when the user cannot reach Docker. Executable in git. In the user guide (Help, Neighbourhood Watch) and `docs/neighbourhood-watch.md`, Updates. Tests: `apps/cnw/test/update-script.test.ts` (shellcheck when installed, the compose files it names, and two real runs against a throwaway clone with a stand-in for docker: healthy, and a failed health check that rolls back; fails with the rollback taken out). **Decided by me:** the rollback uses `git reset --hard` to the noted commit rather than a detached checkout, so the next run's `git pull` works; it is safe because the script stops first if any tracked file was changed by hand.
- **D-099, the evidence-backed stakeholder summary.** In a session's Evidence, Copy a summary (`GET /api/evidence/summary`, `apps/host/src/stakeholder-summary.ts`) gives Markdown for people who did not watch the change, made from its evidence pack only (D-003), never from Claude's replies: the files and lines, the capabilities it touches and their owners (D-047), the decisions (by a person, by the rules, refused), the commands and how they ended, each claim Codehouse confirmed and how, the sealed checks, dependencies, findings, performance budgets and fitness checks. Every sentence names what it rests on, the pack and the audit records or the part of it. A claim Codehouse could not confirm or found untrue, and an assumption not yet verified, is not stated: it is listed under Not stated: no evidence, with why. Every line is redacted with your list. Tests: `apps/host/test/stakeholder-summary.test.ts` (every sentence has its evidence; the unconfirmed and contradicted claims and the assumption are withheld and listed; redaction); each fails with its part taken out; the evidence pack's tests still pass.
- **D-090, the measured time panel.** On Insights, Measured time per change (`GET /api/insights/time`, `measuredTime` in `apps/host/src/outcomes.ts`): Claude's own time per accepted change, from the usage data's measured durations (D-100's samples), as the 10th to 90th percentile with the median and the sample size, for the period chosen and for your baseline, the same length of time just before it; overall and by project. It says faster or slower only when both periods have at least five sessions with a change and the medians differ by a fifth or more, otherwise about the same or not enough history. It is worked out on this computer from its own data and sent nowhere. Tests: `apps/host/test/outcomes.test.ts` (faster, slower, about the same, too little baseline, a project in only one period) and `apps/web/test/measured-time.test.ts`; each fails with its part taken out.
- **D-086, a local judge for claims.** A done-claim about tests or a fix that passed its check (D-009) is then judged for reward hacks (`apps/host/src/claim-judge.ts`, run from the session's claim check through `claim-judge-run.ts`). A built-in check always runs: a changed source line that branches on a test's own input and returns the value that test expects (`if (n === 7) return 49` beside `expect(square(7)).toBe(49)`, a `case` too), the hack D-001's guard on test files cannot see. When you choose a model in Settings, Other AI providers (Also check Claude's done for reward hacks with), it is asked too, with the claim and the change's diff against HEAD, your redaction list applied; it must be one of the models listed on a runtime on this computer and is refused on a gateway, so the diff never leaves the computer. The judge's outcomes are flag, escalate or none, with no allow: a flag, or an answer it cannot read, or no answer, lowers Verified to Unverified with the reasons in the claim's why and its audit record; a Contradicted or Unverified claim is not asked about and is never raised. **Decided by me:** invariant 15 is about the policy judge and is kept as it is; this judge is a separate, local-only check that can only make a claim less trusted, which is why it may read the diff. Threat model T158. Tests: `apps/host/test/claim-judge.test.ts` (a seeded hard-coded answer in an if and in a switch, the same in a test file, an honest change; every kind of model answer, including allow and verified, gives flag, escalate or none; the lowering table; local, listed and not a gateway; redaction; an unreachable model; on a real session a flagged claim shows Unverified and a failing one is not judged); each fails with its part taken out; the claim, provider and boundary tests still pass.
- **Queue order (decided by me):** D-084 needs C-043 (cross-model review) and C-044 (best of N), neither built yet; it is built after them in queue section 7.
- **D-083, the ubiquitous language glossary.** On Insights, The project's words (`GET /api/insights/glossary`, `apps/host/src/glossary.ts`): the words a project's code names things with (identifiers split at capitals and underscores, plurals folded, common programming words left out), counted by how many distinct names use each; and the clashes, two words for one thing each used in at least two names (customer and client, cart and basket, organisation and organization), with examples and the word most names use. A project can give its own groups in `.codehouse/glossary.yml` (`synonyms: [[client, customer]]`, the word to keep first), which replace any built-in group sharing a word. Names in strings, comments and tests do not count. **Decided by me:** the built-in groups are a short list of nouns and UK and US spellings, not verbs (get and fetch, remove and delete are often deliberate), so a clash is worth reading. Tests: `apps/host/test/glossary.test.ts` (splitting and plurals, a clash with examples and its counts, a word only in a comment, a string or a test, a word in one name only, the project's own groups) and `apps/web/test/glossary.test.tsx`; each fails with its part taken out.
- **D-082, the stale instruction detector.** In the same Insights panel as D-077 (`apps/host/src/stale-instructions.ts`, in `GET /api/insights/context`): what the project's CLAUDE.md or AGENTS.md names in backticks, checked against the project as it is now. A name in code style (camelCase, PascalCase, SCREAMING_CASE or a call) that no code file contains any more is flagged, with the commit that last added or removed it in the code when git finds one quickly (the first five, the instruction files left out of the search); so is a path that is not there, and `npm run x` where no package.json has a script x. Plain words, URLs, placeholders and fenced examples are not checked. **Decided by me:** only backticked terms are checked, because prose mentions too much that is not code; the C-031 outline was not needed for this, a word index of the code files is enough to know a name is gone. Tests: `apps/host/test/stale-instructions.test.ts` (a real repository where a function was removed, a missing file, a missing script, and names, paths and a script that still hold); each fails with the word check, the script check, the fence handling or the instruction files' exclusion taken out.
- **D-081, why is this code like this.** In the file view, under any file read as code, Why is line N like this? (the line at the cursor; `GET /api/files/why`, `apps/host/src/why.ts`) answers from git blame: the commit that last changed the line, with its subject, author and date; that commit's Codehouse note (D-030: the session, its audit record, its evidence pack) or that it has none; and the ADRs that bear on it (D-038): decided in the same session (an ADR's front matter now gives its session to `readAdrs`), naming the commit by hash, or covering the file by its paths. Each sentence of the answer says what it cites; a line not committed yet says so. It goes through the same folder and floor checks as every file route, and runs git with the credentials taken out of its environment. Tests: `apps/host/test/why.test.ts` (a real repository with a noted and an un-noted commit and four ADRs; an uncommitted line; a line past the end); each fails with the session match, the commit match, the evidence citation or the uncommitted case taken out; the file view's and the ADR tests still pass.
- **D-078, claim calibration.** On Insights, under What went wrong, how often each model's done-claims held when Codehouse checked them (D-009): Verified over Verified and Contradicted, with the counts (`claimCalibration` in `apps/host/src/failure-taxonomy.ts`, in `GET /api/insights/failures`). A claim that could not be checked, or one whose tests turned out flaky, is counted but left out of the score, because the tests were at fault, not the claim; under five checked claims the score says it is too few to go on. A claim's audit record now carries the session's model, so it counts for the model that made it (older records fall back to the model the session's other records name). Tests: `apps/host/test/failure-taxonomy.test.ts` (the score, the fallback, the flaky and unchecked cases, too few) and `apps/web/test/calibration.test.ts`; each fails with its part taken out; the claim tests still pass.
- **D-077, the context efficiency report.** On Insights, Instructions a session did not use (`GET /api/insights/context`, `apps/host/src/context-efficiency.ts`): the project's CLAUDE.md, CLAUDE.local.md, AGENTS.md and .claude/CLAUDE.md are split into blocks at their headings (not inside a code fence), and a block counts as used in a session when one of its tool calls named something the block names (a command, path or name in backticks, or a path written out). The blocks never used are listed with their rough size in tokens and how many of the project's other sessions in the last 30 days used each; a block that names nothing checkable is listed apart, never called unused. **Decided by me:** use is judged from the audit log's tool inputs rather than from Claude's words, because what Claude does is what an instruction is for, and the audit is already redacted; the context meter's totals are not split by block in the SDK, so the size is an estimate (four characters a token). Tests: `apps/host/test/context-efficiency.test.ts` (headings and fences, the terms, used and unused against a session and two others, the unchecked block, the token totals); each fails with the fence handling, the path separators, the unchecked case or the unused total taken out.
- **D-076 and D-100, cost per outcome and estimates from history.** On Insights, Cost per change (`GET /api/insights/outcomes`, `apps/host/src/outcomes.ts`), for the period chosen: an outcome is an accepted change, a commit a session made and Codehouse noted (`commit_lineage_noted`, D-030), and its cost is everything spent in the project over those days (the Usage page's figures, with your own prices), so the spend on work later undone, on failing test runs that were run again, and in sessions that ended with no change is in it. Beside it, the rework: undone changes (`files_undone`, `change_undone`, `edit_files_reverted`), failed test runs, and the sessions with no change and what they cost. D-100: each session that made a change is one sample (its spend and Claude's time per change); the estimate is the 10th to the 90th percentile with the median, and says how many sessions it rests on; with fewer than five it says there is not enough history. **Decided by me:** a change is a commit rather than a merged pull request, because Codehouse sees commits and not every project has pull requests; the interval is a percentile range of real samples rather than a fitted distribution, so it never claims more than the history shows. Tests: `apps/host/test/outcomes.test.ts` and `apps/web/test/outcomes.test.ts`; each fails with rework left out of the cost, the threshold lowered, the percentiles changed or an undo kind dropped.
- **Queue order (decided by me):** D-074 needs C-008 (pinning a newer Claude Code) and D-079 needs C-036 (the local codebase index), neither built yet; each is built straight after its prerequisite in queue section 7 rather than as a stub now.
- **D-074, behaviour regression for Claude Code upgrades (22 Nov, feat/d074).** `apps/host/src/behaviour.ts` compares two behaviour reports task by task: blocking when a task's check passed before and fails now, when a call meets a refusal or hold (tool, outcome and rule) it did not meet before, or when a task was not run (the spend cap, or a stop); noted only when the tools used differ. `rangeChangeBlocked` allows `CLAUDE_CODE_RANGE.to` only when it is the baseline's version, or with a committed `docs/behaviour/<version>.json` from that version that shows no blocking change against `docs/behaviour/baseline.json`, and `apps/host/test/claude-range.test.ts` holds the real range to it, so the gate refuses a range change without a clean report. `npm run behaviour:check -- --cap <dollars>` (`apps/host/src/behaviour-run.ts`) is started by the owner only: it runs the real Host (policy, audit, organisation check), replays each task of the owner's `behaviour-tasks.json` in a new scratch folder in Manual mode, refuses whatever would wait for a person (recorded by the broker as any refusal), gives each session what is left of the cap as the SDK's `maxBudgetUsd` (new session option, recorded in `docs/sdk-notes.md`) and starts no task once it is spent, runs each task's own check with no credential in its environment, writes the report atomically and removes the scratch folders. **Decided by me (stricter):** the committed baseline has no tasks yet, and a baseline without tasks blocks any widening, so the owner records one first (owner to-do 7); a task's files may not name a path outside its folder; the cap is at most $100. Tests: `apps/host/test/behaviour.test.ts` (a version that behaves the same is let in with its notes; a seeded regression, a failing check, a new denial and a capped task, blocks; no report, an empty baseline or a report of another version blocks; tolerant reading; the cap reaches the SDK) and the range test; each fails with its code taken out, and widening the range to 2.1.340 fails the range test.
- **D-073, the undo-learning loop.** On Insights, Lessons from your undos and denials (`apps/host/src/lessons.ts`, `lessons-store.ts`; desktop commands `lessons_list` and `lesson_add`): when you undo changes in the same folder (`files_undone`, `change_undone`), or say no yourself to the same command and subcommand, edits in the same folder or the same other tool, three times or more in a project in the last 90 days, a line for its CLAUDE.md is proposed with the audit records it rests on. Adding it goes through D-059: the page sends only the lesson's key and the Host makes the line from the records; it is refused while the project's instructions have a change from outside you have not reviewed (adding would pin it unread); `instruction_learnt` is recorded before the file is written; the line goes under its own heading with a marker so it is not proposed again; and CLAUDE.md is pinned again as written, so it does not block the next session. The rules' or the floor's denials are not counted (they are not you). D-072's failure kinds now count `files_undone` and `change_undone` as undos too (they counted only `edit_files_reverted`). **Decided by me:** a command word and a folder are held to a small set of characters, so a session cannot carry its own words into the instructions (threat model T157); a lesson is added, never removed or edited by Codehouse (editing CLAUDE.md stays yours). Tests: `apps/host/test/lessons.test.ts` (three similar undos propose, two do not; a person's denials of a command, a folder and a tool; the rules and another project are not counted; hostile paths and commands are not learnt; adding is recorded first, pinned, and refused over a pulled change; the heading and order); each fails with its code taken out (the threshold, the person check, the character limit, the outside check, the repin, the record first).
- **D-068, dead code with evidence.** On Insights, Dead code, for the project of an open session (`GET /api/insights/dead-code`, `apps/host/src/dead-code.ts`): in its JavaScript and TypeScript, the named exports no other file names (and their own file names only where declared), the ones only tests name (kept apart: a test keeping code alive is not a use), and the files nothing imports that are not an entry point (a package.json main, bin, exports or scripts, a page's script tag, a config file, an index, a script). Each has its static evidence and, when the project has a coverage report (Istanbul's `coverage-final.json` or `lcov.info` under a coverage folder, the newest one), whether the tests ran it, with the report's age. Removal is proposed a slice at a time, a file or at most five names from one file, each as a task to copy into the session that says what to check first and to run the tests after. It reads files and changes nothing. **Decided by me:** default exports are left out (frameworks call them by convention), so is a package's public entry, and names in a file nothing imports go with that file's slice; a name reached by a string or reflection is not seen, and the panel says so. Tests: `apps/host/test/dead-code.test.ts` (a fixture project with unused, tests-only and live code, an entry from scripts, a dynamic import, a dependency, an Istanbul report and an lcov file; fails with the entry check, the tests-only split, the coverage reading or the slice limit taken out).
- **D-065, test impact analysis.** A coding session's Companion offers `affected_tests` (`apps/companion/src/test-impact.ts`): given the changed files, or none for the working tree's changes since the last commit, it lists the tests that reach them through imports (JavaScript and TypeScript relative imports, re-exports, `import()`, `require()` and `vi.mock()`, at any depth; Python's relative and module imports) and the tests that changed in the same commits in the last 200 (leaving out the first commit, merges and commits of more than 40 files, which say nothing about which test goes with which file). Always a subset of the project's own tests, never from dependencies or builds. It reads files and `git log` and runs nothing, so it is allowed from the Host's in-process Companion in coding sessions only (rule `test-impact-tools`; a chat, or a server of the same name from your settings, asks). Its answer tells Claude to run the whole suite once before saying the work is done. **Deviation from the spec:** the spec's "releases still run everything" and its test that `npm run release` runs the full gate predate the owner's 13 Nov decision that a release runs the security invariant tests rather than everything; the test proves instead that the release script chooses its tests itself (the invariant tests), never through this analysis. Tests: `apps/companion/test/test-impact.test.ts` (imports through other files and `vi.mock`, Python, shared history, the subset, the tool's text from a real git working tree, the release); `packages/policy/test/golden/07-mcp-unknown.yaml` rows for code, chat and another server. Each fails with its code taken out (the `vi.mock` pattern, the first-commit skip, the coding-session limit on the rule).
- **D-063, package provenance (safe variant).** A new NuGet package a call adds (D-053's reading of `dotnet add package` and manifest edits) has its downloaded `.nupkg` read on this computer, offline (`packages/policy/src/nuget-signature.ts`): a package is signed when its archive holds a `.signature.p7s` entry, read from the archive's own directory without extracting or running anything. One with none escalates as `nuget-unsigned` (risk 76), with the licence's and D-053's reasons beside it. A package not downloaded yet cannot be read, and D-053 already asks about it. **Decided by me:** the policy engine stays pure, so whether a signature is valid (`dotnet nuget verify`, a separate process) is not checked inside it; the Host could run that as a Codehouse action later. **List for IT:** npm provenance attestations need the registry (network), so they stay off until a registry connector is switched on (the owner's "no IT requests" rule: a connector the owner names, which can only tighten a decision). Tests: `packages/policy/test/nuget-signature.test.ts` (signed and unsigned packages built as real archives; adding the unsigned one escalates; fails without the engine's check); the policy's licence, dependency and golden tests still pass (749).
- **D-051, personal data flow map (a draft).** On Insights, Personal data, for the project of an open session (`GET /api/insights/personal-data`, `apps/host/src/personal-data.ts`): the fields that look like personal data by their names (contact details, names, dates of birth, addresses, identity and account numbers, IP addresses and device ids), and where each goes on the same statement: a log, storage or a network call. It reads the project's own source, not its dependencies, builds or tests, and changes nothing. It is marked a draft everywhere it appears. **For LEGAL:** Copy the draft for legal review gives Markdown with its scope (the project, how many files were read, what was left out) and the field list with where each goes. **Decided by me:** a flow is followed on the same statement only, and the draft says so, rather than claiming a whole-program trace it cannot give; patterns are by name, not by type, because most of the projects here are TypeScript and JavaScript where types rarely say a field is personal. Tests: `apps/host/test/personal-data.test.ts` (a logged email, a stored phone number and a posted card number are found; a comment is not read; tests and dependencies are left out; fails when tests are let in).
- **D-049, performance budgets.** A project sets budgets for its hot paths in `.codehouse/budgets.yml`: each the benchmark's name, the paths it guards and the most it may take on average (`apps/host/src/budgets.ts`). When a change touches a budget's paths, its evidence pack runs the project's benchmarks with the tool it uses, found from its own files: tinybench through Vitest's bench (`npx vitest bench --run --outputJson`), or BenchmarkDotNet (`dotnet run -c Release ... --exporters json`), as a Codehouse action, so the rules judge it, it runs in a job at the session's level without credentials, and the kill switch stops it. The pack says which budgets were met and which were not (shown in the evidence view), and a breach goes in its notes; a budget no benchmark measured is said, never passed. A change that touches no budget runs nothing. **Decided by me:** the budgets live with the evidence pack (where fitness checks are, D-045) rather than as a separate gate, so they run once per change; neither tool is installed by Codehouse (detected, never installed). Tests: `apps/host/test/evidence.test.ts` (a seeded slowdown fails its budget; fails when every budget passes) and `apps/host/test/budgets.test.ts`.
- **D-047, capability map.** A project names its business capabilities in `.codehouse/capabilities.yml`, beside D-041's architecture.yml: each a name, its paths (globs, matched as the architecture rules match them) and, if given, an owner (`apps/host/src/capabilities.ts`). Each change's evidence pack (D-003), its change report, then names the capabilities its files touch, with how many files each, and shows them in the evidence view ("Capabilities it touches"). A project with no map gets a note saying how to add one; a map that cannot be read says why. **Decided by me:** the evidence pack is the change report, since it is what describes a change for review; D-042's diagrams, drawn as text for want of a library, could now use C-057's Mermaid drawing, left for its own item. Tests: `apps/host/test/evidence.test.ts` (a change in billing code names Billing and its owner; no map, a note; fails with the capabilities taken out).
- **D-032, agent-aware build provenance.** Each release now carries `provenance.intoto.json` (`apps/host/src/slsa.ts`, written by `scripts/release.ts ship`): an in-toto Statement v1 whose predicate is SLSA provenance v1. Its subjects are every file of the release with its SHA-256; its resolved dependency is the commit; and its byproducts are the Codehouse sessions whose commits went in since the last published release, read from their git notes (D-030): each session's commits, the audit record sequence and hash each is tied to, and its evidence pack hash (D-003), with a hash over that so a changed entry is caught. The statement is checked against the schema and the files before it is added. **Decided by me:** a failure to make it is said in the release log and never stops a release (provenance is evidence, not a gate); when the last release's tag cannot be found, the last hundred commits stand in. Unsigned for now: the release key signs the update files, and signing the statement as a DSSE envelope is a later step. Tests: `apps/host/test/slsa.test.ts` (validates against the schema, hashes verify; a changed file or session fails; fails with the session check taken out; sessions from real git notes).
- **D-069, hotspot map.** On Insights, Hotspots, for the project of an open session (`GET /api/insights/hotspots`, `apps/host/src/hotspots.ts`): the files most worth attention, ranked by how often they changed (commits in the last 90 days, from `git log`), how tangled they are (decision points: branches, loops, catches and short-circuits, counted outside comments and strings), and how often agent work on them went wrong (failed calls on the file and files put back, from the audit). Each measure is scaled against the project's largest and weighted (changes 0.4, failures 0.35, decision points 0.25), so no single one swamps the rest; a file that never changed and never caused trouble is not a hotspot. The numbers are shown beside each file. For this, `tool_failed` now also names the file a call was about, from the session's folder (only inside it). Read only. Tests: `apps/host/test/hotspots.test.ts` (the top hotspot matches the fixture; fails when untouched files are let in; churn from a real repository).
- **D-072, failure taxonomy.** What went wrong in agent work, from the audit alone (`apps/host/src/failure-taxonomy.ts`, pure functions like analytics.ts): went round in circles (the loop guard's thrash_warned and thrash_paused), a tool call failed and tests failed (a new `tool_failed` record), edits put back (edit_files_reverted), said it was done and it was not (claim_verified, denied), and refused (the floor, the rules, a person). Counted per project and per model: a session's model is learnt from any of its records that name one, so a denial counts for the model its session ran on. Shown on Insights, What went wrong (`GET /api/insights/failures`), in words with every number. **New audit record, decided by me:** tool errors and failing tests were not in the audit, so each failed call now writes `tool_failed` with the tool, whether its command was a test run, and the session's model, never what the call said (a test of the loop guard checks the error text is absent); the loop records now name the model too. Tests: `apps/host/test/failure-taxonomy.test.ts` (fixture events classified correctly, on the audit's own record shape; fails when a claim that held is counted), `apps/host/test/loop-guard.test.ts` and `apps/web/test/failure-taxonomy.test.tsx`.
- **C-057, Mermaid and Markdown artifacts.** Markdown artifacts were built already (a text kind, shown as a document). Mermaid diagrams are a new kind, `mermaid`, that a chat saves with `save_artifact` (a flowchart, sequence, C4 or class diagram in Mermaid's own syntax). The Host serves one as a page made around its source (`mermaidPage`, `apps/host/src/artifacts.ts`): the source as escaped text, Mermaid's one file inlined, Mermaid at its strict level with `securityLevel`, `htmlLabels` and the other keys locked so a `%%{init}%%` directive cannot loosen them, under the same sandbox and policy as every artifact, so it draws offline and reaches nothing. A build without Mermaid shows the source. Download gives the `.mmd` source. **Dependency:** `mermaid`, pinned, a development dependency of the web app: its build copies only `dist/mermaid.min.js` into `dist/artifact-lib/` (a small plugin in `apps/web/vite.config.ts`, no copy plugin added), so the packaged app gains that one file and none of Mermaid's own dependencies; the packaging checks it is there. ADR 0003 asked for a vetted library from Codehouse itself, which this is. **Checked in Chromium, 19 Nov,** under the real artifact headers with the real Mermaid 12.3.0 file (5.5 MB, which the packaged app now carries): a flowchart is drawn as SVG with its labels; a hostile one with a `%%{init}%%` asking for the loose level, an `<img onerror>` label and a `javascript:` click is drawn with the label as text, no image element, no script run and no request outside. Tests: `apps/host/test/mermaid-artifact.test.ts` (a hostile diagram stays text, the directive cannot loosen it, a </script> in the library cannot end its script, the sandbox is the same; fails with either escape taken out); threat model T156.
- **C-032, the TypeScript language service.** Under a .ts, .tsx, .js or .jsx file in the file view: its problems (errors and warnings, each a place to go to), what the name at the cursor is (a line of text beside the code, never a hover tooltip, as DESIGN.md asks), Go to definition (F12, or the button; one definition goes straight there, in another file too) and Find references (a list of places). It is TypeScript's own language service (`apps/host/src/ts-worker.mjs`), with the project's tsconfig.json when it has one, in a worker thread with a memory cap (768 MB of old generation by default, `apps/host/src/ts-service.ts`): past the cap or on a crash the worker ends, what it was asked fails with a plain reason, and the next question starts a new one. The route (`GET /api/files/ts`) has the same checks as reading a file (the session's folder, the floor's blocked files), answers only with places inside the session's folder, and gives paths from it. **Dependency:** `typescript` 5.9.3, the version the repository already builds with, is now in the Host's own dependencies (it was a development one), because the packaged app installs only those; the packaging checks it is there. The lockfile change is that flag only. **Decided by me:** the worker is plain JavaScript so it loads the same under tsx, Vitest and Node; hover is shown as text. Tests: `apps/host/test/ts-service.test.ts` (a real project: diagnostics, info, definition, references; a worker past a tiny cap ends with a plain reason and the next question starts another; fails without the cap), `apps/host/test/files.test.ts` (the route's checks; fails without the file-type check) and `apps/web/test/typescript-help.test.tsx`.
- **T-004, a welcome that finds what is here.** Setup's last step, Found on this computer (`apps/host/src/welcome.ts`, `GET /api/welcome/found`; `apps/web/src/components/WelcomeFound.tsx`): the coding agents on the PATH (Claude Code, Codex, Gemini, Copilot, Cursor, Aider, opencode, Goose; shown only, since Codehouse runs Claude through its own copy), the model runtimes answering on this computer (Ollama, LM Studio, llama.cpp, each asked once at 127.0.0.1 without following a redirect, pointing to Settings), and the projects of your recent Claude Code conversations from the history Codehouse already reads, each with Open (the start form on its folder) and Carry on (its latest conversation resumed). Nothing is imported or started until you choose. Tests: `apps/host/test/welcome.test.ts`, `apps/web/test/welcome-found.test.tsx`.
- **T-008, projects inside a WSL distro (safe variant).** Running Claude Code inside the distro would mean a Linux binary Codehouse does not bundle, which invariant 6 forbids (only the bundled, unmodified binary, signed in through Anthropic's flow), so Claude Code stays the Windows binary and the project lives in the distro: opened by its `\\wsl.localhost\<distro>\...` path, so the file tools, the floor (which already denies the distro's home secrets) and the rules see it as any folder, and a coding session there is told to run every command inside the distro as `wsl.exe -d <distro> --cd <path> -- <command>` (`apps/host/src/wsl-projects.ts`). The start form lists the distros with their home folders (`GET /api/wsl/distros`, Docker's own left out, a home that is not a plain path not offered). **For the owner's decision:** E-028 judges what a wsl call runs for its dangers, but the allow rules still match the outer `wsl.exe` text, so a routine command in a WSL project (`npm test`) asks you unless Extreme approval answers it; letting the allow rules see through the wrapper would loosen the engine's allow path, so it is listed rather than done. A test proves a wrapped command is never judged more loosely than the same command on its own. Tests: `apps/host/test/wsl-projects.test.ts`, `apps/web/test/wsl-folders.test.tsx`.
- **T-015, a budgeted hand-off to another provider.** Changing the model mid-conversation was there (`set_model`). Carrying a chat on with another model (B21) now lets you choose the model, when there are several, and how much of the conversation it is given: Small, Medium or Large (about 2,000, 8,000 or 25,000 tokens). It keeps your first message (what the conversation is for, cut if it alone would take half), then the newest messages that fit, and says how many in between were left out; the button's tooltip says what it carries. It replaces the fixed last 30,000 characters. Tests: `apps/web/test/fallback.test.tsx`.
- **T-023, drag to reorder the sidebar.** A session can be dragged within its section to place it, and onto another section to move it: Pinned pins it, a project moves it into the project, its own folder (or Chats) takes it out of Pinned and the project; a section it cannot belong to (another folder, Working, or for a chat the chat project it was started in) says why and changes nothing. Your order is kept on this computer with the sidebar's other choices; sessions you have not placed keep the sidebar's own order after those you have. The row menu has Move up and Move down for the keyboard. `apps/web/src/lib/rail-order.ts`. Tests: `apps/web/test/rail-order.test.ts`; the sidebar's tests still pass.
- **T-029, several runs on several models, in the background.** Best of several runs (G25) now gives each run its own model, and In the background starts them while the start form stays ready for the next thing; they are in the sidebar and compared when opened. `apps/web/src/lib/runs.ts`. Tests: `apps/web/test/runs.test.ts`.
- **T-037, Markdown in the message box as you type.** The box stays a plain text box, so everything it does with /, @, pasting and recall is unchanged. The key that makes a new line (Shift+Enter, or Enter when Ctrl+Enter sends) continues a list (numbers count up, a task box starts empty) and ends it on an empty item; Tab and Shift+Tab indent a list item; opening a code block (``` with a language) and making a new line closes it with the caret inside, and inside a block a new line keeps the indentation. A backdrop behind the text colours headings, list markers, quotes and strong text and shades code, by colour and shading only so the caret stays on its letter. **Decided by me:** not a rich-text editor, whose own editing would fight the box's commands, mentions and voice input. `apps/web/src/lib/markdown-edit.ts`. Tests: `apps/web/test/markdown-edit.test.ts`; the composer's tests still pass.
- **T-048, context chips in the message box.** A terminal excerpt (Ask Claude about this), your notes on the changes, review findings to fix and a side session's answer now go into the box as one short token, `⟦terminal 1: npm test⟧`, which the backdrop (T-037) draws as a chip, as it does a file named with @. What a chip stands for is kept beside the box and put in its place when you send (in the transcript, the held list and the prompt alike); delete the token and that context is not sent; a token typed with no chip, or of another kind, is left as typed. Prompt templates and Edit on a sent message still put plain text in, since they are for you to change. `apps/web/src/lib/chips.ts`. Tests: `apps/web/test/chips.test.ts`, and in `apps/web/test/composer-box.test.tsx` the real box from chip to sent prompt; each fails with its part taken out; the terminal, review, changes and side tests still pass.
- **T-052, maths in messages.** Claude's replies draw maths with KaTeX (`apps/web/src/components/MathView.tsx`): `$...$` inline or as a block, ```` ```math ```` blocks, and, turned into those first (`apps/web/src/lib/math-text.ts`), `\(...\)`, `\[...\]` and a single-dollar span that is plainly maths (no space just inside the dollars, and a command, ^, _ or = in it), so a price such as $5 to $10 stays text; inline and fenced code are never changed. KaTeX runs with trust off (no `\href`, `\url` or HTML), small size and expansion limits, and maths it cannot read is shown as its source in code. **Dependencies (stated as the conventions ask):** `remark-math` 6.0.0, the remark plugin for maths in Markdown, and `katex` 0.18.10, already installed for Mermaid and now named directly, both as web devDependencies since the interface is bundled (nothing new ships at run time). `remark-math`'s parser asks for KaTeX 0.16, which has a low-severity advisory and which nothing here uses (it is for HTML output); an `overrides` entry in the root package.json pins it to 0.18.10, and `npm audit` reports none. Tests: `apps/web/test/math.test.tsx` (maths and money, code untouched, inline and block drawing, an `\href` refused, unreadable maths as source); each fails with its part taken out; the reply and copy tests still pass.
- **T-055, live output of a running command (waits for the SDK).** Checked again: the SDK still sends no output while a tool runs. Its bundled code has `Query.getTaskOutput(taskId)` (the last 8 KiB a background shell task wrote, read-only) and sdk.d.ts types the request, but the `Query` interface does not declare the method, so, as the conventions ask and as with `askSideQuestion`, Codehouse does not use it until it is declared (docs/sdk-notes.md, No live tool output).
- **T-058, visual replies in any session.** A coding session now has the Companion's `save_artifact` too (it was a chat's only; the rule `artifact-tools` already allowed it from the Host's own Companion in any session), and an Artifacts button beside its other panels. An HTML artifact is shown in Codehouse's light or dark theme: the page is given the theme's colours as CSS variables and a quiet default before its own content, which anything it sets overrides; the address takes only `light` or `dark`, anything else is ignored, and the page keeps the same sandbox (scripts of its own, no network, no same origin). The viewer zooms from 50% to 200%. Tests: in `apps/host/test/api.test.ts` the themed page, the sandbox kept and a hostile theme value ignored; `apps/web/test/artifact-zoom.test.tsx`; the artifact tests still pass.
- **T-063, the details card with chosen rows.** A session's Details has Choose rows (environment, context, usage limits, connectors, changes, held-out checks and locked tests, background work, side chats), kept on this computer. The context meter marks where Claude Code compacts the conversation by itself (the SDK's `autoCompactThreshold`, when `isAutoCompactEnabled`), and says so in words; usage limits show the plan meter; each background task (a dev server, a watcher) has Stop, a desktop command `stop_task` that records `task_stopped` first and then stops only that task through the SDK's declared `Query.stopTask`, refused for a task that is not the session's; and the side chats started from the session are listed with Open. Tests: `apps/web/test/session-details.test.tsx` (the mark, Stop, side chats, rows chosen and remembered) and in `apps/host/test/background.test.ts` one task stopped, recorded first, and another session's refused.
- **T-067, managed policy settings.** IT's signed `policy.yaml` (B37) can now hold `settings`: `managedBy` (the label), `maxMode` (the broadest permission mode), `extremeApproval`, `providers` (claude, local, gateway), `phone` and `locked` (settings keys). They are enforced where they apply: a session does not start, or change, broader than the mode cap or with Extreme approval off (after a resume too); a Claude session or a chat on another model outside the provider list is refused, and so is a runtime of a kind not allowed in Settings; with the phone off no pairing, request, notification or push channel goes out (a decision still waits on the desktop) and Settings refuses to turn a push channel on; a locked setting cannot be changed through Settings. Each refusal says `Managed by ...: why.`, and Settings shows the same at the top (`GET /api/managed`). A key this version does not know is refused like a bad signature, so a policy it cannot enforce in full stops sessions rather than being half applied; a project's overlay cannot set them. `npm run policy -- check|show [--managed-dir]` (`apps/host/src/policy-cli.ts`) says whether IT's policy is signed and in force, and what it allows. **Decided by me:** limit clients is the phone, the Relay and the push channels (the only clients beside this desktop); **listed:** updates from a mirror, because a change to the updater needs the update end-to-end check first, so a policy naming one is refused for now. docs/managed-policy.md has the guide. Tests: `packages/policy/test/managed.test.ts` (read, hashed, in words; unknown keys and bad values refused; each check narrows only; a project overlay refused), `apps/host/test/managed-settings.test.ts` (mode cap, Extreme, providers; `policy check` and `show` on a real signed folder, and a bad signature), and in `apps/host/test/api.test.ts` a locked setting, a push channel with the phone off, and `/api/managed`.
- **T-075, the Pull requests page (safe variant).** Beside the open pull requests and their checks (T-061), a pull request now shows its description, its comments and reviews in order, who is asked to review and whether it merges by itself (`GET /api/forge/thread`, `viewThread` in `apps/host/src/forge/github.ts`); others' words are shown as text and, when one reads like an instruction to an AI (D-061's scan), flagged and never given to Claude.
