What you can do to it (`POST /api/forge/act`, `apps/host/src/forge/actions.ts`): comment, ask reviewers (GitHub logins or org/team only), check it out, merge (merge, squash or rebase), merge once its checks pass or stop that, revert, and tick or untick a task box in the description (only that box changes, on the description as GitHub has it). Each is one gh command made from fixed words and checked values, with your text in a temporary file removed after, run as a Codehouse action: judged as the same command from the session (gh writes ask, rule git-remote at risk 80), on the approval card, recorded, stopped by the kill switch, and with Extreme approval answering as it does for that session (owner, 14 Nov). **Decided by me:** link previews are not fetched (a fetch from a page is the network), links open outside Codehouse as every link does. Also fixed: T-080's watch route returned "not watching" for every request, so a watch could never be turned on; it now only does that when you turn one off. Tests: `apps/host/test/forge-actions.test.ts` (the commands and their refusals, the box toggle, the thread with a flagged comment, each change through a file removed after and its output redacted, the watch turned on) and `apps/web/test/pull-requests.test.tsx`.
- **T-081, stacks of pull requests (safe variant).** A pull request built on another's branch is shown in its stack, bottom first (`stackOf`, `GET /api/forge/stack`; a fork in the stack ends the walk rather than guess). Merge stack and Rebase stack (`POST /api/forge/stack`) go bottom up, one gh command at a time, each a Codehouse action on its own approval card as in T-075, and stop at the first that does not go through, saying how far they got. **Decided by me:** Rebase stack is GitHub's own `gh pr update-branch --rebase` for each, so no branch is force-pushed from this computer. Tests: in `apps/host/test/forge-actions.test.ts` the stack found and a fork, both actions bottom up, a refusal stopping the rest, a lone pull request refused; `apps/web/test/pull-requests.test.tsx`.
- **T-091, project actions (safe variant).** The terminal pane shows a project's actions (`apps/host/src/project-actions.ts`, `apps/web/src/components/ProjectActions.tsx`). Yours are kept in Settings by project (`projectActions`: a name, a one-line command, setup or run, and a preview address on this computer only), written one a line as `name: command @ http://localhost:port`, and run on your click as if you typed them (your own terminal, recorded, not through the Approver, as before). A repository's own, in `.codehouse/actions.json`, are someone else's commands: shown under their own heading and run only as a Codehouse action (`POST /api/project-actions/run`), judged by your rules like Claude's, on the approval card, output redacted. **Decided by me:** nothing runs by itself, not when a worktree is made nor when a session settles, because a repository's file deciding to run commands is the untrusted case D-059 guards against; t3code's own `t3.json` is not read (its format is t3code's), and shortcuts for actions wait for T-071's registry. Tests: `apps/host/test/project-actions.test.ts`, the routes in `apps/host/test/api.test.ts` (yours saved by project and never run by the Host, the repository's run only through the Codehouse action), `apps/web/test/project-actions.test.tsx`.
- **T-092, projects in the sidebar (in part).** Each project or folder section has a monogram (its initials) in a colour made from its name, the same every time, and a menu with Pin project to the top and Archive project; archived ones leave the list, with a line saying how many and Show to see them again; pinning one brings it back from the archive (`apps/web/src/lib/project-marks.ts`). Kept on this computer with the sidebar's other choices; the sidebar's own groups (Pinned, Working, the chats) stay where they are. **Not yet:** the view of how a project's settings inherit from yours and IT's, which the review rates low priority. Tests: `apps/web/test/project-marks.test.ts`.
- **T-101, drafts on the phone (safe variant).** What you write to a session in the phone app is kept as a draft for that session on the phone (`apps/phone/src/drafts.ts`), through a reload or a lost connection, and cleared once it is sent. While the phone is offline, Send says Offline: kept as a draft and a line says why. Nothing is signed or queued offline: a signed message carries a fresh nonce and lives a minute (invariant 8), so it is signed with Face ID only when you send it online. Tests: `apps/phone/test/drafts.test.tsx`.
- **T-071, customisable shortcuts.** A registry of the commands a key can start (`apps/web/src/lib/shortcuts.ts`: search and commands, the shortcut sheet, the sidebar, copy the last reply or code block, help), each with its default. Your own keys are kept in Codehouse's settings (`shortcuts`, by command id; saved by `PUT /api/shortcuts`, which adds to the file and is recorded) and read tolerantly: a command this version does not know is left alone, and one added later starts with its default. Yours come first, a default yields to a key you gave another command, a key is given once, and the keys that decide a request (Ctrl+Enter, Enter, Esc, Tab) and the kill switch (Ctrl+Shift+K) are never given to anything (D-092, U4); each refusal says why. Keys are read by the physical key, so Ctrl+Shift+; works on any layout. Settings, Keyboard shortcuts takes keys by pressing them, with None and Default; the shortcut sheet shows the keys in force. Ctrl+1 to 9, a request card's own letters and the Mac's Command-comma stay as they are. Tests: `apps/web/test/shortcuts.test.ts`.
- **T-107, keyboard pickers.** Alt+M, Alt+E and Alt+O open the model, effort and mode pickers of the session in front (focused, with its list shown where the browser allows), and Ctrl+Alt+Up and Ctrl+Alt+Down move its effort one step, never above the cap in Settings and not for a chat or a model on this computer (`apps/web/src/lib/pickers.ts`). They are in T-071's registry, so each can be given other keys. Ctrl+1 to 9 for the first sessions was there. **Not yet:** workspace and branch pickers, since the start form has no such picker of its own to open. Tests: `apps/web/test/pickers.test.tsx`.
- **T-110, right-to-left text (in part).** A message you send, each paragraph, list item, heading and table cell of a reply, the message box (and its Markdown backdrop) and the phone's conversation now find their own direction (`dir="auto"`, `unicode-bidi: plaintext`), so Arabic or Hebrew reads right to left beside English; code and code blocks stay left to right. **Listed:** translating the interface itself (every string through a catalogue, about two thousand of them), since the two owners work in English; dates and numbers already follow en-GB. Tests: `apps/web/test/rtl.test.tsx`.
- **T-111, trackpad swipe for back and forward.** A two-finger swipe goes back (to the right) or forward (to the left) through the pages you visited, as Alt+Left and Alt+Right do (`SwipeDetector` in `apps/web/src/lib/navHistory.ts`): the horizontal scrolling must add up to 160 pixels within a short run with little vertical movement, counts once and then rests; pinch zoom, a scroll down the page and anything that scrolls sideways itself (a wide table, code, the diff) are left alone. Tests: `apps/web/test/swipe.test.tsx`.
- **T-112, the terminal kept through a reconnect.** The Host keeps each session's terminal, your commands and what they printed, the last 5,000 lines, in memory only (`apps/host/src/terminal-scrollback.ts`; never written to disk, so nothing new to redact or keep), and a terminal pane that opens with nothing (a reload, a reconnect) asks for it (`term_history`) and shows it again. Clear clears the Host's copy too (`term_clear`). The pane has Select all, and follows the output unless you scroll up to read, with Jump to latest to come back. Each session already had its own terminal. Tests: `apps/host/test/terminal-scrollback.test.ts`, `apps/web/test/terminal-restore.test.tsx`.
- **T-113, the browser panel (in part).** A local page opens with tabs above it (pages on this computer, up to eight, kept while the panel is open) and the ports the session's own processes listen on, each a button that opens it (`GET /api/session/ports`, `apps/host/src/session-ports.ts`): from the process Codehouse started the session in (its job's helper, whose id the limiter now reports) down through everything it started, read on Windows with one fixed PowerShell query; none for a session without a job, or on a Mac yet. **Listed:** find on the page, since the page is another origin's and only the window's own find (WebView2's) can search it, a packaging change that waits for an update end-to-end check. Tests: `apps/host/test/session-ports.test.ts`, `apps/web/test/browser-bar.test.tsx`.
- **T-118, a device beside the session (in part).** A session's Details can show A device (a row you can choose): the Android emulators running here (when adb is on this computer) and, on a Mac, the booted iOS simulators (simctl), with a picture of the chosen one's screen taken when you ask (`apps/host/src/devices-panel.ts`, `GET /api/device-panel/list` and `/shot`; a picture only of a listed device with a plain id, and only kept when it is a PNG). Nothing is installed. **Listed for the owner:** device tools for agents (tapping, typing, installing on a device) and SSH device hosts, because they give Claude new powers outside the Approver's view unless built as Companion tools with their own rules; they are not started. Tests: `apps/host/test/devices-panel.test.ts`.
- **Updates never break (T-087, T-136, T-137, 20 Nov).** These change the window, the installer and the quick prompt, so `scripts/update-e2e.ts` was run with an install of the last release (2.0.520, E2E_FROM) updating to this package: it passed. A first run failed when the new version's Approver took over 30 seconds to start on a busy machine ("The Approver did not become ready in time"); a baseline of 2.0.520 updating to itself, and the same check again, both passed.
- **Frontier D-084, an adversarial pair.** Beside C-043's review, Try to break it (`adversary_start`, `apps/host/src/adversary.ts`) starts a breaker: a session on the reviewer model in Settings, reached only through the runtime or gateway on this computer (so its data-boundary card applies as to any session on it), in a new worktree of the repository holding a copy of the change (C-043's diff against HEAD, with the floor's secret files left out, applied there). It is in Plan mode with the read tools a local chat has and one more, RunTests, which runs the project's own test command as a Codehouse action in its worktree, takes no input, and is judged like any call (an unknown tool to the rules, so it asks you unless Extreme approval answers). Any edit it tries goes to you as `breaker-edit`, whatever the rules say. It reports each problem on a line starting FINDING:, and those findings are kept with the session it tried to break and put in that session's evidence pack (`adversary`: the model, when, whether it has reported, and the findings), shown in Evidence. Recorded as `adversary_started`. Tests: `apps/host/test/adversary.test.ts` (RunTests offered only to a breaker and run only once allowed; a breaker's edit escalates) and the D-084 case in `apps/host/test/evidence.test.ts` (the findings land in the pack).
- **Frontier D-079, codebase tours.** A session's menu has Codebase tours... (`apps/host/src/tours.ts`, `GET /api/tours`, the `tour_ask` message). A tour is a file in the project's `.codehouse/tours` folder: a title and ordered steps, each naming a symbol, its file and a note. Every step is checked against the C-036 code index (brought up to date first): it links to the symbol's line, which opens the file there; a step whose symbol moved to another file is flagged with its new place, and one whose symbol is gone is flagged as no longer in the code. A file that is not a tour is left out with why. Ask Claude for a tour of a topic sends a prompt with the format, so the tour it writes is read the same way. Test: `apps/host/test/tours.test.ts` (steps linked through the real index; a moved and a removed symbol flagged after a change).
- **Frontier D-098, the NPU judge profile.** With D-086's claims judge on and the model runtime Foundry Local (C-067), the judge runs on a model whose catalogue entry runs on the NPU (`runtime.deviceType` NPU), when one is downloaded and on your list of models, in place of the one chosen, leaving the CPU and graphics card to the work (`judgeModel` in `apps/host/src/claim-judge-run.ts`, `foundryNpuModels` in `providers/foundry-local.ts`). Otherwise the chosen model judges. Either way the claim's reason says where it ran ("it ran on the NPU, with ...", or that no model on your list runs on the NPU, so the chosen one judged it). The judge's limits are unchanged: a model on this computer only, never a gateway, and it can only lower a claim. Test: `apps/host/test/npu-judge.test.ts`.
- **Frontier D-097, Constrained Language Mode awareness.** Doctor has a PowerShell language mode line (`apps/host/src/lockdown.ts`): the mode Codehouse's PowerShell runs in, and what sets a lockdown, from one PowerShell query that needs no administrator rights, read the first time Doctor is opened: Device Guard's user-mode code integrity (WDAC enforcing or auditing), the number of AppLocker rules in effect, and `__PSLockdownPolicy`. Anything but FullLanguage is a warning, with what it means. When a PowerShell or shell call fails with a lockdown's signature (method invocation only on core types, blocked by group policy or the administrator, Application Control blocked the file, scripts disabled), the error Claude reads carries the likely cause and asks it not to retry the same way (through the same path as the loop guard's warning). Test: `apps/host/test/lockdown.test.ts`.
- **Frontier D-095, Group Policy and Intune templates.** `packaging/policy-templates/Codehouse.admx` and `en-US/Codehouse.adml` give IT four settings under HKLM\Software\Policies\Codehouse, each stricter only: Commands Codehouse refuses (DenyCommands), Commands Codehouse asks about (AskCommands), Allow rules to remove (RemoveAllow), each one to a line, and The most a session may do by itself (MaxAutonomy). The Host reads them once when it starts, with reg.exe (`apps/host/src/group-policy.ts`, `packages/policy/src/registry-policy.ts`), and merges them like IT's managed policy: deny and ask rules added (shown as IT's in explanations), allow rules removed, never an allow added; MaxAutonomy is a ceiling over every project's autonomy cap. Doctor has a Group Policy line with what was applied; a value that tries to allow (AllowCommands) or one Codehouse does not know is ignored, and Doctor warns about it. The rollout is listed for IT. Test: `apps/host/test/group-policy.test.ts` (a registry value tightens a decision and shows in Doctor; one that allows is ignored with a finding; the templates write exactly the values read).
- **Frontier D-094, a verifiable egress report (the safe variant).** Every minute on Windows, one fixed PowerShell query lists the processes and their TCP connections (Get-NetTCPConnection, the table GetExtendedTcpTable reads, no administrator rights) and UDP endpoints, and each session gets the remote endpoints its own processes reached: its Claude Code process and everything below it, found from the root T-113 already keeps (`apps/host/src/egress.ts`). Each endpoint is checked against what the session may reach: this computer always; for a Claude session, Anthropic's published API ranges (160.79.104.0/23, 2607:6bc0::/48), the addresses its hosts resolve to at the time, and hosts you add (Settings, Privacy, Where sessions connect); for a session on a model on this computer, nothing else. One outside the list is flagged (`egress_flagged`); with strict on it also stops the session (`egress_stopped`, recorded first). A session's menu has Where it connected..., which shows them and downloads the report signed with this computer's key (D-080's, sealed with DPAPI), checkable with `verifyReport`. Deviation: the plan named a new C# helper (packaging/Egress.cs); PowerShell's networking cmdlets read the same tables without one, so nothing new had to be packaged (no update check needed). ETW capture, which needs administrator rights, is listed for IT. Strict is off by default, since a CDN address an Anthropic host moves to between samples could be flagged. Test: `apps/host/test/egress.test.ts`.
- **Frontier D-080, signed knowledge packs.** A session's menu has Knowledge pack... (`apps/host/src/knowledge-pack.ts`, `knowledge_export` and `knowledge_import`). Export gathers the project's ADRs (docs/adr/*.md), its glossary settings (.codehouse/glossary.yml) and what it tells agents (CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md) into one pack signed with this computer's Ed25519 key, made once and kept with the private half sealed by the computer's protection (DPAPI), and shows the public key to give to whoever imports it. Import applies a pack only when it is signed by a key you trust (Settings, Shared rule packs, Trusted keys, the same keys as C-055), nothing in it changed after signing, and every path is one of those files; otherwise it is refused, recorded as `knowledge_refused`, and nothing is written. Claude Code's own settings files are never carried, since they could change what a session may do. An imported CLAUDE.md or AGENTS.md is taken as D-059's new pinned version, since you imported it. Recorded as `knowledge_exported` and `knowledge_imported` (before anything is written). Test: `apps/host/test/knowledge-pack.test.ts` (export and import; an altered pack, an untrusted key and a settings file refused).
- **Frontier D-071, dependency upgrades with evidence.** When a change touches a lockfile, Changes has Upgrade evidence (`upgrade_pack`, `apps/host/src/upgrade-pack.ts`): one pack with each upgrade (package, from and to, its lockfile), what was added and removed, the tests run after the change and how they ended (from the change's D-003 evidence pack, which is made or refreshed), the SBOM of what changed (D-056), and the admission checks: names newly in a manifest judged as D-053 judges them (an invented name refused), the licences of new and upgraded dependencies against the managed list, yours, or the permissive default (D-055), and the vulnerability snapshot (D-057). The pack is complete only when the last test run after the change passed and no check refused a dependency; otherwise it says what is missing. Nothing is run or fetched; recorded as `upgrade_pack` with counts. Test: `apps/host/test/upgrade-pack.test.ts`.
- **Frontier D-070, PowerShell modernisation.** A session's menu has Check PowerShell for version 7... (`pwsh_modernise`, `apps/host/src/pwsh-modernise.ts`): the project's .ps1 and .psm1 files (build output and node_modules left out, at most 200) are each read with the AST worker the policy uses, and its commands checked against what PowerShell 7 does not have: the WMI cmdlets (Get-CimInstance and the like instead), the event log cmdlets (Get-WinEvent), New-WebServiceProxy, snap-ins (modules), transactions, the aliases 7 removed (curl and wget, which now run curl.exe, sc, which runs sc.exe, and the WMI ones), `-Encoding Byte` (`-AsByteStream`) and workflows. Each finding gives the line and what to use instead. Where PSScriptAnalyzer is installed, its findings for the 5.1-to-7 rules (PSUseCompatibleSyntax for 7.0, PSAvoidUsingWMICmdlet, PSAvoidUsingCmdletAliases) are added, from a short script Codehouse writes to its data folder; PSScriptAnalyzer reads the scripts and runs none of them, and nothing is changed. Recorded as `pwsh_modernise` with counts. Test: `apps/host/test/pwsh-modernise.test.ts` (findings per script on a fixture, with the real parser).
- **Frontier D-067, a migration planner with evidence.** A session's menu has Plan a migration...: give what the migration is and a folder, and its JavaScript and TypeScript sources (tests and build output left out, at most 300) are split into waves from their own relative imports, leaves first, so each wave uses only files of earlier waves; files in an import cycle share a wave (`apps/host/src/migration.ts`, the `migration_plan` and `migration_wave` window messages). For each wave: Capture before records the behaviour of its files that export a function (D-066's capture, from the file as it is now, as a Codehouse action in a sandbox copy); Ask Claude to do this wave sends a prompt naming that wave's files and no others; Compare after replays the captured inputs (D-006) and shows each changed output in words. A wave counts as done only when every such file has both; capturing again starts the comparison over; a file that exports nothing callable is left to the project's tests. Plans and captures are kept under `migrations` in the data folder, redacted; each step is recorded (`migration_planned`, `migration_captured`, `migration_compared`) with counts only. Tests: `apps/host/test/migration.test.ts` (waves, a cycle, done only with capture and comparison) and `apps/web/test/migration-panel.test.tsx`.
- **Frontier D-052, a multi-repo contract check.** When D-046 finds that an edit removes exports from a library, the reason it is asked about now also names the other repositories on this computer that use them (`apps/host/src/contract-check.ts`): the folders beside the library (its parent folder's other folders, at most 50) whose package.json depends on the library's package name, and in them each import of a removed name from the package or a subpath of it (named, default, type and require imports, aliases followed), with file and line. node_modules, build output and links are skipped, at most 3,000 files a repository, read only, and only when there is a break. The C-036 index was not needed: the search runs only on a break, over the repositories that depend on the package. Test: `apps/host/test/contract-check.test.ts` (a breaking change in a provider reports the affected consumer repositories, through canUseTool's reason).
- **Frontier D-012, a time-boxed fuzz pass (the safe variant).** Changes, Fuzz the parsers (`fuzz_run`) takes the change's JavaScript and TypeScript files (not tests), finds their exported parsing functions (parse, decode, tokenise, lex, deserialise; at most twenty), and calls each with strings generated from a seed by a small runner Codehouse writes to `fuzz/runner.mjs` in its data folder (`apps/host/src/fuzz.ts`): short and long strings of letters, punctuation and brackets, numbers, whitespace and awkward characters, and long repeats for deep nesting. Codehouse's own runner was chosen over fast-check, which the frontier plan allowed, so no dependency was added. Each run is a Codehouse action (judged like the same command from the session, without credentials, in a job at the session's level, stopped by the kill switch), with a 256 MB heap cap (`--max-old-space-size`) and the minute shared between the functions. A parser's own SyntaxError on bad input is expected; a TypeError, RangeError, ReferenceError, a thrown non-error, running out of the heap cap or a hang is a finding, with the input it was given (written to an open file before each call, so a run that dies still says). Each finding's input is written to `testdata/fuzz/<function>/<hash>` in the project and handed to D-008, which offers it to pin. The run is recorded as `fuzz_run` with counts and where findings were written, never the inputs. Native fuzzers (jazzer.js, SharpFuzz) are listed for IT and the owner, not built. Test: `apps/host/test/fuzz.test.ts` (a seeded crash found well within 60 seconds, the heap cap kept, a hang stopped, SyntaxError left alone, a refusal stops the pass).
- **Frontier D-093, cognitive load presets.** Settings, General, Appearance, Lighter load has four presets, each on or off with the others and kept on this computer with the rest of the appearance (`load`, read tolerantly): Chunked plans shows a plan waiting for approval a step at a time (its numbered or bulleted items, or its paragraphs), with Step N of M and Previous and Next, the whole plan still one Copy away (`components/ChunkedPlan.tsx`); Calm turns movement off as Reduce motion does; Fewer panels keeps a session's side panel closed until you open it, as in a narrow window; Focus on one task keeps the sidebar of chats and sessions hidden, and the Sidebar button shows it until the preset is turned off and on again. They are put on the page as `data-load`, read by the stylesheet and the page. Tests: each preset passes the axe-core scan on a session, Settings and a plan card (`apps/web/test/a11y-scan.test.tsx`), and `apps/web/test/load-presets.test.tsx`.
- **Frontier D-092, safe keyboard approvals.** On the approval card and the workspace's request panel, a High or Severe request is never allowed by one key (`apps/web/src/lib/allow-chord.ts`): Ctrl+Enter, or Enter or Space on Allow, only arms it and says Press Enter again within 2 seconds to allow; a second press within two seconds allows it, and the arming lapses after that. Plain Enter never arms it. Severe still asks you to type allow on the card (U4), and High still waits a second before Allow can be used. Below High, one Ctrl+Enter allows as before, and a pointer click on Allow is unchanged. Tests: `apps/web/test/allow-chord.test.ts`, and the D-092 cases in `approval-card.test.tsx` and `quick-decision.test.tsx` (a single key cannot allow High).
- **Frontier D-091, narrated diffs.** Each change listed under a file in the Changes pane is now a stop of its own (`apps/web/src/lib/narrate.ts`): a screen reader hears one sentence in a fixed order, the file, which change of how many, its kind (added, changed, removed), where and how many lines, then the risk of the decision that allowed the edit behind it (or risk not known for a change no edit of Claude's made). The arrow keys move to the next or previous change of the file, Home and End to the first and last; keys pressed on a button inside a change are left to the button. The approval card already reads as labelled fields (Where, Effects, Would touch, Review, Why), so it is unchanged. Owed by the owner: an NVDA run over the Changes pane and an approval card, recorded in the to-do list. Test: `apps/web/test/narrate.test.tsx` (the order of the accessible text, and moving between changes).
- **Frontier D-089, the review load meter.** Every pending change (a Write, Edit, MultiEdit or NotebookEdit request) shows about how many minutes it takes to review (`packages/shared/src/review-load.ts`, exported through `contracts` so no build setting changed): its lines at five a minute, a minute for the file, half as long again for High risk and twice as long for Severe. `GET /api/review-load` adds up the changes allowed in the last hour from the audit log, each call once by its id, denied and other calls left out. Settings, Sessions, Review load (`reviewCapacityMinutes`, 5 to 600, read each time; absent: no warning) sets the minutes of review you can give an hour; Approvals then shows the last hour's load under Waiting on you, and warns when it is above your capacity. Tests: `apps/web/test/review-load.test.tsx` and the D-089 case in `apps/host/test/api.test.ts`.
- **Frontier D-088, adaptive explanation depth.** D-020's explanation now has a Detail choice (`apps/web/src/components/Explanation.tsx`, `lib/explain-depth.ts`): Short says only what would change the decision, Normal (the default) adds the stages that matched a rule, Full shows every stage. The choice is kept on this computer for each area separately: How it was decided on the approval card (approvals) and the policy tester's result, which now shows the explanation too (policy). A floor decision is always shown in full, with no choice offered, and says it cannot be changed. Test: `apps/web/test/explain-depth.test.tsx`.
- **Frontier D-087, the interruption budget.** Settings, Phone, Fewer interruptions (`noticeBatchMinutes`, 5 to 240, read at each notice; absent sends each at once, as before): the phone's non-urgent notices (a session finished, a note from Claude, the daily digest) are held by `NoticeBatcher` (`apps/host/src/notice-batch.ts`) and sent as one sealed summary when the time is up, a lone one as it was; what is held goes when Codehouse closes or the setting is turned off. Approvals never pass through it (the broker delivers each to the phone at once), nor do warnings such as a stuck, repeating or paused session, a spent risk budget, drift from the goal or an empty build queue, which keep their own immediate lines. Test: `apps/host/test/notice-batch.test.ts` (an approval reaches the phone at once while notices wait).
- **Frontier D-024, a second confirmation for loosening (the safe variant).** Three kinds of change now loosen only after a phrase typed on the desktop and a ten-minute wait (`apps/host/src/loosening.ts`, `loosening-api.ts`): a rules save that takes out a floor, deny or ask rule (`loosen remove <ids>`; D-026's own phrase for what it would allow still comes first), a settings save that stops a project being strict about its tests, the built-in ask Extreme approval waits on (meanwhile both stay strict, and the rest of the save applies at once), and raising a project's autonomy cap, by hand or by accepting a D-019 proposal (after the phone's passkey when one is paired). Each is recorded first (`loosening_requested`; one that cannot be recorded is not kept), told to the phone and the push channels for information only, listed on the Policy page and in Settings with a Cancel (`loosening_cancelled`), and applied by the Host once the wait is over (`loosening_applied`, then `autonomy_raised` for a cap); a rules change whose file changed in the wait is dropped instead (`loosening_dropped`). What waits is kept in `loosening.json` in the data folder, so a restart neither loses it nor skips the wait. Tightening (a new rule, a project made strict) applies at once, as before. D-019's no-phone accept therefore no longer raises at once: it is recorded as `autonomy_raise_accepted` and waits like any raise. A phone passkey in place of the wait is listed for security, not built. Tests: the D-024 cases in `apps/host/test/api.test.ts` and `apps/web/test/policy-loosening.test.tsx`.
- **Frontier D-023, scope lease.** In Auto and Accept edits, where Claude Code approves much by itself, a session can hold a lease (`apps/host/src/scope-lease.ts`): path globs from the project's folder it may write, and the command families (first words) it may run. An approved plan that names paths sets it, recorded as `scope_lease_set`: the folders of the files and folders it names, each with everything under it, and the families its code spans start with (npm, git, dotnet and so on); a plan that names no path sets none. You can set, change or clear it from the session's menu (Scope lease..., the `set_lease` window message), recorded first and refused if it cannot be. A write outside its paths or outside the project, a command whose family it does not name, or a redirect or move onto a path outside it (D-014's reading) is asked about whatever the rules allow: the floor hook answers ask and canUseTool escalates it as `scope-lease`. Commands that only read (ls, cat, Get-Content and the like) are always within it. It is not used in Manual or Plan, where every call is asked already, nor in Extreme approval, which is a flag on Manual. Decision recorded for the owner to confirm (it only makes Codehouse stricter): the plan's lease applies by itself when the plan is approved, because approving the plan is the owner's act and the lease only narrows what runs without asking. Tests: `apps/host/test/scope-lease.test.ts` (a write outside the lease asks in Auto, through the hook and canUseTool, though the rules allow it).
- **Frontier D-020, explain why and what would change.** `PolicyEngine.explain(call, ctx)` (`packages/policy/src/explain.ts`) runs the same evaluation as `evaluate` and keeps a trace of each stage and the rules it matched: Floor, Listed terms, Input, Deny rules, Dependencies, Parse, Remembered rules, Built-in checks (Codehouse's own), Ask rules, Allow rules, What it also runs (D-013), then Outcome. It adds the smallest change that would alter the decision: for a deny, the deny rules to remove or narrow, or that only IT can change it (the loader now marks IT's managed rules `managed` in `sources`), or that it is Codehouse's own check, which no rule switches off; for an ask, every reason it asks that would have to go (Codehouse's checks, the ask rules, the allow a call lacks); for an allow, the allow or remembered rule to remove. A floor decision is fixed: it says it cannot be changed and offers no change. The approval card's Why has How it was decided (`GET /api/pending/:id/explain`, read only, redacted, nothing recorded); when the request was asked by a check the session makes beyond the rules (a value read from the web, a locked test), the explanation says so. The policy tester's answer carries `explain` beside the verdict as before. The approval path itself is unchanged: an explanation is only worked out when asked for. Tests: `packages/policy/test/explain.test.ts` (every decision in a run has a trace agreeing with evaluate, every escalation a change, no floor decision a change), and the D-020 cases in `apps/host/test/api.test.ts`.
- **Frontier D-019, earned-autonomy proposals.** On C-015's caps: when the record is read (five minutes after start, daily, or Look at the record now), each capped project's record since its cap was set (or since you last said no) is counted: your approvals, changes you undid from a checkpoint, sealed held-out checks passed and failed, done-claims Verified and Contradicted, and floor denies. At least 20 approvals and 3 Verified claims, with not one undo, failed sealed check, Contradicted claim or floor deny, earns a proposal to raise the cap by one step, kept in `autonomy.json` (added keys `proposals` and `declined`; older versions keep them) and recorded once as `autonomy_raise_proposed`. A proposal never applies by itself: Settings, Autonomy shows it with Raise and Not now. With no phone paired, Raise applies it, recorded first as `autonomy_raised` (refused if it cannot be recorded). With a phone paired, Raise asks the phone, end to end encrypted, and the cap rises only when the phone's passkey confirms: the broker refuses an allow for this request from any other channel (desktop, Neighbourhood Watch, push codes), and the Host checks the answer came from the phone; a paired phone that cannot be asked raises nothing. Not now is recorded as `autonomy_raise_declined`, and the record must be earned again from then. A worse record drops the proposal, and lowering or raising the cap clears it. The audit index now marks a passed sealed check and a Verified claim (`good`), so they can be counted. Tests: `apps/host/test/earned-autonomy.test.ts`, and the D-019 cases in `apps/host/test/api.test.ts`.
- **Frontier D-014, consequence preview (the safe variant).** The approval card (Would touch) and the phone card (Touches) now list the paths a shell call would move or overwrite, beside what G20 already said it would delete or throw away: `mv`, `move`, `Move-Item` and `Rename-Item` move their sources (onto a destination, which is overwritten; a rename overwrites nothing), `cp`, `copy`, `Copy-Item`, `xcopy` and `robocopy` overwrite their destination, and `>` (also `2>`), `Set-Content`, `Out-File` and `tee` overwrite their file; appends (`>>`, `tee -a`, `-Append`), the null device and `2>&1` are left out. Each path is shown with what is there now (file, folder, not there, or pattern for a variable or wildcard), looked at only, links not followed. `touchesOf` in `apps/host/src/effects.ts` runs nothing, not even git, so nothing executes before you decide; the list rides on the request (`touches`, redacted by the broker with the rest, at most 20) and in the phone's sealed payload only when there is one, so other requests hash as before. It changes no decision. Shadow execution in an isolated environment (Windows Sandbox, or a restricted job with AppContainer) is listed for IT and security, not built. Test: `apps/host/test/consequence-preview.test.ts` (the call is asked about while the file is where it was, and does not run on a no).
- **Frontier C-067, Foundry Local profiles.** Settings, Other AI providers, Runtime now offers Foundry Local (Microsoft's model runtime, which IT installs; Codehouse never installs or starts it). It is saved as an OpenAI-compatible runtime with `profile: "foundry-local"` rather than a new kind, so an older version still reads the settings file (it treats it as any OpenAI-compatible server). Its port changes each time it starts, so Find it (`GET /api/providers/foundry`) runs `foundry server status` (then the older `foundry service status`), a program file only and never a script, with no shell, takes only addresses on this computer from what it prints, then the port in Microsoft's examples (5272), and keeps the first that answers `/openai/status` as Foundry Local does, never following a redirect; it fills in the address and the models it has downloaded. The models offered are the listed ones it has downloaded (`/openai/models`), and IT's signed model list and managed policy apply as for any runtime. A model's licence and its description come from Foundry Local's catalogue (`/foundry/list`, by name or alias) and are shown before its first use, as Ollama's are. Microsoft documents the REST API as a preview (checked 21 Nov), so a Foundry Local that answers differently is shown as not reachable rather than guessed at. Test: `apps/host/test/foundry-local.test.ts`.
- **Frontier C-044, best of N in worktrees, judged by tests.** On G25's runs of one prompt (2 to 4, each in its own worktree), each run's bar now shows its place: Best so far, or 2 of 3 and so on, ranked by its checks first (passing, then not run yet, then failing), then by the size of its change (smaller first), then by the calls that asked you or were stopped (fewer first). The memory governor caps N: when it refuses a run for memory once two or more have started, those are kept and compared, and you are told how many memory allowed (before, every run was closed). Test: `apps/web/test/run-rank.test.ts`.
- **Frontier C-055, signed shared rule packs.** A team's rule pack (the library's pack format) in the `rule-packs` folder under the data folder applies only when `<pack>.yaml.sig` is an Ed25519 signature by a key you trust (Settings, Privacy, Shared rule packs, `rulePackKeys`). Its deny and escalate rules always apply. Its allow rules apply only where IT's managed policy does not forbid them: none when IT sets the new `packAllows: false`, and IT's `removeAllow` removes any of them as it does any other. A pack that is not signed, signed by another key, changed after signing, or whose rule ids are not its own is a load error, so the policy stays as it was; only IT's policy may set `packAllows` (a project overlay that does is refused). The pack folder is watched like the others; a key change takes effect when Codehouse starts again. Rules from a pack show as `shared:<pack>` on the Library page. Tests: `packages/policy/test/shared-packs.test.ts`.
- **Frontier C-043, cross-model review through the gateway.** A second model reviews a session's change when you ask, from a new Review row in the session's details. It is reached only through the model runtime or organisation gateway on 127.0.0.1 named in Settings (`providers.reviewer`, a model listed there; ADR 0010: no key or token leaves Codehouse). It is sent the change against HEAD, new files included, built in a throw-away index so yours is never touched, with the floor's secret files left out (the same globs checkpoints use) and the rest redacted, and at most 60,000 characters of it. It is offered no tools: the request carries none, so it can only answer in words. Its answer becomes findings (file, line, how serious, what) shown in the pane; one that cannot be read is said rather than dropped. Each review is recorded (`review_run`, by model and number of findings). It decides nothing. Nothing is sent without a loaded policy. Tests: `apps/host/test/review.test.ts`.
- **Frontier C-036, a local codebase index.** Each project's text and symbols in an SQLite file of its own under the data folder (`code-index`), with FTS5 for the text: Node's own `node:sqlite` has FTS5 (verified on Node 24.15), so there is no native addon, nothing downloaded, and no inverted-index fallback is needed. Symbols come from a quick look at each line for TypeScript and JavaScript, Python, C#, PowerShell, Go and Rust (the tree-sitter outline, C-031, stays the exact view of one file). It is brought up to date incrementally, at most once a minute when asked: a file is read again only when its size or time changed, and one that is gone leaves the index; batches let the Host answer while it works. Never indexed: .git, node_modules, build output, links, files over 512 KB, binaries, and anything the floor's secret globs match (the same list checkpoints use), and nothing at all without a loaded policy. `GET /api/index/search`, `GET /api/index/symbols` and `POST /api/index/refresh`, for a session's folder. 50,000 files indexed in 138 s on this laptop, and again with nothing changed in 16 s (`scripts/index-bench.ts`). Tests: `apps/host/test/code-index.test.ts`.
- **Frontier C-008, a newer Claude Code pinned through conformance.** The Agent SDK goes from 0.3.310 to 0.3.329 (exact, in apps/host and apps/approver), which bundles Claude Code 2.1.329, and the signed range gate (`CLAUDE_CODE_RANGE`) is 2.1.329. What changed between them was read from the SDK's changelog and is recorded in docs/sdk-notes.md: the one breaking change (hook options must be arrays) the Host already met, and the rest is stricter or does not reach it. The shadow warning invariant 4 rests on is unchanged. The conformance suite, the provider suite, the invariants and the range test pass, and a live probe against 2.1.329 showed calls still reach the PreToolUse floor hook and then `canUseTool`, with the Companion's source `sdk`. `npm audit`: 0 vulnerabilities. A dependency change, so the gate ran everything, and `scripts/update-e2e.ts` checked that the last release updates to it.
- **The bypass bar folds (owner, 20 Nov).** Once you bypass a session's limits, its warning bar has Fold it away: it folds to one line, "Limits bypassed: $0.00 of $100.00" (or until when), with Show and End the bypass still on it. The choice is kept for that bypass only (by when it began), so a new bypass shows in full again. Test: `apps/web/test/bypass-fold.test.tsx`.
- **Frontier C-015, adaptive autonomy that only lowers.** Each project can have a cap on how much a session there may do on its own: Plan, Manual, Accept edits, Auto, or Extreme approval (no cap, the default). Five minutes after start, then daily, or with Look at the record now in Settings, Sessions, Autonomy, the last 30 days of the audit log (through D-031's index) are read for each project: 3 floor denies, or 5 of your own denies, cap it at Accept edits; 10, or 15, at Manual. A lower cap is kept (`autonomy.json` in the data folder, read loosely) and recorded as `autonomy_lowered`. A session in that project then starts no higher than the cap (a session asked for Auto starts in Accept edits, and Extreme approval is off), shows the cap, and is refused a higher mode or Extreme approval mid-session, saying why and where to raise it. Nothing automatic ever raises a cap: only Raise in Settings (`autonomy_raised`, from the desktop). A cap you raised comes down again only for what happened after you raised it. A chat is not capped. D-019's proposals to raise build on this. Tests: `apps/host/test/autonomy.test.ts` (including that a clean record never raises a cap), `apps/host/test/autonomy-session.test.ts`, and the autonomy block in `apps/host/test/api.test.ts`.
- **T-087, prompt links (safe variant).** `codehouse://new?prompt=...` opens Codehouse on a draft. The handler is registered for you only (HKCU): by the installer, whose uninstaller removes it (`uninsdeletekey`), and by the window at each start, so an in-app update (which does not run the installer) keeps it pointing at the installed copy; only a copy under your Programs folder registers it, so one built from source never takes it over. `Codehouse.exe --link <link>` (or the link alone) leaves it for the window, as T-002 does a folder, and the page reads it: only `new`, only the prompt (up to 100,000 characters), with every character you cannot see (the G21 set) taken out and named in a warning, and anything else the link names (a mode, a folder, an account, a model) listed as not used. The prompt is shown on the Sessions page and waits in the message box of the session you start next, unsent. Tests: `apps/web/test/prompt-link.test.ts`.
- **T-136, voice through a speech server on this computer (G9).** Settings, Voice: recognised by the system's own voice typing (as before) or a Whisper-compatible server (whisper.cpp's server, faster-whisper-server) at an address you give, which must be on 127.0.0.1, localhost or ::1 with no sign-in in it (invariant 6, ADR 0010: no cloud service and no key). Press the microphone to record and again to stop; the Host passes the clip to `/v1/audio/transcriptions` with your words to expect as the prompt, takes um, uh and the like out of what comes back, and the words go where the caret is. Nothing is kept. The window now lets its own page use the microphone (notifications were the only permission before); the quick prompt's window still allows nothing. Kept in `voice-server.json` in the data folder; changes recorded. **Not built:** downloading and testing models, a project dictionary. Tests: `apps/host/test/voice.test.ts`, `apps/web/test/voice-server.test.tsx`.
- **T-137, the window you were in (the part asked for).** The quick prompt's shortcut with Shift added (Ctrl+Alt+Shift+Space by default; none when yours already has Shift or another program holds it) opens the quick prompt with the window you were in already pictured, and Settings says so. A window picture now carries its program (its description, else its name) and title: the attachment is named after the program and the message has a line such as "(A picture of Microsoft Excel: Budget.xlsx)". **Not built:** accessibility text. Tests: `apps/web/test/window-from.test.ts`.
- **CNW pairing kept across caddy's renewals (owner, 20 Nov, priority; ADR 0020).** Pairing kept breaking, often after a CNW update, leaving the computer "Offline, never connected" until it was deleted and paired again. Confirmed: the Host's log on the owner's computer has "its certificate is not the one it had when you paired" seven times on 17 and 18 Nov, hours apart, each followed by a refusal that was never retried. CNW's Caddyfile says only `tls internal`, so caddy's leaf lasts its default 12 hours with a new key each time, and Codehouse pinned that leaf. caddy's root is in the named `caddy-data` volume, which `up -d --build`, `scripts/update.sh` and the updater's apply and rollback never remove. Now Codehouse pins caddy's root (read from CNW's new `/agent/ca`, believed only once CNW's certificate verifies against it), or the system's store for a public certificate, and every connection is verified by Node against it, chain and address, with no verification turned off. Renewals, restarts and rebuilds keep connecting. A pairing pinned on its leaf moves to the root by itself the first time it connects with the leaf still matching (CNW's welcome carries the root; `cnw_trust_moved` is recorded). A broken one has **Trust CNW's certificate again** in Settings: it shows the root's fingerprint, also on CNW's Server page now, and keeps it, with the same computer id and key, only when you confirm that fingerprint (`cnw_trusted_again`). A real change (another root, a certificate for another address) is still refused: Codehouse says "CNW's certificate changed, so nothing was sent. Trust it again, or pair again" and looks again every 15 minutes. It tells CNW with its id and a signature over `cnw-refused:` and the challenge, which cannot be replayed as a sign-in, so the board says "certificate refused by this computer" rather than "never connected". The pairing file keeps the leaf pin beside the new trust, so an older Codehouse after an undone update reads it as before. CNW is 1.0.1. Tests: `apps/host/test/cnw-trust.test.ts` (against a real TLS server with a chain shaped like caddy's, made with openssl), `apps/cnw/test/hub-certificate.test.ts`, `apps/web/test/cnw-trust-again.test.tsx`, and the existing CNW tests. Checked once against caddy 2.10.2's own internal authority (`scripts/cnw-caddy-check.ts`, caddy's download checked against its published SHA-512): paired on its root, a new leaf with a new key after a restart kept connecting, and a leaf-pinned pairing moved to the root.
- **T-135, a secret for one command (safe variant).** A coding session can call the Companion's `request_secret` with a variable's name, why, and the exact command it is for (allowed by the rule `secret-request-tools`, Host's own Companion only; the tool itself sets and sends nothing). A card shows on the desktop only, never the phone or a push channel (invariants 9 and 11): you type the value or decline, through `/api/secret-requests`, which needs the per-launch token. The value is kept in memory for that session only (it is forgotten when the session ends; nothing is written to disk, so the spec's sealing is not needed), added to the redaction terms at once (invariant 12), and recorded by name only (`secret_requested`, `secret_given` or `secret_declined`, `secret_used`). Claude is told only that it was given. When the session then runs exactly that command with Bash or PowerShell, the call is decided as it stands, without the value; only if it is allowed does Codehouse set the variable for that one run (`export NAME='...' && command`, or `$env:NAME = '...'; command`, single-quoted so nothing in it runs), as an edited input that the floor and the full policy check again (invariant 10). For that check the value is masked, so no evaluator (and never the judge, invariant 15) sees it; a block denies the call. A person who edits the command makes it another command, which gets no secret. One use. Names Codehouse or Claude Code relies on (ANTHROPIC_*, CLAUDE_*, GATEHOUSE_*, PATH, HOME and the like) and a value that is an Anthropic credential are refused (invariant 6). Tests: `apps/host/test/secret-requests.test.ts` and the secrets block in `apps/host/test/api.test.ts`.
- **T-134, 1Password references (safe variant).** Settings, Connectors, 1Password, off by default (`onePassword: true` turns it on, read each time a credential is needed). With it on, the Slack bot and app-level tokens and the ntfy token or password may be a reference such as `op://Private/Slack/bot-token` instead of the value; the sealed store keeps only the reference. Each time the channel needs it, Codehouse runs 1Password's own `op read --no-newline <reference>` directly, with no shell and a 15-second limit, and the value is added at once to the redaction terms (invariant 12), so it is taken out of the audit, logs and anything sent; it is never written anywhere. A reference naming Anthropic or Claude, and a value that is an Anthropic credential, are refused (invariant 6). Off, a reference is refused with a reason rather than sent as it is. No dependency added: `op` is 1Password's own program, used only if you have it. Test: `apps/host/test/secret-providers.test.ts`.
- **For the owner to confirm (T-124):** the invariant 6 test (`apps/host/test/invariants.test.ts`, no Host source reads a token file) had one reviewed exception, the local Relay's own enrolment token. It now also lists `cli-access.ts` and `cli-client.ts`, which write and open the command line's own token, sealed by Codehouse for you; like the Relay's, it is Codehouse's own secret, never a Claude credential, and the test still fails if either file mentions credentials. Every other file is checked as before.
- **T-129, limits pooled across accounts.** Settings, Usage now shows, when two or more accounts are signed in, all their plan limits as one pool: each limit (the 5-hour window, each weekly one) is one bar with a segment for each account that has it, filled as far as that account has used it; the pool's share used; and the next reset still to come with its account and how much of the pool it gives back. Read through the same per-account usage call as the ring, so nothing new is asked of Claude Code. Test: `apps/web/test/plan-pool.test.ts`.
- **T-132, the Host stalling or slow (the part asked for).** Doctor has a Responsiveness check: a timer that should fire every half second notes when it was a second or more late (the Host's event loop held up), not when minutes late (the computer asleep); and a request that took over 5 seconds is noted by its route's pattern only, never its address, query or body, except those that wait on purpose (the WebSocket, the command line waiting for a turn). Anything in the last hour is a warning with the longest of each. Test: `apps/host/test/host-stalls.test.ts`.
- **T-124, scripts on this computer (safe variant).** `npm run codehouse -- list`, `start <folder> [prompt]` (or t3code's `thread start`), `send <session id> <prompt> [--wait]` and `show <session id>`. Off until you switch it on in Settings, Command line (desktop only, recorded as `cli_access`). Switching it on makes a token of its own, sealed for you with the same protection as the phone's keys (DPAPI through the helper on Windows, the Keychain on a Mac), written to the data folder with the Host's port; switching it off deletes it, and switching it on again replaces it. Its routes are under `/cli`, not `/api`: the per-launch token is never given out, the command's token opens nothing under `/api`, and the Host and Origin checks apply as to every request (invariant 7). It can list sessions, start one in a project folder in Manual mode (whatever the request says; never Extreme approval or another mode) through every check a session you start goes through, send a prompt and wait for the turn to end (up to 10 minutes), and read a session's state and last answer. There is no route for a decision, the policy, a mode or the kill switch. Every call is recorded (`cli_call`, with `via: cli`, by action and prompt length, never the prompt). **Not yet:** a `codehouse` program in the installed app, which is a packaging change and waits for `scripts/update-e2e.ts`. No dependency added. Tests: `apps/host/test/cli-access.test.ts`.
- **T-120, routines started by a call (safe variant).** A routine can start when a program on this computer sends `POST http://127.0.0.1:<port>/hook/<routine id>` with the routine's own bearer token. There is no public address (invariant 7): the Host still listens on 127.0.0.1 only, and the Host and Origin checks apply as to every request, so no web page can call it. The token is made by the Host, shown once (with a Copy as a curl command button) and kept only as its SHA-256; an edit keeps it, it is never taken from what the screen sends, and New token replaces it. Placeholders such as `{{action}}` or `{{pull_request.title}}` in the prompt become `[value n]`, with the values listed below the prompt as untrusted data, never instructions, scanned as D-061 scans a page (what looks like injection is taken out); the session counts them as read from outside, so a network call carrying one asks (D-016), and after injection every one does. Up to 5 filters (a field and the value it must have). A delivery seen in the last day (its `X-GitHub-Delivery`, `X-Delivery-Id`, `X-Gitlab-Event-UUID` or `Idempotency-Key`, else the same body) does not start it again. Bursts are held to the trigger caps (one start in 15 minutes, 12 a day): a held call starts when the gap is over, the newest request kept; past the day's number it is refused (429). Every call naming such a routine is recorded (`routine_hook`, refused or not, by result, never the token or the body), and the run is recorded as `routine_run` with `via: webhook`. The body is limited to 64 KB. **Not built:** a public address, a signature check (the token does that job), and forge events polled through T-061. Tests: `apps/host/test/webhooks.test.ts` and the routine's address block in `apps/host/test/api.test.ts`.
- **T-119, scheduled tasks.** A routine can now run once, at a date and time (`once`, due within the hour after it and never again), as well as on days or on a commit or a change; Run now starts one at once, whatever its schedule, recorded as `your request from the desktop` (`POST /api/routines/:id/run`); and each run either starts a new conversation (as before) or, with Each run: Carries on the last one, sends its prompt into the last run's conversation while that is open and idle, else starts a new one (`into`). Pause was there (the On switch). **Not yet:** a fresh session each run shown as one thread, which waits for a session to be put in a sidebar group when it starts. Tests: `apps/host/test/routines-once.test.ts` and Run now in `apps/host/test/api.test.ts`.
- **T-005, drop a folder on the sidebar.** The check it waited on, done: the WebView2 SDK Codehouse builds against (1.0.4314.35) has `postMessageWithAdditionalObjects` and `CoreWebView2File.Path`. A folder dropped on the sidebar goes to the window with the dropped items (`dropToShell`, `apps/web/src/lib/shell.ts`); the window reads its real path (`FolderDropped`, `packaging/CodehouseApp.cs`), checks it (`NavGuard.Dropped`: a folder, not a file, no line break, at most 1,000 characters) and sends the same `open-folder:` message as `Codehouse.exe <folder>` (T-002), so the start-session form opens with it and nothing starts by itself. A file is answered with a toast. **Decided by me:** Windows window only for now; in a browser, CNW or the Mac window (whose WebKit drop is the Mac side's own file) the page says to start a session and choose its folder; the drop is stopped so it never navigates the window. The drop itself cannot be driven by a test here (it needs an OLE drag into a running window); its parts are tested: `apps/host/test/navguard-exe.test.ts` (the compiled decision and its wiring) and `apps/web/test/folder-drop.test.ts`, each failing with its code broken.
- **Notification channels and rules, email invites and password reset (deferred from uplift phases 3 and 4).** Server, Notification channels (an owner or admin): email by SMTP, ntfy, Pushover, Telegram and webhooks (signed with `X-CNW-Signature: sha256=...` when given a secret), each with its own rule: every request, High and Severe, or Severe only; the computers it tells about; quiet hours (across midnight too); a Test button. They are told from the same 15-second look as Web Push, with the same message: the computer, tool, project and risk, never the command. Secrets are sealed and never sent back. No channel reaches CNW's own network (its services, 172.30.0.0/24, this machine, link-local), by address or by a name looked up just before connecting. Email uses a small SMTP client on Node's own net and tls (`apps/cnw/src/channels.ts`; no new dependency): STARTTLS or TLS from the start, the certificate checked, no password before TLS. With email on, invites are emailed as well as shown, and Forgot your password? on sign-in emails a reset link (used once, 30 minutes; the same answer for any email; every device signed out; a second factor still asked). The board groups computers by their first tag. Tests: `apps/cnw/test/channels.test.ts` (fails with the name lookup, the internal-host check, the STARTTLS refusal or the one-use reset taken out) and `apps/cnw/test/page.test.ts` (the groups); threat model T155. **Listed for the owner's decision, with my recommendation:** pairing and second-factor set-up by QR code (no QR encoder is in the repository, a hand-written one cannot be checked here as no decoder runs on Windows, and a broken code would quietly not scan; I recommend adding `qrcode-generator`, small and without dependencies, pinned); Wake-on-LAN (it needs a container on the host's own network listening for CNW, a new way into the host, and this laptop has no Docker to test it; I recommend a separate opt-in compose file with a bearer token, if wanted). Meanwhile the key and link, and pairing by code, work as before.
- **Web Push from CNW (deferred from uplift phase 3).** On You, Notify this device: with the browser's permission, CNW's service worker (`apps/cnw/public/sw.js`) subscribes, and from then on a closed tab or a locked phone hears when something newly waits on a computer that person sees, for every request or High and Severe only, with a test button. CNW looks at its computers every 15 seconds while anyone is subscribed; the first look only learns what waits already. Built on Node's own crypto with no new dependency (`apps/cnw/src/webpush.ts`): VAPID signing (RFC 8292) with a P-256 key made once and kept sealed, and message encryption (RFC 8291, aes128gcm) so only the subscribing browser reads a push. A push says the computer, the tool, the project and the risk band, never the command, as the ntfy channel's rule. **Decided by me:** CNW posts only to the known push services' hosts over https (a subscription naming anything else, CNW's own network included, is refused), so a signed-in page cannot use CNW to reach the updater or another site. A browser that does not trust the certificate refuses the service worker; the page then says to download and trust the root certificate (Server) and reload. On an iPhone it works for Neighbourhood Watch added to the Home Screen (iOS 16.4 and later), which the page says. Tests: `apps/cnw/test/webpush.test.ts` (decrypted as a browser does, the signature checked, and fails with the host check, the computer filter or the risk choice taken out); threat model T154.
- **CNW uplift, phase 6: critique, audit and polish.**
  - **Updates from the page are as safe as the script:** after the updater rebuilds CNW it waits for CNW's own health check (`waitHealthy`, `apps/cnw/src/updater.ts`: the same 48 looks five seconds apart as `scripts/update.sh`), and if CNW is not healthy it goes back by itself to the image and commit it kept, answering "Rolled back". **Decided by me:** the updater keeps its own copy of the rule rather than calling the script, because the page updates to a signed release's exact commit while the script follows `git pull`; the two share the rule, and both are tested.
  - **Intent include and evaluate, read from the code:** each computer on the board is a real link inside a list item (an `a` with role listitem hid it from screen readers); every `confirm()` and `prompt()` became the danger notice, a modal dialog that says what breaks, Cancel first and focused, Escape cancels (`apps/cnw/test/page.test.ts`, which fails on the page before).
  - **Impeccable, critique of every page at 390 and 1440, light and dark:** empty status and error lines no longer leave gaps; dates in UK style ("18 Nov 2026, 20:56"); a signed-in device by name ("Safari on iPhone") rather than its browser string; the roadhouse picture faintly behind sign-in only, as DESIGN.md says; `npx impeccable detect` on the files and on a live page: no anti-patterns and no contrast faults; its advisories fixed (two sizes off the type ramp, the dialog's scrim now a DESIGN.md token, `text-wrap: pretty` so no word sits alone on a last line).
  - **Screenshots** (including those deferred from phases 4 and 5): `docs/images/cnw-you-390-dark.png`, `cnw-server-1440-light.png` (Health, People, Updates, Backup, the audit log), `cnw-signin-390-light.png`, and the board and inbox again.
  - **Tests:** `apps/cnw/test/updater.test.ts` (the health wait; fails when a timeout counts as healthy) and `apps/cnw/test/page.test.ts`.
- **CNW uplift, phase 1 (owner, 17 and 18 Nov; `docs/plan/cnw-uplift.md`):** the product record, the design system and the journeys that phases 2 to 6 build against.
  - **Files:** `apps/cnw/PRODUCT.md` (who CNW is for, its purpose, constraints and principles; facts not in the owner's own words are marked inferred), `apps/cnw/DESIGN.md` ("The watch board": Codehouse's own tokens with explicit dark values, phone first, a bottom tab bar of Board, Inbox, Server and You), and `docs/design/cnw-journeys.md` (the intent, nine journeys with the phase each lands in, the structure, the anti-patterns ruled out, inclusion, and what each phase hands over). The root `PRODUCT.md` and `DESIGN.md` are unchanged.
  - **Decided by me:** CNW drops its amber accent and grey cards for Codehouse's tokens, so moving from CNW into a computer's interface is one product; CNW follows the device's light or dark theme; the roadhouse picture sits only behind sign-in, since on a phone it costs contrast on the board; the pages stay plain HTML, CSS and JavaScript with no build step.
  - **Tools (third-party, as the plan asks to record):** Impeccable was used as the skill already present in this project (its init and design-system method); its hooks were not switched on. Design with Intent was not installed: its skills install into a `.claude` folder, which the floor refuses to write. Its method (intent, journey, organize, fortify, include, specify) and its anti-pattern library were applied by hand from designwithintent.ai. The owner may install it with `npx skills add ghaida/intent --all` for phases 2 to 6.
  - **No code changes**, so no tests and no screenshots in this phase.
- **CNW uplift, phase 2 (first part): email sign-in, and the protocol handshake.**
  - **Email sign-in** (`apps/cnw/src/server.ts`): the email is the sign-in name, matched in any case. First start asks for an email. An account from before (a name and a password) signs in once with its name, is then asked for its email on one page (`POST /api/account/email`, no password asked again since it has just signed in), and can do nothing else until it has one: every route and the computer's live view answer "Add your email first". Its password and passkeys stay; the name stays as the display name and as the name in each computer's audit. After that the name no longer signs in. Changing an email later needs the current password and is throttled; an email used by another account is refused. An older page sending `name` still works.
  - **Protocol handshake** (owner, 18 Nov): Codehouse's connector sends `protocol: 2` in its hello (`CNW_PROTOCOL`, `apps/host/src/cnw/connector.ts`) and CNW answers with its own in `welcome` (`PROTOCOL`, `apps/cnw/src/hub.ts`; 1 means a side from before the number existed, 2 means parts and the resync snapshot). On a mismatch the connection stays up and both say which side needs updating: CNW on its list of computers ("Codehouse needs updating" or "CNW needs updating"), and Codehouse in Settings, Neighbourhood Watch and its log. A new Codehouse with a CNW from before says "CNW needs updating" itself, since that CNW cannot.
  - **Decided by me:** the connection is kept on a mismatch rather than refused, so an older CNW still shows what it can while it waits for its update; nothing is lost silently because both sides say it. The number goes up only when either side's messages change in a way the other must know.
  - **Tests:** `apps/cnw/test/server.test.ts` ("signing in with an email"), `apps/cnw/test/hub.test.ts` and `apps/host/test/cnw-connector.test.ts` ("the protocol handshake"), `apps/web/test/cnw-panel.test.tsx`; threat model T147 and T148. The migration test fails with the "add your email first" check taken out.
  - **Released:** the handshake touches Codehouse, so it goes out in the next desktop release; the CNW side goes to main for `git pull` and a rebuild on the VM.
- **CNW uplift, phase 2 (second part): the Server page.** CNW's address, its kind of certificate and its port are set on its page (`apps/cnw/src/server-settings.ts`, `GET` and `PUT /api/server`), saved in the data volume (`server.json`), and win over `.env`, which is now only the start.
  - **Applying:** a change caddy uses goes to the updater (`POST /apply`, `apps/cnw/src/updater.ts`), which checks it again, answers at once, then runs cnw-config (which now reads `server.json` before `.env`) and recreates caddy and cnw with the port in the environment so Docker publishes it. Behind your own proxy only cnw is recreated, and the trusted proxy is set on the page too (applied at once, no restart).
  - **Safety:** only a signed-in person reads or changes these (an allowed network is refused, `server` joins the actions that always need sign-in). A change that gives a new certificate (another address, or the other kind) names every paired computer it unpairs and is made only when confirmed. Each change goes into CNW's own audit (`audit.json`, the newest 1000, for phase 5's viewer) with who made it.
  - **The certificate:** the page shows the SHA-256 of the certificate caddy serves, read by a TLS handshake with caddy (the one Codehouse pins), in groups of four, and offers caddy's root certificate to download and trust. cnw-config copies the public `root.crt` only into the config volume; cnw never mounts caddy's data, so it cannot read the authority's key.
  - **Deviations, decided by me:** `CNW_HOST` is still needed in `.env` at the first start: from inside its container CNW cannot tell the machine's own address reliably, and a wrong guess would make a certificate for the wrong address. Docker publishes the port from `.env` when the stack is started by hand, so a port saved on the page and a different `CNW_PORT` in `.env` would disagree after a manual `docker compose up`: the page says so and asks for the two to match. Sign-in policy, notifications and updates move to the page in phases 3 to 5, with their features.
  - **Tests:** `apps/cnw/test/server-settings.test.ts` (fails with the sign-in rule, the new-certificate warning or the updater's check taken out); threat model T149.
- **CNW uplift, phase 3: the board, the inbox and notifications, in the new look.**
  - **The board** (`GET /api/board`, `apps/cnw/src/board.ts`): each connected computer is asked for its state (its Host's `/api/state`, at most every three seconds) and kept to what the board shows: sessions working and waiting, what waits, the kill switch and safe mode, never transcripts. Computers with something waiting come first, oldest wait first, then working, idle and offline (with when they were last seen). A request the board cannot read is shown at the highest risk.
  - **The inbox** (`POST /api/inbox/answer`): every waiting request across computers, oldest first, each opening to its pass (the computer, what it would run, why it waits, the rule and the risk as a word, a number and a shape). Deny is one step and first; Allow once is never the default at 70 and over. The answer goes to the computer as from its person (`Hub.commandAs`), whose own rules decide, and is in CNW's audit. The risk is read again from the computer at the moment of the answer. A Severe allow (85 and over) needs a signed-in person, never an allowed network, who signed in or signed in again (`POST /api/reauth`, the password) in the last five minutes.
  - **Notifications:** the page looks again every 10 seconds; anything newly waiting shows as a toast, and as a system notification while a tab is open if you turned them on in You (asked for only when you press the button). **Deferred, decided by me:** Web Push for a closed tab or a locked phone needs a service worker, push keys stored sealed, and a trusted certificate; it comes with the notification rules and channels after phase 5, recorded in the queue, rather than half-built now.
  - **The look** (apps/cnw/DESIGN.md): Codehouse's tokens in light and dark from the device; a slate shell with the Needs you count (yellow only when something waits); a bottom tab bar of Board, Inbox, Server and You on a phone, a rail from 760px; 44px targets; one motion, the time cell flipping in, none with reduced motion. Removing a computer moved from the board to You. Screenshots: `docs/images/cnw-board-390-light.png`, `cnw-inbox-390-light.png`, `cnw-inbox-1440-dark.png`, `cnw-server-1440-light.png` (made from a local CNW with a fake computer).
  - **Tests:** `apps/cnw/test/board.test.ts` (fails with the five-minute rule or the allowed-network refusal taken out); threat model T150.
- **CNW uplift, phase 4: people, roles, a second factor and devices.**
  - **Roles** (`apps/cnw/src/roles.ts`): owner (everything), admin (the server and people, not owners), approver (looks and answers what waits: its tab's commands are approve, deny and answer only, and a computer's own API is read only for it) and viewer (looks only). An account from before roles is an owner. The role is read at every request and every live command. A person can be given only some computers; others answer as not found. There is always one owner, and only an owner invites, changes or removes one.
  - **Invites** (Server, People): an owner or an admin makes an invite for an email and a role; it is a link to copy and send (kept as a hash, used once, for seven days), which opens a page to choose a password. **Deferred, decided by me:** sending it by email, and password reset by email, need SMTP, which comes with the notification channels; until then an owner or an admin sends the link, and a forgotten password is a new invite after removing the person.
  - **Second factor** (`apps/cnw/src/totp.ts`, You): a six-digit code from an authenticator app (RFC 6238 on Node's own crypto, no new dependency), set up with its key and an otpauth link, confirmed with a code, with ten single-use recovery codes shown once. With it on, the password gives only a five-minute ticket for the code; each code works once; signing in again for a Severe allow wants a code too; turning it off wants the password and a code. The secret is sealed (AES-256-GCM) under a key made once in the data volume (`seal.key`, readable only by CNW), which SMTP passwords and push keys will use too. **Decided by me:** a passkey sign-in asks no code, as a passkey is already two factors; the QR code for set-up waits for the QR pairing in phase 5, and the key and link are shown meanwhile.
  - **Signed-in devices** (You): each sign-in records its browser and address; each can be signed out but this one. A removed person is signed out everywhere at once.
  - **Phase 5 (Server):** Health (free space for CNW's data, memory, uptime, whether the updater and caddy answer, each computer's last connections and drops, `GET /api/health`); the audit log of CNW's own changes and answers, newest first, a page at a time (`GET /api/audit`); Updates with the version, the newest signed one and its release notes (a new `notes` field in the signed `cnw-update.json`), going back to the version kept at the last update (the updater tags the running image `codehouse-cnw:previous` and keeps its commit before each update; `POST /api/update/rollback`, an owner or admin signed in within five minutes), and updating by itself at an hour you choose; Backup and restore (`apps/cnw/src/backup.ts`): every data file and the sealing key, encrypted as a whole with a passphrase of 12 or more characters (scrypt, then AES-256-GCM), made and restored only by an owner signed in within five minutes, a restore audited and followed by a restart. Computers can be renamed and tagged (on You). **Deferred, decided by me:** pairing by QR code (it needs a QR encoder; none is in the repository and one must not be added without a reason, so it comes with its own item), Wake-on-LAN (CNW's container sits on a bridge network and cannot send a broadcast to the home network; it needs host networking, a change to the stack's safety to weigh on its own), a release channel other than stable (there is only one), and groups shown as sections on the board (tags show beside each name meanwhile). Tests: `apps/cnw/test/server-care.test.ts` (fails with the recent-sign-in rule taken out); threat model T153.
  - **Tests:** `apps/cnw/test/people.test.ts` (fails with the role check, the computer filter or the last-owner guard taken out) and `apps/cnw/test/totp.test.ts` (RFC 6238's vector; fails with the code step or the one-use rule taken out); threat model T151 and T152. Screenshots of You and People follow with phase 6, taken when the machine is not running the gate.
- **E-097, crash bundle:** Doctor's "Make a crash bundle" shows every file first, then "Save to Downloads" writes exactly the previewed files as a zip. The preview is kept under an id for ten minutes and used once, so the two cannot differ. It holds:
  - the last 2 MB of host.log, redacted;
  - version.json;
  - the Doctor report;
  - the last 200 audit records as when, kind, channel, outcome, rule and risk (never inputs, folders or reasons);
  - the crash codes the log names, with what they usually mean;
  - the settings' key names, never their values.

  Each saved bundle is audited as `crash_bundle_exported` with its file names and size; one that cannot be recorded is deleted. **Decided by me:** the zip is written with Codehouse's own zip writer (`update/zip.ts`); no dependency. Threat T145. Tests: crash-bundle (host and web).
- **E-096, safe mode:** `apps/host/src/safe-mode.ts`.
  - **What it leaves out:** no session brought back (they are kept for later), no routines or triggers, no MCP servers started with Codehouse, the push channels (ntfy, Slack) paused, no warm chat, and every installed plugin off in each session started (`claude plugin list`, then `enabledPlugins` false for each). A banner across the top says why.
  - **How it starts:**
    - after three crash restarts in ten minutes;
    - when a safety canary fails;
    - from Doctor ("Start in safe mode", from the next start);
    - with `GATEHOUSE_SAFE_MODE=1` at start, for the tray.
  - **Leaving:** the banner's "Leave safe mode", on the desktop. The Host's API is the desktop's alone, and Neighbourhood Watch shows the banner without the button. Both are audited: `safe_mode_entered` with the reason, `safe_mode_left`.
  - **Counting crashes:** the Host notes each start in `starts.json` and marks it clean on an orderly stop, so a start before which three starts in ten minutes never stopped cleanly is the fourth after three crashes. Nothing outside the Host is needed.
  - **Decided by me:**
    - The tray's "Start in safe mode" menu item is left for a packaging change of its own (the shell starting the Host with `GATEHOUSE_SAFE_MODE=1`), which needs the update checks run; the Host side is ready.
    - Entering or leaving takes effect from the next start (restart Codehouse), so nothing running is torn down under you.
  - Tests: safe-mode (three crashes; clean stops and old crashes not counted; entered on purpose once; a session in safe mode has every plugin off and no warm chat), safe-mode-banner.
- **CNW was not a faithful live copy of the desktop (owner, 18 Nov).** A long session showed "Nothing yet", and a chat stayed behind, still asking for permission for a Grep already stopped.
  - **Confirmed from the code.** The Host's log shows only reconnects, because the connector logged errors and not close codes; CNW's log is on the owner's VM. The three causes:
    1. CNW's hub closes a computer's connection (1009) on any frame over 4 MB, and a long session's transcript reply was one frame.
    2. When the computer reconnected, the hub only said it was online and never asked for the open tabs' state; the connector had dropped its per-user connections to the Host, so those tabs got no events at all until reloaded.
