    3. The page asked for a conversation's history only for resumed sessions, so a live session opened part way through showed nothing earlier.
  - **The fix:**
    - The connector sends any message over 256 KB as ordered parts. The hub puts them back together (at most 64 MB, eight at once per computer, in order, or the connection is closed). A reply over 64 MB comes back "too large" rather than never.
    - A computer that reconnects is asked for each open tab's state. The connector restarts that user's connection to the Host, so a fresh greeting (sessions, pending prompts, state) reaches every tab; this also fixes a second tab of the same user, which got no greeting before.
    - In CNW, each greeting reads every session's conversation from the computer and replaces what the page had, so a gap leaves nothing missing and nothing doubled.
    - The page shows "Reconnecting to this computer..." until the greeting has arrived.
    - The connector now logs close codes.
  - **Event types:** every event type reaches CNW tabs, in order, because CNW's per-user connections are ordinary clients of the Host's server.
  - **Decided by me, theme:** CNW's /app view keeps its own theme per browser. The desktop's theme is a setting of the desktop window's browser storage, not of the Host, so there is no computer setting to read. Light or dark on a phone usually follows the phone. Recorded here as asked.
  - **Tests:** cnw-sync (a 6 MB message through the real connector and hub; a forced drop and reconnect leaves the tab with the desktop's state, nothing missing or twice; a prompt withdrawn during a drop disappears), hub (parts and their limits; a returning computer asked for each user's state), cnw-history (the page reads and replaces each conversation on every greeting). Each fails without its fix. Threat T143.
- **No stop at the start picture (owner, 17 Nov: "The start screen never had buttons. It shouldnt need buttons now. It should just load into the main screen on the computer it is connected to").**
  - The window opens on the main screen, the workspace (`MAIN_ROUTE` in `apps/web/src/store.ts`), or the page you were on. An older version's saved Home opens the main screen too.
  - Home is the picture alone, reached from the command deck: the invisible buttons laid over the drawn ones are gone.
  - In Neighbourhood Watch the page counts setup as done (setup belongs to the computer itself) and opens on that computer's main screen.
  - While looking at this, the invisible buttons were found to be aligned and clickable in a headless browser, so they were removed rather than mended.
  - Tests: home (no buttons; where the window opens, desktop and CNW), restore, app.
- **Lifecycle invariant test made patient:** it read the session's state the moment the broker held the request, before the session said it was waiting. Under load that failed a release. It now waits for the state. Only the test changed.

## Why Codehouse stopped: keeping the Host's last words (21 Nov, owner: "This has happened a few times")

host.log shows the Host stopping with code 0xC0000409 about ten times between 13 and 21 November, and restarting by itself each time. That code is Node aborting on a fatal error (the JavaScript heap running out, or an internal assertion); Windows kept no crash record, and the launcher held the Host's error output only in memory, so the reason was lost. The Host used about 460 MB fifteen minutes after the last restart, against a heap limit of about 4 GB, so a slow growth or a native assertion are both possible; nothing in host.log ties the stops to one feature (Neighbourhood Watch's reconnect loop is busy near several, but stops began on 2 November, before it existed).

- **The launcher** (`packaging/CodehouseApp.cs`) keeps the Host's last 40 error lines and, when it stops unexpectedly, writes them with the exit code to `last-stop.txt` in the data folder.
- **The Host** (`apps/host/src/last-stop.ts`), at its next start (seconds later, since it restarts itself), redacts that note with your redaction list and the built-in secret patterns, adds it to host.log line by line, and deletes it. It also notes its memory in host.log every 15 minutes (heap used, the heap limit, all memory, memory outside the heap), so a slow growth shows before a stop.
- **The Mac** launcher (`packaging/mac/AppDelegate.swift`) can write the same `last-stop.txt`; the Host side is shared. Left for the Mac machine (a platform hook).

The next stop will say why in host.log. Test: `apps/host/test/last-stop.test.ts`.

## Expansion E3 to E6 (from 22 Nov)

The expansion items in waves E3 to E6 (`docs/plan/expansion-build-prompt.md`), in file order, after the frontier build.
- **E-025, an environment manifest and drift.** A session's menu has Environment... (`POST /api/environment`, `apps/host/src/env-manifest.ts`): Codehouse asks node, npm, pnpm, the .NET SDKs, python, pwsh, git and java for their versions with fixed commands (a tool that is not there is left out), reads what the project asks for (.nvmrc or .node-version, global.json's SDK, rust-toolchain, package.json engines), and keeps both in the project's `.codehouse/environment.json` with the names of the environment variables, never a value. Where an installed version does not meet what is asked for (the common range forms: 22, 22.x, ^, ~, >=, <, ||), that is drift and the panel lists it, such as engines asking for Node 22 on a computer running Node 24, or a global.json SDK that is not installed. Test: `apps/host/test/env-manifest.test.ts` (that drift reported; no variable value stored).
- **E-027, Dev Drive and Defender awareness.** Doctor has a Project volumes line (`apps/host/src/devdrive.ts`): for each open project, the volume it is on, Dev Drive, ReFS or NTFS, and Defender's performance mode for Dev Drives (on, off, or not known), read once a start with one fixed PowerShell query (Win32_LogicalDisk, `fsutil devdrv query`, `Get-MpPreference`) that never asks to run as administrator; whatever needs that is reported as not known, and only drive letters reach the query. With no project on a Dev Drive, it says how to make one. On a Mac it is not shown. Test: `apps/host/test/devdrive.test.ts` (the volume type for each project; nothing elevated in the query; a real read on Windows with no prompt).
- **E-029, port and process tracking.** A session's menu has Ports and processes... (`apps/host/src/session-procs.ts`, the `session_processes` and `stop_process` messages): the processes under the session's Claude Code process (from its job's root, as T-113 finds its ports), each with how long it has run and the ports it listens on; Claude Code's and Codehouse's own processes are never listed. A process stops only after Stop... and a second click to confirm, recorded first, and only while it is still under that session (with what it started). A Bash or PowerShell call that would start a server (an explicit --port, -p or PORT, or the usual port of vite, next, nuxt, react-scripts, ng serve, http.server, runserver, flask or dotnet run) on a port something already listens on goes to you first as `port-clash`, saying what holds the port. One fixed PowerShell query on Windows; on a Mac nothing is listed. The plan's offer to stop them at session end is this panel: a session in its job object ends its processes with it, as before. Test: `apps/host/test/session-procs.test.ts` (a fixture dev server found with its port; nothing stopped without confirmation; the clash asked about).
### E-026 Container runs for Codehouse actions (feat/e3)

- `apps/host/src/container-runs.ts`: the setting `containerRuns` (`enabled`, `image`), off unless switched on. Podman is found on PATH. A heavy job (held-out and sealed checks, a mutation run's test reruns, a breaker's tests) runs as `podman run --rm --network=none --read-only --tmpfs /tmp -v <project>:/work:ro -v <scratch>:/scratch -w /work -e HOME=/scratch <image> sh -c <command>`, still inside the session's job object when Codehouse-Limits is present. The scratch folder is per session under the data folder. An image name that could read as an option is refused and the default (`docker.io/library/node:22-alpine`) used.
- The command is judged by the policy as written before anything starts, so a container only confines further; forge actions and the fast-forward pull, which need the network, never use it.
- Doctor has a "Container runs" line: on, off, or "Unavailable: Podman is not on this computer" (a warning only when switched on). Settings, Connectors, has the switch and the image field; other keys a newer version keeps in `containerRuns` stay.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/container-runs.test.ts`, with a stand-in podman script that records its arguments (no network flag, read-only mount, scratch volume, a denied command starts nothing, unavailable without Podman). Six breaks, all caught.
- Stricter-only decision for the owner to confirm: none; the feature is off by default.
### E-012 Schema diff view (feat/e3)

- `apps/host/src/schema-diff.ts` reads the database schema a project keeps, the first found of: a Prisma schema, an EF Core model snapshot, SQL migrations (as E-011's `migrationKind` finds them, applied in name order) or a DDL file. Before is the last commit (`git show HEAD:<file>`, a migration not yet committed is left out), after is the working folder. SQL is read by statements, as E-011 does: CREATE TABLE, DROP TABLE, and ALTER TABLE's ADD, DROP, RENAME and ALTER or MODIFY of a column's type and RENAME TO; comments are taken out first. Prisma's scalar fields (relation fields and lists left out, `@map` and `@@map` followed) and EF Core's `Property<T>("Name")` under `ToTable` are read the same way.
- The difference is a Mermaid ER diagram of the changed tables only, each column marked "added", "removed" or "changed from <type>", saved in the session's artifacts as "Schema changes" (C-057's sandbox draws it, strict and offline). Names and types are reduced to letters, digits, underscores and hyphens, so a schema cannot carry Mermaid directives into it.
- The session's menu has "Schema changes..." (`SchemaDiffPanel`): the change in words, one line a table, and a button to the diagram. Window message `schema_diff`; read only. `migrationKind` is now exported from `@gatehouse/policy`.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/schema-diff.test.ts` (SQL applied in order; an added column shown as added and a dropped one as removed, unchanged tables left out; hostile names; Prisma and EF Core; where a schema is found; a real repository's last commit against its working folder). Ten breaks, all caught (one after a comment was added to the fixture).
### E-013 Migration rehearsal (feat/e3b)

- `apps/host/src/migration-rehearsal.ts` finds a project's SQL migrations by convention (name.up.sql with name.down.sql; one file with goose, sql-migrate or dbmate markers; Flyway's V<n>__ with its U<n>__ undo; any other migration E-011 recognises, up only, Prisma's named by its folder) in version order, and runs each one up, then down, comparing the schema with how it was before, then up again before the next.
- A migration with no down step, or whose down step leaves the schema different, is reported as irreversible; a step that fails stops the rehearsal and is reported with its first error line.
- SQLite runs in the Host (node:sqlite, already used by chats and the code index) on a new file in the temporary folder, deleted with its journal files afterwards whatever happened. Migrations written for Postgres (by words only it uses) run only in an E-026 container: `postgres:17-alpine`, no network, the migrations mounted read-only, a database made inside it with initdb and gone with it, each step marked in its output and the schema compared by a hash of `pg_dump -s`. Without container runs they are reported as not rehearsed, with what to switch on; SQL Server migrations are reported as not rehearsed.
- **Decided by me (stricter):** a migration that uses ATTACH, VACUUM, load_extension or a store-directory pragma is not run at all, since each could write a file outside the throwaway database (threat model T160).
- The session's menu has "Rehearse migrations..." (`RehearsalPanel`); window message `migration_rehearse`, recorded as `migration_rehearsed` with its counts.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/migration-rehearsal.test.ts` (up and down on SQLite with one irreversible and the file deleted; a down that leaves the schema different and a failing step; nothing outside the throwaway database; the conventions and their order; Postgres left to a container and SQL Server alone; the container script and its output read back). Ten breaks, all caught.
### E-014 Query plan review (feat/e3b)

- `apps/host/src/query-plan.ts` finds the SQL queries a session's change adds: tree-sitter (C-031's grammars) over its TypeScript, JavaScript, Python and C# files, every string literal that starts like a query (SELECT, WITH, UPDATE, DELETE, INSERT) on a line the change adds (from `git diff HEAD`, sast.ts's `addedLines`; a new file counts every line), interpolations read as placeholders. Placeholders of the common drivers ($1, :name, @name, %s, %(name)s) are read as SQLite's ?.
- The project's migrations (E-013's finder) build a throwaway SQLite database in the temporary folder, deleted afterwards, and each query is run through EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN, which compiles it without running it. A full scan of a table is reported with an index on the columns its WHERE compares (`CREATE INDEX users_email ON users (email);`), and a temporary sort for an ORDER BY is noted.
- A query of more than one statement, or one with ATTACH, VACUUM or load_extension, is not explained. Postgres and SQL Server migrations are said to be out of reach here: E-013 builds them only in a container, and plans there would need the container's client as well (left for later).
- The session's menu has "Query plans..." (`QueryPlanPanel`): each query with a link to its line, what its plan does, and the index to copy. Window message `query_plan`, recorded as `query_plans_read` with its counts.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/query-plan.test.ts` (queries found in the three languages and only on changed lines; an unindexed fixture query reports a scan of users and suggests `CREATE INDEX users_email_name ON users (email, name);` while an indexed one uses its index; several statements and errors; placeholders). Ten breaks, all caught (one after a %s placeholder was added to the fixture).
### E-015 Synthetic fixtures with PII scan (feat/e3b)

- `apps/host/src/fixtures.ts`. Generating: seed rows for every table the project's migrations make (E-013's throwaway SQLite database, deleted afterwards), from a small generator of Codehouse's own rather than faker (a seeded mulberry32 and a few word lists are all it needs, so no dependency). The same seed (the project's name) gives the same rows. Values follow the column: an email at example.com, a phone number in Ofcom's drama range (07700 900xxx), names from an obviously made-up list, dates from 2020, a post code of ZZ99 9ZZ. The schema's rules are kept: NOT NULL, UNIQUE and primary keys (a unique column too short for its value gets the row's number in base 36), a varchar's length, and foreign keys to rows of tables made first (tables are ordered by what they refer to). Every row is inserted with foreign keys switched on, so SQLite checks them, CHECK constraints included; a refused row is made again with other values up to five times and otherwise reported.
- Scanning: files under fixtures, seeds, testdata, test/data and mocks folders, or named like a fixture or a seed. Flagged: an email at a domain that is not reserved (RFC 2606 and 6761: example.com/.org/.net, .example, .test, .invalid, .localhost), a UK phone number outside Ofcom's drama ranges, a National Insurance number in the real format (with the prefixes never issued left out), and a card number that passes the Luhn check and is not a documented test card (E-093's `isRealCard`, whose `CARD_CANDIDATE` is now exported). A finding is the file, the line, the kind and the value mostly masked (a card only by its last four digits).
- The session's menu has "Test data..." (`TestDataPanel`): the findings with links to their lines, and "Make synthetic seed rows", which shows the INSERT statements to copy; nothing is written into the project. Window message `test_data`, recorded as `test_data_checked` with counts and kinds only.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/fixtures.test.ts` (25 rows a table that a fresh database with the same schema and its rules switched on takes, unique emails at example.com within their lengths, foreign keys that join, and the generated rows pass the scan; the same seed gives the same rows; refused rows are made again, or reported; a real-looking email, phone, NI number and card are flagged and masked while reserved and test ones are not; only test data is scanned). The card number in the test is made at run time, so none is written in the repository. Fourteen breaks, all caught (one after a check that half the values fail was added).
### E-018 IaC plan as policy (feat/e3c)

- `packages/policy/src/iac-plan.ts` reads plan output in four shapes into one list of changes: `terraform show -json` (and OpenTofu's), Bicep and ARM what-if JSON, a CloudFormation change set (`describe-change-set`, Replacement True or Conditional read as a replacement) and `pulumi preview --json`. Deleting or replacing a resource that keeps data (databases, storage accounts and buckets, key vaults, secret stores and keys, queues, topics and streams, disks and volumes, backups and snapshots, by provider type in all four namings; settings around a store, such as a bucket policy, a subnet group or a firewall rule, do not count) is `iac-destroys-stateful` at Severe (90), naming each resource with its type. Opening something new to the internet (an ingress from 0.0.0.0/0, ::/0, * or Internet; publicly accessible, public network access or public blob access switched on; a public-read ACL; a public access block switched off; a principal of *; a new public address) is `iac-public-exposure` at High (75); exposure a resource already had is not counted again.
- `apps/host/src/iac-plans.ts` keeps, per session, the last plan each tool printed (the PostToolUse hook passes a successful Bash or PowerShell call's output on, `onCommandOutput`), and reads plan files in the session's folder and its direct subfolders written in the last six hours. An apply (`terraform`/`tofu`/`terragrunt apply`, `az deployment ... create`, `New-Az*Deployment`, `aws cloudformation execute-change-set` or `deploy`, `pulumi up`) inherits the strictest finding of those plans in canUse, which only ever raises a verdict.
- **Decided by me (stricter):** an apply whose plan Codehouse has not seen goes to a person under `iac-plan-unseen` at High (75), saying which command shows the plan as JSON.
- **"Extreme never approves an apply that deletes"** follows the owner's 14 and 15 Nov decisions, as E-011 and E-016 did: Rules-based Extreme approval waits on it (90 is over its limit of 80); Extreme max approval approves it.
- Threat model T161. Tests: `packages/policy/test/iac-plan.test.ts` with 23 golden plans in `packages/policy/test/iac-golden.json` across the four tools (12 Severe, 6 High, 5 clean), plus stateful and exposure reading and apply detection; `apps/host/test/iac-plans.test.ts` (a plan that deletes a database escalates the apply as Severe naming it; Rules-based Extreme waits on it; a clean plan lets the apply through and an unseen one holds it; recent plan files count and old ones do not; plans stay with their session and the strictest wins; the hook passes on only successful output). Eighteen breaks, all caught (one after the strictest-plan case got two findings).
### E-024 Blast radius preview (feat/e3c)

- `blastRadius` in `packages/policy/src/iac-plan.ts`: for each resource a plan deletes or replaces, what depends on it, directly or through other resources, from the plan's dependency graph: Terraform's configuration (each resource's `depends_on` and the resources its expressions refer to, modules included, a counted resource found by its configuration address) and Pulumi's step dependencies (unchanged resources included). Data sources are left out. Up to 30 dependents a resource and 10 resources, with how many more. ARM what-if and CloudFormation change sets carry no graph, so they show none.
- The approval card of an apply (`apps/host/src/iac-plans.ts` `blastFor`, through canUse's new `iacBlast`) shows "Blast radius": what it deletes or replaces, and what depends on each. `blast` is a new optional field of the escalation request (`Blast` in contracts.ts), redacted like the rest; it goes into the phone's end-to-end encrypted payload only when there is one, so a request without it hashes as it always did (invariant 8). The phone app does not draw it yet.
- Tests: `packages/policy/test/iac-blast.test.ts` (a replaced virtual network lists its subnet, NIC and machine but not a data source or a module's own reference; Pulumi's graph; a counted resource; the cap); `apps/host/test/iac-plans.test.ts` (the card request of an apply carries the blast radius; a request without one hashes as before). Nine breaks, all caught (one after the counted-resource case was added).
### E-019 Infrastructure cost delta (feat/e3d, the safe variant)

- `apps/host/src/price-sheet.ts`: a price sheet (`{ v: 1, currency, generated, items: [{ type, sku, region?, low, high }] }`, a monthly cost as a low and a high figure per resource type and SKU) signed like D-057's vulnerability snapshot: ECDSA P-256 over the file's bytes, SHA-256, base64. The key that checks it is in the managed folder, which only an administrator can change (`prices-key.json`, or `osv-key.json` when there is none of its own). The sheet is read from the managed folder, or from the data folder when imported in Settings, Connectors, Price sheet (window message `price_sheet_import`, recorded either way); it is kept only when it verifies, and a sheet that is unsigned, signed with another key or changed afterwards is not used at all. Nothing is fetched. Doctor shows the sheet in use, or why it was refused, only when there is one.
- An apply's card shows "Cost a month": the change its plan makes as an interval (a creation adds its low to high, a deletion takes its high to low away, a resize counts the difference), from each resource's SKU (instance class or type, node or machine type, VM size, `sku.name`, in all four plan namings) and region (an availability zone is priced as its region). A SKU the sheet does not know is listed as "Not known". `cost` is a new optional field of the escalation request, redacted, and in the phone's encrypted payload only when there is one (invariant 8 unchanged for requests without it).
- **List for IT (here, whoever looks after this computer, owner 9 Nov):** where the price sheet comes from (the cloud providers' published price lists, or the organisation's agreed rates, turned into the format above) and how often it is refreshed (monthly is suggested; `generated` is shown in Doctor). Signing uses the same key as the vulnerability snapshot unless a `prices-key.json` is placed beside it.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/price-sheet.test.ts` (a known SKU gives an interval and an unknown one is not known; a resize; no SKU, no cost; unsigned, other-key and changed sheets refused; import only when it verifies, and a sheet changed after import no longer used; only applies get a cost; the card request carries it). The signing key is made at run time. Nine breaks, all caught.
### E-020 Least-privilege IAM diff (feat/e3e)

- `packages/policy/src/iam.ts` finds grants broader than they need to be, each with a narrower alternative: an action of * (or a whole service such as s3:*), a resource of * for anything but Get, List and Describe actions, * on *, Owner or Contributor at subscription scope (by name or built-in role id; a resource group scope is not flagged), Owner or Editor on a Google project, and administrator roles (AdministratorAccess, IAMFullAccess, PowerUserAccess, User Access Administrator, Role Based Access Control Administrator, roles/owner, roles/iam.securityAdmin and the like). Read by words in JSON and YAML policy documents, Terraform, Bicep, ARM and CloudFormation; every item of a list counts.
- An edit (Write, Edit, MultiEdit) of a .tf, .tf.json, .hcl, .bicep, .json, .yaml or .template file that adds such a grant escalates in the engine under `broad-iam` at High (80), only for what it adds (a subscription scope set elsewhere in the file counts). An apply whose plan creates or widens one is held too (E-018's `judgePlan`, after a stateful deletion and before an exposure), with policy JSON inside the plan's strings read as well.
- Tests: `packages/policy/test/iam.test.ts` (* on * escalates with its suggestion; only what an edit adds; each kind of grant, and the narrow forms not flagged; a plan's broad grant and an unchanged one; the order with other findings) and 13 golden cases in `packages/policy/test/golden/31-iam.yaml` (8 broad, 5 narrow). Thirteen breaks, all caught (one after the resource group case got a realistic subscription id, which showed the scope match giving up a digit to pass; fixed).
### E-022 In-process IaC misconfiguration checks (feat/e3e)

- `packages/policy/src/iac-lint.ts` reads Terraform, Bicep, ARM and CloudFormation into one list of resources and checks them for public storage (public ACLs and AccessControl, public access blocks switched off, public blob access, a bucket granted to allUsers), open security groups (an ingress from 0.0.0.0/0, ::/0, * or Internet, read as E-018 reads a plan), missing encryption (RDS, EBS, EFS and Redshift unset or false; DynamoDB server-side encryption and Azure SQL transparent data encryption switched off), weak TLS minimums (below 1.2 in any naming, and old load balancer TLS policies) and missing diagnostics (a key vault, SQL server or storage account with no diagnostic setting naming it in the files; a load balancer without access logs). Each finding has its file, line and a fix.
- **Parsers, recorded as asked (`IAC_PARSERS`):** the tree-sitter grammars Codehouse ships (VS Code's set, C-031) have none for HCL, Bicep or YAML. HCL and Bicep are read by a small block reader of Codehouse's own (blocks, labels, attributes, lists, objects, strings and comments; expressions kept as their text); ARM by JSON; CloudFormation by JSON or YAML with the yaml package the policy already uses, which reads short-form tags such as !Ref as plain values. No dependency was added.
- The session's menu has "Infrastructure checks..." (`IacCheckPanel`): every infrastructure file in the project (up to 2,000, each under 2 MB, node_modules and .terraform left out), the findings with links to their lines. Window message `iac_check`, read only, recorded as `iac_checked` with counts. It does not hold calls: applies are already held by their plans (E-018, E-020).
- E-018's exposure reader now also reads CloudFormation's `AccessControl: PublicRead`.
- Tests: `packages/policy/test/iac-lint.test.ts` with fixtures in `packages/policy/test/iac-lint/`: 11 seeded misconfigurations across the four formats are each found once with their lines, and the benign fixtures (Terraform, Bicep, CloudFormation) are clean; the block reader; format detection. Fourteen breaks: twelve caught at once; for the three missed, a filter that could not be reached and a redundant tag stripping were taken out, and the fixture's comment now looks like a setting, so a reader that read comments would be caught.
### E-031 Contract-first conformance probe (feat/e3f)

- `apps/host/src/conformance-probe.ts` reads an OpenAPI 3.x spec in the project (JSON or YAML; `parseYaml` is now re-exported from `@gatehouse/policy`, whose pinned yaml package it is, so the Host adds no dependency), makes one valid request for each operation (path parameters, required query parameters and a JSON body from each schema's example, enum, default or type, minimums and formats kept, local `$ref`s followed) and sends it to the app on this computer. Each answer is checked against the spec with ajv 8.20.0, which the Host already pins (OpenAPI 3.0's `nullable` read as a type that allows null): a status the spec does not list, a body that does not match (such as a missing required field, named by its path) or no answer is a mismatch.
- **Safety:** only a server on this computer is probed (127.0.0.1, any 127.x address, ::1 or localhost); any other address, from the spec or given, is refused before anything is sent. Each request is first judged by the rules as the same curl command from the session would be, and only one the rules allow by themselves is sent; any other is listed as not sent. Redirects are not followed. The probe runs when you start it from the session's menu ("Contract probe...", window message `contract_probe`, the spec a file inside the project) and is recorded as `contract_probed`.
- **Deviation (recorded):** the requests are sent by the Host itself rather than as a section 6 shell action, since they are HTTP requests, not a command. Each is still judged by the rules first, as above.
- The last probe goes into the session's evidence pack (`contract`: when, where, how many operations, the mismatches). While it has mismatches, every claim of done in that session is Contradicted, saying what did not match, until a probe passes.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/conformance-probe.test.ts`, against a real app on 127.0.0.1: a missing required field is reported and matching answers pass, with valid requests sent; a server elsewhere is refused with nothing sent; a request the rules would not allow by themselves is not sent; a status the spec does not list and nullable; and on a real session a claim of done is Contradicted while the probe has mismatches.
- Built and committed together with E-032 to E-037 (one commit): they share the API contract panel, its window messages and the server's cases.
### E-032 Mock definitions from specs (feat/e3f, the safe variant)

- `apps/host/src/mock-gen.ts` makes mocks for the project's own tools from an OpenAPI 3.x spec in the project: MSW handlers (msw 2's `http` and `HttpResponse`, one for each operation, the path's `{id}` as `:id`), WireMock mappings (method and a path pattern, the answer's status and JSON body) and the Prism command that serves the spec on this computer only (`--host 127.0.0.1`). Each operation answers its first success response (or default), with a body made from its schema as E-031's probe makes requests (a response `$ref` followed; no body for 204).
- In the "Contract probe..." panel, "Make mocks" shows them with a copy button each (window message `mock_defs`, read only). Nothing is written into the project.
- **List for SECURITY (not built):** a mock server hosted by Codehouse itself would be a listener without a bearer token, against invariant 7, so the Prism command runs the project's own tool instead.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/mock-gen.test.ts`: the generated MSW handlers compile with the TypeScript compiler (strict, against a stand-in for msw's types, since msw is not a dependency here) and cover all three operations, with their statuses and bodies; WireMock patterns match their paths and nothing deeper; the Prism command is loopback only; a file that is not OpenAPI 3 makes nothing.
### E-033 Spec breaking-change detector (feat/e3f)

- `packages/policy/src/spec-diff.ts` compares an edit to an OpenAPI 3 or AsyncAPI spec (YAML or JSON; Write, Edit and MultiEdit applied to the file on disk) with the spec as it is. Breaking: a removed operation or channel; a removed field in what clients read (a 2xx JSON answer, an AsyncAPI message payload, local `$ref`s followed); a changed type (integer to number is allowed); a changed enum; and in what clients send, a new required parameter or request field, a shorter maximum length, a higher minimum or a lower maximum. An added optional field, a new operation, a looser limit or a request field dropped pass.
- A breaking change without raising the major part of `info.version` escalates in the engine under `spec-breaking-change` at 70, naming up to five changes and saying to raise the major version or keep the old shape. With the major version raised it passes.
- **Deviation (recorded):** the item says "from git"; the "before" is the file on disk, so each change is judged once, as it is made. Comparing with the last commit would hold every later edit for the same change again.
- Tests: `packages/policy/test/spec-diff.test.ts` (a removed response field escalates, naming it; an added optional field and a new operation pass; each kind of breaking change, and looser changes not; a major bump lets a break through; AsyncAPI channels and messages; other JSON files left alone).
### E-034 Contract snapshots (feat/e3f)

- `apps/host/src/contract-snapshots.ts`: each contract probe (E-031) keeps the shape of every answer it got, per project (a file named by a hash of the project's folder, under `contract-snapshots` in the data folder, written atomically): an object's keys with their shapes, an array's first item, or a type name. **Decided by me (stricter):** no value is ever kept, so there is nothing to redact; keys go through the redaction list anyway.
- The next probe of the project reports how each answer changed since: a key gone or new, a type changed, a status changed, with the date of the last snapshot. The "Contract probe..." panel shows them under "Changed since the last probe". Shapes stay in the Host; the panel gets only the changes.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/contract-snapshots.test.ts` (a changed shape is reported, nothing when unchanged, a status change; only shapes kept, keys redacted, projects apart; arrays, nulls and types).
### E-035 API security lint (feat/e3f)

- `apps/host/src/api-lint.ts`: rules over an OpenAPI 3 spec, after OWASP's API Security Top 10. Flagged: an operation with no security (none of its own and none for the whole spec, or `security: []` switching it off); a secured operation with no 401 or 403 answer (a 4XX range counts); an object id in a path (`id`, `userId`, `order_id`, `uuid`) with no word about who may reach it in its summary, description or parameter, and no `x-authorization` note; an array with no `maxItems` in an answer or request; no documented rate limit anywhere (a 429 answer, RateLimit or Retry-After headers, or an `x-rate-limit` note); and a sensitive name in a query string (password, token, API key, secret, card, session and the like). Each finding names the operation and what to do.
- In the "Contract probe..." panel, "Check the spec" lists them (window message `api_lint`, read only).
- Tests: `apps/host/test/api-lint.test.ts` (an unauthenticated operation, and one with security switched off, are flagged; each other gap where it is; a spec that covers them is clean).
### E-036 Generated client drift (feat/e3f)

- `apps/host/src/client-drift.ts` finds the project's API client generators: orval (`orval.config.*`, its output target and input), openapi-generator (`openapitools.json`'s generators, output and input spec), NSwag (`.nswag`, each code generator's output and the document it reads) and Kiota (`kiota-lock.json`, its folder and description). Paths are resolved from the config's folder; a URL, an absolute path or one outside the project is not used.
- "Check generated clients" in the API contract panel (window message `client_drift`) makes each client again with the project's own tool (`npx --no-install orval`, `npx --no-install openapi-generator-cli generate`, `dotnet nswag run`, `kiota update`) in a scratch folder inside the project's `.codehouse` folder, so the tool resolves from the project's own node_modules by walking up, with no link to clean up. Only the config and its spec are copied in. The tool runs as a Codehouse action (section 6: judged by the rules, no credentials, at the session's level). The fresh client is compared with the committed one file by file (added, gone, changed; line endings ignored), and the scratch folder is deleted afterwards whatever happened. Recorded as `client_drift_checked`.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/client-drift.test.ts`, with a stand-in generator run in the scratch folder: a spec change with no regenerated client reports drift, a client that matches reports none, a refused or failed generator says why, and no scratch folder is left; the four configs are found, and paths outside the project are refused.
### E-037 Error contract consistency (feat/e3f)

- `apps/host/src/error-contract.ts` checks error answers (4xx, 5xx and default) against RFC 9457 problem details (type, title, status, detail, served as application/problem+json), or against the project's own convention when its spec has one: the schema at least two error answers name, when that schema is not problem details.
- In a spec ("Check the spec" in the API contract panel, beside E-035's findings): an error answer with no body, one without a type field, one served as plain JSON instead of problem+json, or one that does not follow the project's shape. In a probe (E-031): an error answer the app gave with no body, no type field, a status in the body that differs from the answer's, or the wrong content type, listed apart under "Error answers that break the error contract" and not counted against claims of done.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/error-contract.test.ts` (an error without a type field is flagged, as is one with no body; the project's convention is followed when it has one; probe bodies) and a case in `apps/host/test/conformance-probe.test.ts` (the probe lists an error answer without a type field and does not count it as a mismatch). Six breaks, all caught.
### E-038 Local log capture and viewer (feat/e4a)

- `apps/host/src/log-capture.ts`: a log store per project in the data folder (`logs`, a file named by a hash of the project's folder). Lines come from what Codehouse's own commands print (every section 6 action, through a new `onFinished` hook on the action runner, at all five places the Host runs one) and from the log files a project lists in `.codehouse/logs.yml` (`files: [path, ...]`), each read from where it was last read (its first read takes the last 256 KB).
- Each line is parsed: JSON lines into time, level, message and their other fields (pino's and bunyan's number levels, Serilog's compact names), logfmt into its pairs, and the common "time level message", "[time] [level] message" and "LEVEL: message" forms. Everything is redacted before it is kept (invariant 12), message, fields and source.
- **Safety:** a listed file is read only when the rules allow reading it by themselves (the same Read judged by the Approver); the floor's denials, secrets files and anything held are refused, and the viewer says which.
- The session's menu has "Logs..." (`LogsPanel`, window message `logs_query`): search by words, lowest level and time (the last hour, the last day, everything), newest first. Settings has the retention (`logRetentionDays`, 1 to 90 days, 7 when not set); older lines are dropped as new ones come in.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/log-capture.test.ts` (a JSON line parsed into fields; logfmt and text forms; a secret masked in message and fields before it is kept; search, level, time and retention; only new lines read from a listed file, and one the rules refuse is not read; an action's output reaches the store; and, with the real rules, a listed path the floor denies is refused).
- Eight breaks, all caught (E-038's share of the batch's 25).
### E-039 Error signature triage (feat/e4a)

- `apps/host/src/error-triage.ts` finds the errors in a project's log lines (E-038): a line that names an error type (`TypeError: ...`, `...Exception`), or any error or fatal line, with the stack frames that follow it from the same source (JavaScript, Python and .NET frames). Each is reduced to a signature: its type, its message with ids, numbers, quoted values, UUIDs, addresses, emails and times taken out, and its top frame outside node_modules. Two errors that differ only in ids are one signature, counted, with when it was first and last seen.
- A JavaScript frame in the project whose file has a source map (`file.js.map`, or its `sourceMappingURL`) is mapped back to its source with Node's own `SourceMap` (no dependency). A map is read only from inside the project; a data URL or a web address is not followed.
- In the Logs panel, "Errors" lists the signatures, most frequent first, with their frames, and "Ask Claude to fix" sends the session a fix task: the error, how often and when, where it was raised, and to fix it with a test that fails first (window message `log_errors`, read only; the prompt goes as any prompt you send).
- Tests: `apps/host/test/error-triage.test.ts` (two errors differing only in ids are one signature, counted; what is taken out of messages; the three frame forms; a frame mapped through a source map, and one outside the project not; the fix task).
- Four breaks; one missed until the source map case used a real file outside the project; all caught.
### E-040 Local trace receiver (feat/e4a)

- `apps/host/src/trace-receiver.ts`: an OTLP/HTTP receiver for traces in JSON (`POST /v1/traces`), off unless switched on in Settings, Connectors, Traces (`traceReceiver.enabled`, port 4318 unless set), started with Codehouse. A port that is taken says so in the Host's log and nothing else stops.
- **Section 10 and invariant 7:** it listens on 127.0.0.1 only. A request needs a project's own token as a bearer token (`Authorization: Bearer`, which OTLP exporters send through `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_HEADERS`); tokens are kept only as hashes (compared in constant time), shown once when made, and a new one replaces the old. Any Origin header is refused, so no web page can post to it, as is a Host that is not this receiver, anything but POST /v1/traces, and anything but JSON (no protobuf). A body over 4 MB is refused by its length, and cut off as it streams when no length is given.
- Every attribute (resource and span) and every span name is redacted before a span is kept (invariant 12). The newest 20,000 spans of each project are kept, in memory only.
- The session's menu has "Traces..." (`TracesPanel`): whether the receiver is on, a button to make the project's token with the three environment variables to copy, the project's traces (root span, span count, duration, an error flag) and, for one trace, its span tree with each span's duration and place in the trace. Window messages `traces_list`, `trace_tree` and `trace_token` (recorded, never the token).
- Threat model T162. Tests: `apps/host/test/trace-receiver.test.ts` (on 127.0.0.1 only; no token or another's refused; Origin, another Host, protobuf and other paths refused; a 10 MB body refused, with a length and streamed; a fixture trace taken, its attributes redacted, rendered as a span tree, kept to its project; tokens kept as hashes and replaced; base64 ids and an orphan span).
- Eight breaks; one missed until a request stating 10 MB but sending less was added (the stream cap had masked it); all caught.
### E-041 Profiling on request (feat/e4a)

- `apps/host/src/profiler.ts`: the session's "Profile..." panel runs a command of the project once as a Codehouse action (judged by the rules, no credentials, at the session's level) with Node's own CPU profiler: `NODE_OPTIONS` gets `--cpu-prof --cpu-prof-dir=<a temporary folder>`, which writes a .cpuprofile when the process ends. The profile (the largest, when helper processes write their own) is read into the functions that took the most time themselves and with what they called (recursion counted once on each path), and a flame graph, drawn in the panel. The folder is deleted afterwards.
- **Section 10:** no inspector port is opened. The profiler flag only writes a file, and any `--inspect`, `--inspect-brk`, `--inspect-port` or `--debug` flag the environment carries in NODE_OPTIONS is taken out first.
- A command starting with `dotnet` goes through `dotnet-trace collect --format Speedscope` when dotnet-trace is on this computer, its speedscope profile read the same way.
- "Tell Claude" sends the session a summary of the top ten functions (window message `profile_run`; the prompt goes as any prompt you send). `runAction` now takes an environment to add, for this.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/profiler.test.ts`, with a real fixture: its hot loop is the top frame, with its file and line; no inspector is open in the profiled process even when NODE_OPTIONS asks for one (the fixture asks `inspector.url()` itself), and the inspector flags are taken out; a refused run or no profile says why and leaves no folder; dotnet-trace's command and speedscope file; a speedscope profile read.
- Two breaks, both caught.
### E-042 Memory leak detector (feat/e4a)

- `apps/host/src/leak-detector.ts`: "Look for leaks" in the Profile panel runs a command once as a Codehouse action with a small preload script (`--require`, written to a temporary folder) that takes heap snapshots with `v8.writeHeapSnapshot` at intervals while the program works (not at exit, where its objects may already be gone). Each snapshot is read into the size and count of objects by constructor, and the constructors whose size rose at every step (or all but one), by at least 64 KB and a tenth of where they started, with no drop larger than a quarter of their growth (a sawtooth is a cache filling and emptying), are reported, the largest growth first, with their counts at each snapshot. The folder and its snapshots are deleted afterwards. "Tell Claude" sends a summary.
- **Section 10:** no inspector port is opened: the preload only writes files, and any --inspect flag in NODE_OPTIONS is taken out first.
- **Recorded:** sizes are each object's own size summed by constructor, which a snapshot gives without building its dominator tree; growth there is what a leak looks like from outside. Snapshots over 400 MB are not read.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/leak-detector.test.ts`, with real fixtures: one that keeps a record on every request reports `LeakyRecord` with its rising count; one that lets go does not; inspector flags are taken out, and too few snapshots says why; growth counts only when it rises step by step and by enough.
- Three breaks, all caught. While testing, the fixture's list was rightly collected once nothing referenced it, and the snapshot at exit was unreliable, so it was dropped; the growth rule was tightened after a sawtooth passed.
### E-045 Crash summary (feat/e4b)

- `apps/host/src/crash-summary.ts` reads what a crash leaves: a Node diagnostic report (`report.*.json`, from `--report-on-fatalerror` or `--report-uncaught-exception`: the event, its message, its JavaScript frames, or its native frames when there is no JavaScript stack), a .NET "Unhandled exception." block with its frames, and a Windows crash code in an exit message (0xC0000000 and up, described as Codehouse's own crash notes describe them; a plain exit code is not a crash). Frames are mapped to source through E-039's mapper (maps inside the project only).
- In the Profile panel: "Crashes found" summarises the reports in the project's folder from the last week and the .NET crashes and crash codes in its log lines (E-038); "Run with crash reports" runs the command once as a Codehouse action with Node's report flags on (`NODE_OPTIONS`; reports only write files into a temporary folder, deleted afterwards, nothing listens, and inspector flags are taken out) and says how it ended. "Ask Claude to fix" sends the summary. A .NET dump in the project is read with `dotnet-dump analyze ... clrstack -all` only when dotnet-dump is on this computer (window message `crash_summary`).
- Tests: `apps/host/test/crash-summary.test.ts`: a real fixture's fatal error yields its top frame (`loadConfig`, its file and line) through Node's own report; a report left in the project's folder (out of memory, native frames only); a .NET unhandled exception with its frames, and Windows crash codes but not plain exits; the options open nothing; the dotnet-dump command. Seven breaks, all caught.
### E-043 Verified reproduction recipes (feat/e4b)

- `apps/host/src/repro.ts` and the session's "Reproduce a bug..." panel. From the bug's description Codehouse asks Claude for a minimal test in the project's own framework that fails now for the bug's reason, without fixing it yet. With the test's file and the command that runs only it, "Confirm it fails before the fix" runs it as a Codehouse action: a pass is rejected, since a test that passes before the fix does not reproduce the bug; a failure is recorded with the test's contents hashed. After the fix, "Confirm it passes after the fix" requires the same test, unchanged since it failed (a change is rejected: a pass would prove nothing), runs it, and when it passes locks it through D-007, recorded as `test_locked` with the reason. Each project's reproductions are kept in the data folder (`repros.json`). Window message `repro` (ask, before, after, list); only a test inside the project is taken.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/repro.test.ts` (a reproduction that passes before the fix is rejected; one that fails is accepted, a still-failing run after is refused, and a pass locks it; a test changed after it failed, or never confirmed failing, is refused; a test outside the project and a refused run). Six breaks: one missed until the outside case used a real file; all caught.
### E-044 Bisect assistant (feat/e4b)

- `apps/host/src/bisect.ts` and the session's "Find the bad commit..." panel: git bisect between a good and a bad commit with a check command (exit 0 is good), in a scratch worktree (`git worktree add --detach` in the temporary folder), so the bisect's refs and checkout are that worktree's own and your working tree, its HEAD and its uncommitted changes are never touched. `git bisect start` and `git bisect run <check>` run as Codehouse actions, judged by the rules and recorded; the run is recorded as `bisect_run`. The first bad commit is reported with its subject, the number of steps, and its Codehouse session note when it has one (D-030's `refs/notes/codehouse`). Commit names that could carry anything but a name are refused.
- **Junction-safe deletion:** the project's node_modules is linked into the worktree by a junction so the check can run. On the way out the bisect is reset, each link is removed by itself first (never followed), and only then is the worktree removed and git's record of it pruned, so nothing a link points to is touched (a recursive delete of a folder holding a junction can follow it on Windows; this happened to the main checkout while building on 22 Nov).
- Tests: `apps/host/test/bisect.test.ts`, on a real repository of eight commits: the seeded bad commit (change 5) is found; your HEAD, status and uncommitted change are the same afterwards, no bisect is in progress in your tree, what node_modules holds is still there, and no worktree or scratch folder is left; unsafe commit names are refused, and a refused check says so and still cleans up. Six breaks, all caught, including removing the worktree without unlinking first.
### E-049 Resource-aware scheduler (feat/e4b)

- `apps/host/src/scheduler.ts`: Codehouse's own heavy jobs wait in a priority queue until memory and CPU allow: claim checks (D-009), held-out and sealed checks, mutation runs, and the actions run on request (profiles, leak checks, crash runs, bisects, client drift). Each waiting job says why ("waiting for memory: 92% committed, 85% is the point", or the CPU's load), shown under "Waiting to start" in the Ports and processes panel (window message `scheduler_status`). Jobs start one at a time, highest priority first, then in order, so each reading sees what the last one uses. The CPU's load is sampled from the processors' times (os.loadavg says nothing on Windows).
- Following the owner's 16 Nov decision for the memory governor (C-004), it holds nothing unless the governor is switched on; then a job waits at or over the governor's point, or at 90% CPU.
- **It never delays approvals, the kill switch or Stop:** approvals and Stop do not pass through it; engaging the kill switch cancels every waiting job at once (through the switch's own interrupt path); Interrupt and Stop cancel their session's waiting jobs. A cancelled job is simply not run, and says why.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/scheduler.test.ts` (under simulated memory pressure a queued job waits, says why, and starts once memory frees; priority then order; the kill switch and Stop cancel what waits; nothing waits with the governor off; an approval through canUseTool is answered at once while a job is held; the CPU sampler); the claim, held-out, mutation and process tests still pass through the wrapped paths. Seven breaks: one missed, on an early return that duplicated what the queue does with the governor off, which was taken out; all others caught.
### E-050 Cross-session locks (feat/e4b)

- `apps/host/src/locks.ts`: a lock table in the Host for files, ports and named resources, in memory (locks last as long as the sessions do). A session takes a file's lock when it first writes the file (the PostToolUse `onWritten` hook), and a port's when a command of its starts a server on it (E-029's port detection, through a new `onCommandStarted` hook). Files are keyed by their full path, without case on Windows and the Mac.
- Another session's edit (Write, Edit, MultiEdit, NotebookEdit) of a locked file, or its server on a locked port, escalates in canUseTool under `cross-session-lock` at 50 or more, naming the session that holds it ("Another session (Shop fixes) is working on this file..."); the finding only raises a verdict. A person can let it go ahead.
- A session's locks are released when it ends or fails for good (in the session's own state change, so every way a session ends releases them). The Ports and processes panel lists what each session holds (window message `locks_list`).
- Tests: `apps/host/test/locks.test.ts` (a file taken once and named for another session, its path matched without case; a served port held and released with its session; and, on real sessions in one folder, a second session's write to a file the first wrote waits for a decision, naming the first, and the first's locks go when it ends). Eight breaks, all caught.
### E-055 Agent handover notes (feat/e4b)

- A new in-process Companion tool, `write_handover` (where the work stands, next steps, risks, open questions for the person, files changed), offered to coding sessions only and allowed by the rule `handover-tools`, with `mcpSource: [sdk]` and `sessionKinds: [code]`: a server of the same name from your settings is not the Companion, and a chat has none.
- `apps/host/src/handover.ts`: each file a note names is checked against the real change (the last commit against the working folder, untracked files included); one that has not changed is marked, in the reply to Claude and in the note itself, so a note cannot claim work that is not there. Notes are kept per folder in the data folder (`handovers.json`, the newest 20) and recorded (`handover_written`, with counts).
- The next coding session started in that folder receives the newest note it has not had, written in the last two weeks, once, in its instructions, introduced as the last session's account to check against the code (recorded as `handover_received`). A session brought back after a restart does not take it, nor does the session that wrote it. Every coding session is told to leave a note before it stops work, and the session's menu has "Ask for a handover note" (window message `handover_ask`) for pause, stop or end.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/handover.test.ts` (a note naming an unchanged file marks it, while changed and new files are not marked; the next session receives it once, not the writer, not another folder; a note over two weeks old is passed over); `apps/host/test/handover-session.test.ts` (on a real session a new session receives the note in its instructions, once, and every session is told to leave one); three golden cases for the rule in `07-mcp-unknown.yaml`. Seven breaks, all caught.
### E-053 Verified playbooks (feat/e4b)

- `apps/host/src/playbooks.ts`: playbooks in `.codehouse/playbooks/*.yml`, each a name, its steps and an end checklist. A checklist item can carry automatic checks: `changed` (files matching a glob changed), `exists` (a file inside the project is there), `contains` (a changed file contains some text) and `run` (a command passes, run as a Codehouse action); an item with none is for a person.
- The session's "Playbooks..." panel (window message `playbooks`): "Start" gives the session the playbook's steps and what will be checked; "Verify the checklist" checks each item against the real change (the last commit against the working folder, new files included) and lists each as met, unmet with why, or for a person. Recorded as `playbook_verified` with the unmet items.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/playbooks.test.ts` (playbooks read, one without a name left out; a run that misses the docs step lists it as unmet while tests, the changelog and lint are met and the review is for a person; with the docs changed it is met, and a failing command is unmet). Six breaks, all caught.
### E-048 Evidence gates between steps (feat/e4b)

- `apps/host/src/gates.ts`: a gate stands between one step and the next, which waits until it passes. Types: `tests: verified` (the session's latest claim that the tests pass was Verified, D-009), `sealed: true` (the project's held-out checks pass, run now, D-003), `mutation: N` (the session's latest mutation score is at least N, D-004; the server now keeps each session's latest score), `a11y: { report, baseline }` (no Serious or Critical violation in an axe JSON report the project's own tool wrote that the baseline does not have; Codehouse has no accessibility checker of its own, so it reads the project's report), and `dod: <playbook>` (the playbook's checklist, E-053, all met, with nothing left for a person).
- A gate that cannot be read fails closed, saying what is missing: no checked claim yet, no mutation score, a report that is missing, broken or has no violations list, no such playbook, or any error. `runGatedSteps` runs steps in order and stops after the step whose gate does not pass, with its reason; E-047's workflows run on it. A gate can also be checked now for a session (window message `gate_check`).
- Tests: `apps/host/test/gates.test.ts` (a failing gate stops the next step and gives its reason; each unreadable case fails closed; each type passes on good evidence and fails on bad, a score at the minimum included; gates read from a workflow file, and an unknown one refused). Seven breaks: one missed until the at-the-minimum case was added; all caught.
### E-046 Role-based agent teams (feat/e4b)

- `apps/host/src/roles.ts`: `.codehouse/roles.yml` defines roles (planner, implementer, tester, reviewer, documenter, or the project's own), each with a model, an effort, the paths it works in, the tools it may not use, and whether it is read-only. The session's "Team..." panel (window message `team`) starts a role as its own session in a worktree of its own, with its model and effort, told its role and task; a hand-off can carry an E-052 result schema, which the session is told to end with and Codehouse checks. Recorded as `role_started`.
- **Section 10, roles only restrict:** in canUseTool the role is an overlay applied after the rules. A tool the role may not use, or any edit by a read-only role, is denied (`role-tool`); an edit outside the role's paths waits for a person (`role-scope`). It never turns a deny into anything else and never allows. A role definition that sets anything that would add an allow (allow lists, auto-approval, a permission mode, rules, bypass or Extreme settings) or anything Codehouse does not know is refused as a whole.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/roles.test.ts` (roles read; each widening key and unknown keys refused; a tester's edit to source held while its tests are left alone, a denied tool and a reader's edits denied; through canUseTool the overlay only restricts, and a rules deny stays a deny in and out of the role's paths; on a real session a tester's edit to source waits for a person). Eight breaks: one missed until the rules-deny case outside the role's paths was added, which showed the guard against softening a deny is needed; all caught.
### E-047 Workflows as code (feat/e4b)

- `apps/host/src/workflows.ts`: `.codehouse/workflows/*.yml` defines a workflow's steps, each with a role from roles.yml (E-046), a prompt (with `{{task}}` and `{{step.path}}` from earlier steps' results), outputs (an E-052 result schema), a gate (E-048) and an optional approval point. Each workflow is validated against the project's roles before it can start: an invalid one is refused, with every problem named (an unknown role, a reference to a later step, a repeated id, no prompt, an unknown gate). A dry run lists what each step would do.
- The session's "Workflows..." panel (window message `workflow`): Start runs it from the session's folder. Each step's role runs as its own session in its own worktree (as E-046), to the end of its turn; a step with outputs must end with a JSON result matching its schema (sent back once when it does not, then the run stops); the gate is checked (failing closed); an approval point waits for your Approve. Every step's start, gate, end, approval, stop and resumption is recorded. The run is saved after each step (`workflow-runs.json` in the data folder), so after a restart each running workflow goes on from its last completed step.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/workflows.test.ts` (a three-step workflow completes with its gates, each step fed the last one's result, every step recorded; a failing gate stops it with the reason; a run saved mid-way resumes from the last completed step; the completed steps are saved when the Host dies mid-step; an approval point waits and goes on when approved; an invalid workflow is refused before it starts with every problem named, and a valid one dry-runs). Seven breaks: one missed until the dying-Host case was added; all caught.
### E-054 Night shift planner and morning report (feat/e4b)

- `apps/host/src/night-shift.ts` and the session's "Night shift..." panel (window message `night`): you queue the night's tasks in the session's folder, each with the tasks it depends on, its own cap and whether it runs with Extreme approval, plus the night's budget and a start time (or now). A night whose tasks depend on each other in a circle, or on a task not queued, is refused before anything starts.
- Tasks run in dependency order; one whose dependency did not finish is skipped. Each runs as a session through the scheduler (E-049), with its cap as the SDK's spend cap (`maxBudgetUsd`, D-074), never more than what is left of the night's budget, until it settles (idle for a minute, since Extreme carries on by itself, or failed or ended). No task starts once the night's budget is reached. Extreme approval keeps its own turn and spend limits and still stops for failure escalations and locked tests. Runs are kept in the data folder (`night-runs.json`).
- The morning report gives each task's status (done, failed, skipped, not run), its evidence (claims Verified, Contradicted and Unverified, D-009), its cost and the decisions still waiting for you, with the night's total and why it stopped.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/night-shift.test.ts` (dependent tasks run in order; a task whose dependency failed is skipped; the night's budget stops the run and each task is capped to what is left; the morning report shows each status, evidence, cost and open decisions; a circular or dangling dependency is refused). Seven breaks, all caught.
### E-062 Epic decomposition (feat/e5a)

- `apps/host/src/epic.ts` and the session's "Break down an epic..." panel (window message `epic`): Codehouse asks the session to break the epic's brief into stories (a title, the story, testable acceptance criteria, and the stories it depends on), as an E-052 result checked against a schema. "Write the stories into the ledger" writes each into the requirement ledger (D-033, `docs/requirements/<id>.md`, front matter the ledger reads) with a stable id: the epic's key (its words' initials) and four hex characters of a hash of the story's normalised title, so breaking the same epic down again keeps each id whatever the order; dependencies are written by id. A story already in the ledger is not overwritten. Recorded as `epic_decomposed`.
- Each story carries an estimate from the project's history (D-100: the cost and time per change over the last 90 days, as the 10th to 90th percentile with the sample size); with fewer than five samples it says there is not enough history, whatever figures exist.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/epic.test.ts` (a fixture epic yields stories with stable ids, criteria and dependencies by id, read cleanly by the ledger; ids stay the same in another order and a person's edit is not overwritten; the estimate only with enough samples; the epic's key; the result schema). Six breaks: one missed until the case of figures from too few samples was added; all caught.
### E-056 Work item linkage (feat/e5a, the safe variant)

- `apps/host/src/work-items.ts`: Azure DevOps ids (`AB#1234`) and Jira keys (`PROJ-123`; look-alikes such as UTF-8, ISO-8601 and SHA-256 left out) are found in the session's branch name, in the messages of the branch's own commits (since it left the default branch, or its last 200), in D-030's session notes and in the requirement ledger (D-033). They are linked to the session, the commits, the commits' evidence packs and sessions (from the notes) and the stories, kept locally per project (`work-items.json`, merged without repeats).
- The session's "Work items..." panel (window message `work_items`) lists each item with what it links to, and exports CSV or JSON; a CSV cell a spreadsheet would read as a formula is quoted and escaped.

Seven breaks, all caught.
### E-057 Definition of done checklist (feat/e5a)

- `apps/host/src/dod.ts`: a project's definition of done in `.codehouse/dod.yml` (a list, or `items:`), each item in E-053's checklist form: checked automatically where it says how (files changed, a file exists, the change contains some text, a command passes, run as a Codehouse action), and otherwise ticked by hand for the session in the "Definition of done..." panel (window message `dod`; each tick and untick recorded). A tick counts only for an item Codehouse cannot check itself: an item it can check is checked, never taken on trust.
- Every claim that the work is done (D-009) carries the definition's status (`dod`: met, total and what is not met), and its reason names the unmet items.

Six breaks, all caught.
### E-058 Release readiness report (feat/e5a)

- `apps/host/src/release-readiness.ts` and the session's "Release readiness..." panel (window message `release_readiness`): for a release candidate (the project's change since its default branch), area by area: the escalations still waiting in the project, the sealed checks (run now when the project has any, D-003), the candidate's commits with and without an evidence pack (D-030's notes), known vulnerabilities in the dependencies from the lockfiles at the project's root (D-057's snapshot), the licences of the direct npm dependencies against the allowed list (D-055), accessibility from the project's axe report (E-048) and the definition of done (E-057).
- It is ready only when nothing blocks. An open Severe escalation (85 or more), failing sealed checks, a known vulnerability, a licence not allowed, a new Serious accessibility violation or an unmet definition of done each make it not ready, with the reason. Waiting escalations below Severe and commits without an evidence pack are flagged to check, and what a project has not set up is said without blocking. The report copies as Markdown, and is recorded as `release_readiness`. The server's vulnerability snapshot dependency now includes its status, so a missing snapshot is "not set up", never "no vulnerabilities".
- Tests: `apps/host/test/release-readiness.test.ts` (ready when nothing blocks; an open Severe escalation makes it not ready with the reason, one below Severe only warns; each blocking area; what is not set up does not block, commits without packs warn, the Markdown). Six breaks, all caught.
### E-059 Change record and CAB pack (feat/e5a)

- `apps/host/src/change-record.ts` and "Draft the change record" in the session's Release readiness panel (window message `change_record`): an ITIL change record for the release candidate, field by field: description (the commits since the default branch and the requirements they name), risk (from the readiness report: High with an open Severe escalation, Low when nothing blocks, otherwise Medium), impact (the changed files and their areas), tests (checked claims Verified by running the checks again, D-009, and passing sealed checks, D-003), implementation, backout (E-060's last check) and schedule (what you type).
- Every field either names the evidence it rests on or is marked "Needs your input"; nothing is invented to fill a gap. No tested evidence, no backout check, a backout that is not reversible and no schedule each ask for your input. "Copy the CAB pack" copies the record followed by the readiness report, as Markdown.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/change-record.test.ts` (every field has evidence or asks for input; drafts from the evidence and asks for what it cannot know; High risk with an open Severe escalation, and an irreversible change needs a backout plan; the CAB pack). Four breaks, all caught.
### E-060 Backout plan verification (feat/e5a)

- `apps/host/src/backout.ts` and "Check the backout" in the session's Release readiness panel (window message `backout_check`): in a scratch worktree (E-044's, with the dependency folders linked and removed junction-safe afterwards), Codehouse reverts the change's commits (from where the project left its default branch) on top of where the project stands now, runs the project's tests as a Codehouse action, and rehearses the down steps of every migration the change adds (E-013).
- A change whose revert conflicts (named files), whose tests fail after it, whose tests could not be run, or whose new migrations cannot all go down is reported as not reversible, with the reason. Your working tree, branch and uncommitted files are never touched, and no worktree is left behind. The result is audited as `backout_checked` and feeds the change record's backout field (E-059).
- Tests: `apps/host/test/backout.test.ts` (a clean revert whose tests pass, with the tree left as it was; a revert that conflicts with later work on the same line; a revert after which the tests fail; nothing to revert). Four breaks, all caught.
### E-061 Environment promotion gate (feat/e5a)

- `apps/host/src/promotion.ts`: a project names its environments, the command that promotes a change to each, and their gates (E-048's: tests verified, sealed checks, a mutation score, accessibility, a definition of done) in `.codehouse/environments.yaml`. A Bash or PowerShell command with a part that runs a promote command goes to a person unless every gate of its environment passes, run then with the session's evidence. A failing gate, no gate set, or one that cannot be read escalates under `promotion-gate`: Severe (90) for an environment marked production (or named prod, production, prd or live), 70 otherwise, naming each failing gate.
- **Stricter decision, following the owner's 14 and 15 Nov decisions over the spec's "Extreme never approves a promotion to production":** a promotion to production goes to a person even when its gates pass (`production-promotion`, 80, the Rules-based Extreme limit), so Rules-based Extreme approval waits on every production promotion and only Extreme max approval answers one. Codehouse's own repository has no environments file, so the owner's unattended builds are not affected. For the owner to confirm.
- In Auto and Accept edits the floor hook asks about a promotion, so it reaches canUseTool and its gate (never an allow). The finding only raises a verdict. An environments file that cannot be read fails closed: each promote line in it counts as a production promotion that cannot pass. With no server to read the evidence (the command-line harness) a promotion cannot pass. Threat T163.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/promotion.test.ts` (production with a failing gate is Severe and Rules-based Extreme does not answer it; passing gates go to the rules, production still asks; a failing gate below production and an environment with no gate; matching only a part that runs the command; a broken file; canUseTool holds it even when the rules allow; the floor hook asks in Auto and Accept edits). Eight breaks, all caught.
### E-063 Effort and billing log (feat/e5a)

- `apps/host/src/effort-log.ts` (`effort.json` in Codehouse's data folder) and the session menu's "Effort and billing..." panel (window messages `effort_active`, `effort_export`, `effort_delete`): for each project and day, your active time, the agent's time and the cost. A minute counts once when you worked in one of the project's sessions in it: the window says so at most every half minute while you type or click in a session, and sending a prompt counts. The agent's time is each turn's duration. The cost is the change in each session's reported cumulative cost.
- It exports as CSV (date, project, human minutes, agent minutes, cost in USD, then a totals row), for the session's project or every project, to the window only. A project name that a spreadsheet would read as a formula is made plain text. A project's log, or all of it, can be deleted (audited as `effort_log_deleted`). It stays on this computer: the module only reads and writes its own file and reaches no network. The file is read tolerantly, and a damaged one starts again empty.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/effort-log.test.ts` (the exported totals of a fixture day, with a minute counted once, cumulative cost made into changes, a project filter and a date range; the file read back and deleted by project and in full; nothing leaves the machine: its imports, and those of the module it borrows the day from, reach no network). Seven breaks, all caught.

Another project's records never count.
