Eight breaks, all caught.
### E-066 Retention and legal hold, the safe variant (feat/e5a)

- `apps/host/src/retention.ts` and the session menu's "Retention and legal hold..." panel (window messages `retention_status`, `legal_hold_place`, `legal_hold_release`): how long Codehouse keeps evidence packs (D-003) and the log viewer's lines (E-038) follows the project's data classification (D-085): by default public and internal 90 and 7 days, confidential 90 and 3, restricted 60 and 1, with your own days by classification and kind in settings (`retention`). The earlier settings (`evidence.retentionDays`, `logRetentionDays`) still hold for public and internal, and never keep confidential or restricted data longer than its default.
- A legal hold on a project, or on every project, for evidence, logs or both, suspends deletion: what it covers survives past its retention until the hold is released. Holds are placed and released on the desktop only, each audited (`legal_hold_placed`, `legal_hold_released`), and kept in `legal-holds.json`; a hold file that cannot be read holds everything (fail closed). Evidence is now pruned at start and once a day, each deletion audited as `retention_deleted` with its age and project, and what a hold kept is logged. Deletion never follows or removes a link or a junction, and audit files are never deleted: retention does not know them as a kind.
- Not covered, and listed: audit retention (security and legal, after audit anchoring, D-029); traces (E-040) are in memory only and go when Codehouse closes; the transcripts Claude Code keeps are its own and Codehouse does not delete them; exports are saved where you choose.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/retention.test.ts` (a pack under legal hold survives past its retention and goes once released; an expired pack is deleted and audited; restricted data kept no longer than its classification allows; a junction named and dated like an expired pack, and its target, untouched; holds on every project and by kind; an unreadable hold file holds everything; a held project's log lines kept). Eleven breaks, all caught (two missed at first: the junction was not named like a pack, and one break was not a break; both fixed).
### E-081 Office files with redaction preview (feat/e5a)

- `apps/host/src/office.ts`, and three more formats in the Export dialog: Word (.docx), PowerPoint (.pptx) and Excel (.xlsx, the export's tables, numbers as numbers). The Host makes the file from exactly the text the preview shows, after the redaction list and secret patterns (W1-13) and the writing rules (E-086), and records the export with its format. Headings, paragraphs, bold, bullets, code and tables come across; PowerPoint gets a title slide and one a heading; Excel a sheet a table, or the text line by line.
- Each file carries the AI-generated mark ("AI-generated: made with Codehouse (Powered by Claude). Check it before relying on it.") as its first line, slide or cell, and in its document properties (description, and the keyword AI-generated). Characters XML does not allow are taken out. An older Host that cannot make Office files is told, not mistaken.
- **Deviation:** the spec asks for pinned pure JS libraries. Codehouse writes Office Open XML itself, in a zip made with Node's own zlib, so no dependency, licence or lockfile change is added. Checked by hand on this PC: the fixture's .docx, .pptx and .xlsx open in Word, PowerPoint and Excel (21 paragraphs and a table; 3 slides; the numbers read as numbers).
- Tests: `apps/host/test/office.test.ts` with `apps/host/test/ooxml.ts` (a zip read by its central directory with each CRC checked by Node's zlib.crc32, and each part well-formed XML): a fixture report's .docx opens with the mark first and in its properties; the .pptx and .xlsx open with the mark; characters XML does not allow are left out; a redaction term is masked in the Office file, which is the previewed text, and the export is recorded. Twelve breaks, all caught (three missed at first: a position check that passed with the mark gone, a CRC checked by the writer's own function, and a break that did not match; all fixed).
### C-059 XLSX attachments (feat/e5a, before E-085)

- `apps/host/src/xlsx-read.ts`: an Excel workbook read with Node's own zlib, no library (as G7 planned): its sheets' cells as text (shared and inline strings), numbers, booleans and formulas with the values Excel saved. Macros are never read or run (vbaProject.bin and every part but the workbook, its relationships, sheets and shared strings are ignored, and the reading says the workbook has macros), and external links are not followed.
- The reading runs in a worker thread with a 256 MB memory limit and a 20 second time limit, and caps the file (25 MB), each part's unpacked size (64 MB, so a zip bomb stops) and the cells (200,000) and sheets (50), saying what it left out. A chat project's .xlsx knowledge file gets a Markdown copy beside it (`<name>.xlsx.md`, a table a sheet, formulas with their saved values) for Claude to read, removed with it; a workbook that cannot be read gets a copy saying why.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/xlsx-read.test.ts` (sheets, strings, numbers, booleans and formulas; macros never read; a hostile file refused within its limits; reading in a worker; a chat project's Markdown copy that goes with its workbook). Nine breaks, all caught (one missed at first: the cell cap stopping only at a row's end; the test now pins the cells).
### E-085 Formula-first spreadsheets (feat/e5a)

- `apps/host/src/formulas.ts`: a workbook Codehouse exports (E-081) writes a table's totals as formulas, not pasted values. A row whose first cell says Total, Subtotal, Sum, Average, Mean, Min, Max or Count gets SUM, AVERAGE, MIN, MAX or COUNT over the data rows above it, column by column: those since the header or the last total, past any total rows just above it (a Total then an Average cover the same rows).
- Every formula is checked by a small evaluator built in here, so no formula engine is added, GPL or commercial (section 10). It supports cell references, ranges, numbers, + - * /, brackets, and SUM, AVERAGE, MIN, MAX and COUNT. A formula it can evaluate is stored with its value and marked verified; one outside the subset is marked "not verified" with the reason; one whose value differs from what the text said is kept as a formula and marked as a mismatch with both values. The workbook gets a "Formula checks" sheet listing them, and the Export dialog says when a total differs. Checked by hand on this PC: Excel shows the fixture's totals as =SUM(B3:B4) and =SUM(C3:C4), evaluating to 1500 and 1670.5.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/formulas.test.ts` (a fixture's totals are formulas that evaluate to the expected values, a subtotal covers only its rows and an average after it the same rows, a wrong total is a mismatch, the workbook holds formulas not pasted numbers; the export's workbook with its checks sheet; the evaluator's subset, and anything else not verified). Ten breaks, all caught.
### E-082 Meeting notes to backlog (feat/e5a)

- `apps/host/src/meeting-notes.ts` and the session menu's "Meeting notes to backlog..." panel (window message `meeting_notes`): from a meeting transcript in the project (plain text, Markdown, a Teams or Zoom .vtt, or an .srt, up to 5 MB), the decisions, the actions with their owners, and the user stories with their acceptance criteria, each linked to the line of the transcript it came from, as numbered on disk (cue numbers and timings are skipped, speakers kept).
- It reads the words as written, with no model: a decision is a line, or a sentence in one, that says Decision, Decided, Agreed or We agreed; an action says Action or TODO, or is a name then "to" a verb, or a speaker's "I'll"; a story is "As a ..., I want ... so that ...", and its criteria are the Given, When, Then, And or AC lines after it. Line keywords are never taken for a speaker's name.
- "Add to the backlog" writes each story into the requirement ledger (D-033) as a draft requirement (`docs/requirements/REQ-<title>.md`, with `source: <transcript>#L<line>` and a link to the line for itself and each criterion; a story with none says criteria are needed), and the decisions and actions into a meeting record beside the transcript (`<name>.notes.md`), each with its line. Nothing already there is overwritten, nothing is written outside the project, ids never clash with the ledger's, and the write is audited (`meeting_notes_written`).
- Tests: `apps/host/test/meeting-notes.test.ts` (every story, criterion, decision and action links to its line on disk; stories into the ledger as drafts that read back cleanly with their links, and the meeting record; nothing overwritten or written outside, and no id clash on a second run; plain text and .srt; criteria end at the first line that is not one). Twelve breaks, all caught (one missed at first: a story's criteria ending at the first line that is not one; a test added).
### E-083 Evidence-grounded documents (feat/e5a)

- `apps/host/src/grounding.ts`, in the export preview (W1-13): each sentence of a draft's prose is checked for a source: a link, a web address, a file in the project (a path, with or without a line, that is there), an evidence pack (D-003, by its id or by name), or a numbered citation ([1], [^1]) that the draft's own sources resolve (footnote definitions, or a numbered list under a Sources, References, Citations or Bibliography heading). A citation the sources do not resolve, or a path to a file the project does not have, is not a source.
- A sentence with none is ungrounded. The Export dialog lists them before you save ("N sentences have no source", each with its line) and highlights each in the preview, on its own line only. Headings, code blocks, tables, the sources list, questions and sentences under five words are not claims and are not checked. Saving is not blocked: the list is for you to act on.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/grounding.test.ts` (ungrounded sentences found with their lines and sourced ones passed; an unresolved citation is not a source; the export preview lists them before anything is saved or recorded) and `apps/web/test/export-grounding.test.tsx` (each ungrounded sentence highlighted on its line, and nothing else). Eleven breaks, all caught (three missed at first: fixtures too short to be checked, and a highlight test whose other lines lacked the sentence; all fixed).
### E-084 Sequence diagrams from code paths (feat/e5a)

- `apps/host/src/sequence.ts` and the session menu's "Sequence diagram from code..." panel (window message `sequence_diagram`): from an entry point in a TypeScript or JavaScript project (file#function or file#Class.method) and a depth (1 to 8, 3 by default), the calls are followed with Tree-sitter (C-031) and drawn as a Mermaid sequence diagram, saved as an artifact of the session and drawn offline in Artifacts (C-057), with its Mermaid to copy. Nothing is run.
- A call is resolved only when the code itself says what it reaches: a function in the same file or imported from a project file (named, default or namespace imports), a method on `this`, on a field whose class comes from the constructor's parameter properties, `this.x = new X()` or a field initialiser, on a local made with `new` or a typed parameter, or a class's static method. Each resolved call returns to its caller in the diagram. Anything else (a library, a value it cannot type) is drawn as a failed arrow to "Unresolved" and listed with its file and line, never guessed; the language's own built-ins (console, Math, JSON and the like) are left out. Recursion is followed once.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/sequence.test.ts` (a fixture route drawn with its service, pricing and repository calls in order, with returns; the calls it cannot resolve marked and listed, built-ins left out; the depth limit, a class method as the entry, and a missing entry named). Eleven breaks, all caught. The C-031 outline's 50 ms timing test fails on this PC at present (93 ms) with outline.ts unchanged: the machine is short of memory and paging.
### E-087 Research to decision brief (feat/e5a)

- `apps/web/src/lib/brief.ts` and "Draft a decision brief from these sources" in a chat's Sources pane: Codehouse asks the chat for a brief in a fixed shape (Context, Options with one heading each, Risks, Recommendation), listing the pane's sources by their numbers and asking each option to end with the sources that support it. The pane then checks the latest brief in the chat: every option must cite at least one source the pane lists, a citation the pane does not have is not a source (and is named), and every section must be there.
- A brief that passes says so; one that fails lists each option without a source from the pane, each citation the pane does not have, and each missing section, with "Ask Claude to fix it", which sends exactly those problems back. Only an answer after your latest message counts. Exporting the brief applies your writing rules (E-086) and the grounding check (E-083).
- Tests: `apps/web/test/brief.test.ts` (the prompt lists the shape and the pane's sources; every option checked against the pane, with an option without a source and an unknown citation named in the fix; a citation the pane lacks is no source, and every section is needed; the latest brief, not one from before your last message). Eight breaks, all caught (two missed at first: a brief missing only a section, and a citation in the Recommendation counted for the last option; tests added).
### E-092 Signed industry packs, the safe variant (feat/e5a)

- `apps/host/src/industry-packs.ts`, `policy/industry/*.yaml` and the session menu's "Industry packs..." panel (window messages `industry_packs`, `industry_pack_sign`, `industry_pack_install`, desktop only): four draft packs of rules and checklist items, for contact centres (payment descoping and call recording redaction), telecoms (traffic data, lawful intercept), energy (the Priority Services Register, disconnection and forced prepayment) and banking (credit decisions, payment runs; consumer outcomes). Each is marked as a draft for legal and subject expert sign-off, and ships in the policy folder the app already packages.
- A pack can only add: its rules are escalate and deny rules (an allow rule, or any key that removes, disables or overrides something, refuses the pack), and a new version is refused if it drops a rule or checklist item the installed one has, or turns a deny rule into one that only asks. A pack installs only once signed after your review: "Sign after review" needs who reviewed it and what they checked, and signs its rules and its checklist with this computer's key (audited as `industry_pack_signed`). Installing checks both signatures against the keys you trust (Settings, C-055's list; the panel says when this computer's key is not among them and copies it), writes the rules as a signed shared rule pack, which the policy loader checks again each time it loads, and the checklist beside them (audited as `industry_pack_installed`). The shared packs folder is now made at start, so the policy watches it and an installed pack takes effect without a restart.
- For legal and subject experts: the sign-off of each pack's content. The drafts name UK regulators' rules as examples and are not legal advice.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/industry-packs.test.ts` (four valid drafts that only add caution; an unsigned pack, one signed by a key you do not trust, and one changed after signing cannot be installed, and signing needs a review; signed and installed, every draft's rules load as a shared pack and decide its own cases; a pack that removes a rule or checklist item, softens a deny, allows anything or says it removes is refused, and one that only adds is installed). Ten breaks, all caught. Writing the test found the drafts' folder globs matched file names only (an edit inside a credit-decision folder was allowed); the globs now cover folders too.

The record holds counts and area names only, never code or your words; it stays on this computer, is never sent anywhere, and "Delete the record" deletes it (audited as `skills_deleted`).

### E-071 Teach-back check (feat/e6a)

- `apps/host/src/teach-back.ts` and the session menu's "Explain the change..." panel (window message `teach_back`): you explain the change in a few lines, and Codehouse compares your words with the change in git (the files changed since the work left its default branch, with what is not committed yet, and their lines added and removed). Each changed module (a file's first two folders, or the file at the top) that your explanation does not name, by a folder or one of its files' names in any spelling (case, dashes, underscores, singular or plural), is listed with its files and lines, the largest first; folder names every project has (src, lib, tests and the like) name nothing. A small change is said to be small.
- **Deviation:** the spec has a model compare the explanation with the diff, using a local model where available. Sending a diff to a model is for the data boundary rules (C-016, D-085) to allow, so this version makes the comparison it can check (every changed module named or listed) and sends nothing anywhere; comparing the meaning with a model on this computer is listed for later.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/teach-back.test.ts` (an explanation that misses a changed module lists it with its files and lines, largest first; a module named by its folder or a file, in any spelling, and project-wide folder names naming nothing; the change read from git, committed since the default branch and not yet committed). Eight breaks, all caught (two missed at first: a length filter that did nothing, now removed, and a fixture already in order; fixed).
### E-072 Tutor mode (feat/e6a)

- `apps/host/src/tutor.ts` and the session menu's "Start a tutor session..." (window message `tutor_start`): a new session on the project, in Manual mode, with the tutor overlay, told what you want to learn and to teach rather than do: explain, give hints a step at a time, ask leading questions, show small examples in its replies, and run the tests when you change something.
- The overlay can only restrict (like E-046's role scopes). An edit to any file that is not a test (Edit, Write, MultiEdit, NotebookEdit) is denied in canUseTool, and refused by the floor hook too, in every mode, so Accept edits and Auto cannot let it through; the floor deny is recorded. The agent may still read, run the tests and write tests. A command other than one plain test run (npm test, vitest, jest, pytest, dotnet test, go test, cargo test, Pester and the like, with nothing chained or redirected) goes to you.
- **Stricter, for the owner to confirm:** a deny that an overlay makes in canUseTool (a role, tutor mode, a lock and the like) after the Approver's verdict is now recorded in the audit log as `overlay_deny`; before, only the Approver's own verdict was. Threat T164.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/tutor.test.ts` (an agent Write to a source file is denied and recorded, a test can be written, and outside tutor mode the rules decide; the tests run and other commands go to you; the floor hook refuses it in Auto, Accept edits and Manual, recording each; the session is told to give hints, and what is a test file). Nine breaks, all caught (one break at first did not break what it named; fixed). Regression: the invariants, auto mode, roles, scope lease, promotion and IaC plan tests pass.

### E-091 Screen-share safe mode (feat/e6a)

- `apps/web/src/lib/screenshare.ts`, `apps/web/src/components/ScreenShare.tsx`, `packaging/Capture.cs` (`CaptureGuard`) and `packaging/CodehouseApp.cs`: a mode switched by Ctrl+Shift+H, the tray's "Screen-share safe mode", or nothing else, kept across a restart so a share is not exposed by one, with a "Screen-share safe mode is on" bar and Turn off while it is on. While it is on:
  - every word on your redaction list is masked wherever the window shows it, the transcript included and messages that arrive while sharing; what you type (inputs, text areas) is left alone; turning it off puts the words back;
  - the sessions you chose ("Hide this session while sharing" in the session menu) show only a line saying they are hidden;
  - notices that are not urgent (a session finished, a queue emptied) are held and told as one when the mode ends; requests for a decision still come;
  - pairing codes (Devices) and ntfy topics (Push) are not drawn;

Codehouse.exe compiles from them with the Windows compiler (checked). The Mac app has no equivalent window affinity yet; the page's masking, hiding and holding work there too.
- Tests: `apps/web/test/screenshare.test.tsx` (a redaction term masked in the transcript view, in any case, a later message too, a text area left alone, and everything put back; non-urgent notices held and urgent ones not; chosen sessions hidden only while it is on) and `apps/host/test/capture-guard-exe.test.ts` (through the window app's own code, built and run for real: a window gets WDA_EXCLUDEFROMCAPTURE as Windows reports it, and loses it; the quick prompt and pop-outs get it while the mode is on). Ten breaks, all caught (one missed at first: the text area in the test had no text node of its own; it does now). Regression: the notification kind and queue-empty tests pass.
### E-098 Upgrade rehearsal (feat/e6a)

- `apps/host/src/rehearsal.ts`, `apps/host/src/rehearse-run.ts`, `apps/host/src/update/service.ts`, `apps/host/src/main.ts` and Settings, Updates: before Codehouse switches to a downloaded release, it starts the new version against a synthetic data folder in the temporary folder. The folder holds copies of your settings and policy only (the settings file, your remembered and own rules and their limits, project overlays, shared rule packs), plus one sample open session the current version writes in its own format; never your audit log, sessions, transcripts, keys or secrets. The new version (its own Node and `main.mjs`, with `GATEHOUSE_REHEARSE=1`, every path pointed into the copy and no credential in its environment) starts nothing: it reads the settings, loads the policy, runs its invariant canaries (E-100) and plans the sample session's restore, prints one line with each step and exits.
- When every step passes, Codehouse switches as before. When one fails, or the new version does not report in time, Codehouse stays on the current version, Updates says which step failed and why, and "Switch to … anyway" is there, so an update can never be stuck; "Update now" chosen before the version was ready meets the same rehearsal and leaves the version ready. Each rehearsal is audited (`update_rehearsed`), and so is switching anyway (`update_switched_anyway`). A release whose files cannot be found to rehearse is not held back. The synthetic folder is deleted afterwards.
- **Updates never break:** an install of the last release does not rehearse (it has no rehearsal), so its update to this version is unchanged; the rehearsal applies from this version on, always with Switch anyway. The packaging script, the bundle and the program names are unchanged (the rehearsal runs through `main.mjs`). `scripts/update-e2e.ts` was not run on this PC: it packages the app twice, which this PC cannot do while it is short of memory; it is for the next release check. Windows only: the Mac's update service (`service-mac.ts`) does not rehearse yet.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/rehearsal.test.ts` (a version that reads the settings, loads the policy, passes its canaries and can restore sessions passes; one whose policy does not load fails, saying why; the real data folder is untouched, the copy holds only settings, policy and the sample session, every path given is inside it, no API key passes, and it is deleted; a release that cannot be found is not held back and one that does not report fails; the sample session) and `apps/host/test/update.test.ts` (a failed rehearsal keeps you on this version and the release ready, with Update now too, until you switch anyway; a passed or skipped one switches). Checked by hand: the rehearsal through the real `main.ts` passed in 17 seconds and left nothing running. Twelve breaks, all caught (one missed at first: Update now before the version was ready; a test added). Regression: the 38 earlier update tests pass.

### G26 Sandbox: the outgoing-connection proxy (feat/e6a, the safe variant)

- `apps/host/src/egress-proxy.ts`: when switched on in Settings, Connectors, Sandbox (off by default, the 9 Nov connector rule), a session starts with HTTP_PROXY and HTTPS_PROXY pointing at a proxy on 127.0.0.1. Only the hosts on your allow list get through: `example.com` for that host, `*.example.com` for its subdomains, and a `:port` for any port other than 80 and 443. If you name an upstream proxy (an http:// address with no user name or password), everything let through goes by it. Each refusal is audited as `egress_blocked`, with the session, host, port and reason.
- Invariant 7, as a proxy keeps it:
  - It listens on 127.0.0.1 only.
  - Each session gets its own random token as the proxy password. Only its hash is kept, and it stops working when the session closes.
  - A request with an Origin header is refused, and so is one that is neither a CONNECT nor a full URL. That is everything a page rebinding a name to 127.0.0.1 can send, so this stands in for the Host check.
  - HTTPS is only tunnelled, never read.
- Claude Code's own hosts (anthropic.com, claude.ai, claude.com and their subdomains) and this computer go round it by NO_PROXY, so a list cannot cut a session off from its model.
- The setting applies live: the proxy reads it on each connection, and new sessions pick it up. A session started while it was off goes direct until it restarts. One started while it was on is refused everything once it is switched off, until it restarts. Both are said beside the setting.
- **Deviation, and what it is not:** G26 also asked for writes outside the workspace to be denied at the OS level, and for an unlisted host to be unreachable whatever the program does. That needs a restricted token or an AppContainer launch, which is native code beyond what this laptop allows without an administrator (no firewall rules, Windows Sandbox or WSL). The proxy stops tools that honour the proxy settings (git, npm, curl, pip, most SDKs, and Claude Code's WebFetch), not a program that opens its own sockets. Writes outside the workspace stay with the floor and the policy, as before. G26 stays open for that part.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/egress-proxy.test.ts`. It covers:
  - the allow list's matching
  - the upstream address, with credentials refused
  - off by default, with no variables and nothing listening
  - the session variables and NO_PROXY
  - a CONNECT to an allowed host and a refusal audited with its session
  - no token, a wrong token, and a closed session's token
  - an Origin header and an origin-form request
  - plain http:// forwarding without the proxy's token
  - by the upstream proxy, refused when it says no or carries a password
  - refused once switched off
  - a real session pointed at it and released on close

  Threat model T165. Eighteen breaks, all caught (one missed at first: listening on every interface; a test of the address added).

### Fix: secret-in-commit refused every push from C:\dev\peter (24 Nov)

- **What happened:** every push from the P.E.T.E.R. session was refused for "a password in a web address in scripts/update.sh, line 496", even when the push did not touch that file and after the file was reworded.
- **Cause, from C:\dev\peter and the audit log:**
  - A push was judged on every commit on every local branch no remote had (`git log --branches --not --remotes`). Commit 71a1293 on the local branch deploy-alignment (its GitHub branch gone) adds `DATABASE_URL=postgres://peter:<POSTGRES_PASSWORD>@db:5432/peter` at line 496 of its own version of the file.
  - A commit counted as a push when "push" appeared anywhere after git, so the refused commits, whose messages said "push", read the same.
  - The pattern took the placeholder for a password.
  - Removed lines were never read, and nothing was cached; the constant line was that other commit's.
- **Fix (`apps/host/src/commit-scan.ts`):**
  - A push is judged only on the lines added by the commits it sends: `<remote>/<branch>..<local>`; the current branch to its upstream when no refspec is given; for a branch new to the remote, what that remote has nowhere; every branch for --all or a pattern.
  - A push is a push only where push is git's subcommand; `git -C <folder> push` is judged in that folder.
  - A placeholder in a web address is not a password (`<...>`, `$VAR`, `${VAR}`, `%VAR%`, `{{...}}`, `***`, REPLACE_WITH_..., CHANGE_ME, your_..., changeme, example, xxx, password). A real-looking one is still refused, beside a placeholder too.
  - A push finding names its commit, since the line is that commit's version of the file.
  - Removing a secret, or rewording it away, is not refused. Pushing a commit that added a real-looking one still is, since that history would reach GitHub.
- **Tests:** `apps/host/test/commit-scan.test.ts`, seven new cases covering the side branch, rewording and removal, a fresh reading after a change, a commit message that says push, placeholders against a real-looking password, removed lines, and reading refspecs and `-C`. Its addresses are put together at run time, so the file holds none. Eight breaks, all caught (one equivalent mutant at first, taking out one of the two guards on removed lines; rerun with both).

### G38 The test explorer (feat/e6a)

- `apps/host/src/test-explorer.ts` and Tests... in a session's menu (`TestExplorerPanel.tsx`): the project's tests a file at a time, with each test's line, for Vitest, Jest, pytest and dotnet test, found by reading the files (describe names prefix their tests, a pytest class gives Class::test, .NET methods marked [Fact], [Theory], [Test], [TestCase] or [TestMethod]). Listing runs nothing.
- Run, for every test, a file or one test, starts the project's own runner as you would in the terminal: your action, so it does not pass the Approver, but:
  - it is audited first as `tests_run`, and refused when the record cannot be written;
  - it is refused while the kill switch is engaged, and for a file outside the project;
  - it runs one at a time per project and is stopped after ten minutes;
  - it uses the project's installed Vitest or Jest with this Node (never npx, so nothing is downloaded), `python -m pytest` or `dotnet test`, without a shell and with no credential in its environment;
  - a test name reaches the runner as an escaped pattern.
- Results come from the runner's own report (Vitest and Jest JSON, pytest's JUnit XML, dotnet's TRX). Each failure carries its message and its line, from the stack where it names the test file, else the test's own line. Open at line N shows the file there; Fix this puts a scoped task in the prompt box ("fix the code, change only what this failure needs, do not weaken, skip or delete the test"), with the failure fenced so it cannot close its own block. Nothing is sent until you send it.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/test-explorer.test.ts` (reading JavaScript, Python and C# tests; finding runners and files, leaving out installed packages; the command, the project's own runner and never npx; the three report formats; a run audited first with the right arguments; the kill switch, an unrecorded run and a file outside; one at a time and the time limit) and `apps/web/test/test-explorer.test.tsx` (listing and running, results, a failure opened at its line, Fix this unsent, the fence, a runner that reported nothing). Twenty-four breaks, all caught. Threat model T166.

### G34 Language servers you have installed (feat/e6a)

- C-032 already gave TypeScript and JavaScript their language service. G34's other half is here: a language server you have installed (pyright, gopls, rust-analyzer, clangd and the like) for files of its kind in the file view.
- Off until you name one in Settings, Connectors, Language servers, one to a line: name | file endings | the command and its arguments, quoted where they hold spaces. A line without all three is left out, and the field says so.
- `apps/host/src/lsp-client.ts`:
  - It starts the server in the session's folder, without a shell and with the credentials stripped from its environment.
  - It talks the Language Server Protocol over stdin and stdout: the file's problems as the server publishes them, what is at the cursor (hover, as plain text), and where a name is defined or used.
  - Only places inside the session's folder are passed on, from the folder.
  - A server that does not answer in twenty seconds is stopped and starts again at the next look; one idle for ten minutes is stopped, and all stop with Codehouse.
  - It only reads: nothing a server says is applied to a file. A message too large, or a header that never ends, stops it.
- The route `/api/files/lsp` answers as `/api/files/ts` does, and the file view shows the same panel, labelled Language server, under a file a server is named for. Under any other file it shows nothing.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/lsp-client.test.ts`, with a small fake server, covers off until named; problems, what is at the cursor, a definition with the place outside the folder left out, and references, started in the folder without a credential; a hung server stopped and started again; and the protocol's framing, positions and hover text. `apps/web/test/language-servers.test.tsx` covers the panel for a named server, nothing without one, and the Settings lines. Thirteen breaks, all caught. Threat model T167.

### G37 The debugger, through the Debug Adapter Protocol (feat/e6a)

- `apps/host/src/dap-client.ts`: a DAP client for adapters you have installed. Off until you name one in Settings, Connectors, Debug adapters, one to a line: name | python, node, dotnet or other | the command. debugpy, netcoredbg and other stdio adapters are talked to over stdin and stdout; an adapter that listens (js-debug's dapDebugServer.js) gets "{port}" in its arguments, Codehouse chooses the port and reaches it on 127.0.0.1. Never the Chrome DevTools Protocol, and not Microsoft's vsdbg.
- Debug... in a session (`DebugPanel.tsx`): start a program from the session's folder with its arguments and breakpoints (file:line a line each), then Continue, Step over, Step into, Step out, Pause, Stop. A stop shows the stack (a place in the folder opens there; one outside has no path) and the top frame's variables. Nothing is evaluated in the program: no expressions, no changing a variable.
- Claude has the same through the Companion (`debug_start`, `debug_set_breakpoints`, `debug_step`, `debug_state`, `debug_stop`, for coding sessions when an adapter is named). Its tools answer once the program stops or ends (up to 30 seconds), in plain text.
  - `debug_start` runs a program, so it always asks you (new rule debug-start, from any source).
  - The others act only on what was started, through the Host's own Companion in coding sessions (rule debug-tools).
- Every action is audited first as `debug_action` with who took it (you, or Claude through the Companion); a start is refused when it cannot be recorded or while the kill switch is engaged. Only a program and breakpoints inside the session's folder are accepted. The adapter starts without a shell and with the credentials stripped. An adapter's request to run something (runInTerminal, startDebugging) is refused. The stack, variables and output are redacted. A closed session's program is stopped: disconnect, then the adapter's process tree half a second later.
- **Acceptance, checked here:** a real breakpoint in Python hits through the debugpy that VS Code's Python debugger installs (the test finds it, and skips where it is not installed). Node and .NET could not be checked on this PC: there is no standalone js-debug server and no .NET SDK or netcoredbg. The client speaks the same protocol to them, and the fake adapter covers the port connection js-debug uses.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/dap-client.test.ts` (a fake adapter: the stop, redaction, the launch, the order of breakpoints and configurationDone, a refused runInTerminal, stepping and the end, each action audited with who; the kill switch, an unrecorded start, an adapter not named, a program or breakpoint outside; an adapter on a port, on 127.0.0.1; no credential; no step while running; Claude's tools and their text; the real debugpy breakpoint), `apps/companion/test/handlers.test.ts` (the tools reach the debugger; nothing to evaluate), golden 07-mcp-unknown (debug-start asks; debug-tools for the Host's Companion in coding sessions only) and `apps/web/test/debug-panel.test.tsx`. Twenty breaks, all caught (three missed at first: credentials, stepping a running program, and the panel's buttons while running; tests added). Threat model T168.

### G40 Sharing a project as a zip, and publishing with a host's own tool (feat/e6a)

- `apps/host/src/share.ts`, Share and publish... in a session (`SharePanel.tsx`):
  - **Share as a zip.** It takes the files git would (what .gitignore leaves out stays out), or outside git the folder less its build and package folders. It also leaves out:
    - files named like secrets: .env and its kind, though .env.example and other templates are kept; keys and certificates; private SSH keys; .npmrc, .pypirc and .netrc; credentials and service-account files; Terraform state; folders of credentials (.ssh, .aws, .azure);
    - any text file in which the commit scan (G22) finds a secret.

    The panel names what is left out, and why, before the zip is made. A note inside the zip, SHARED-BY-CODEHOUSE.txt, names each file and why, never a value. The share is audited as `project_shared`, and no zip is made when it cannot be recorded. The zip is limited to 5000 files and 200 MB, and each file to 50 MB.
  - **Publish.** It runs a command you name in Settings, Connectors, Publish (name | command), for example `Netlify | netlify deploy --dir dist --prod`. That is the host's own command line tool, signed in its own way; Codehouse keeps none of its passwords or tokens, as the 9 Nov connector rule asks.
    - It is your action from the window: audited first as `project_published` (refused when it cannot be recorded), refused while the kill switch is engaged, run in the session's folder without an Anthropic credential, its output redacted and shown, stopped after ten minutes.
    - On Windows, a tool installed as a .cmd file runs through cmd.exe with Settings' words only, and a word holding a character cmd.exe treats specially (& | < > ^ % ! ") is refused.
    - Claude has no tool for either; a deploy command in a session still goes through the policy as before.
- **Not built (the backlog's wider G40):** app templates, local SQLite and sign-in scaffolding, and keeping secrets in Credential Manager. The frontier row asked for host tools with their own sign-in and the zip without secrets, which is what is here.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/share.test.ts` covers:
  - secret names, with templates kept;
  - git's view with secrets left out by name and by their text, and the folder walk outside git;
  - the zip and its note, never a value, recorded first;
  - a publish in the folder without a credential, output redacted, recorded first;
  - the kill switch, an unrecorded publish, an unknown name, and the time limit;
  - cmd.exe for a .cmd tool, refusing special characters.

  `apps/web/test/share-panel.test.tsx` covers what is left out shown first, the download, a publish and its output, no host named, and the Settings lines. Seventeen breaks, all caught (one, a note showing a value, caught by the mutation failing rather than a test reading the note; the note's own test checks that no value is in it). Threat model T169.

### O48 The menu structure, re-evaluated (feat/e6a)

- The review is `docs/design/navigation-review.md`, made with Design with Intent (https://designwithintent.ai/): its /organize skill for the structure and its /evaluate shape for the findings (by severity, each with what happens, why it matters, what it breaks and the change). Verdict: significant issues. Four are major:
  - most pages hidden behind More;
  - no sense of where you are, nor of the pages beside the one you are on;
  - the same page reached from different menus in different ways;
  - the Chats or Code switch hiding whole pages.

  Intent is written for phones first; its tab bar is read here as the sidebar of a desktop window.
- Built:
  - `lib/sections.ts`: five main sections, each page in exactly one:
    - Work: Dashboard, All sessions, Board, Usage;
    - Ask about code: New question, Projects, Memory, Prompts;
    - Build: MCP servers, Marketplace;
    - Safety: What Claude may do, Approver, Audit log;
    - Settings: Settings, Phone and notifications, Doctor, User guide.
  - The sidebar lists the five sections always, the current one marked, under Needs you, New session (or New question) and Search. Each section opens on the page last used in it. The Chats or Code switch now only chooses which sessions are listed.
  - `SectionPills.tsx`, shown above every page with a section (App.tsx, one line): the section's pages as pills along the top, the current one filled. It is a navigation landmark named for its section with one tab stop; the arrow keys, Home and End move between pills, wrapping round.
  - The profile menu keeps your accounts, the theme and help. More, the pinned pages and Edit sidebar are gone. A choice an older version saved is still read and simply not used, so an update cannot break on it.
- Kept as they were: Needs you with its count; the session list; Search and commands (Ctrl K), which still reaches every page; Settings' own 14 sections with their search; a session's bar and menu.
- Help (Using Codehouse) says where each page now lives. Impeccable could not be run here: its package is not installed, and npx would have downloaded it, which is not done for a check. No Intent check is defined in the repository beyond the review itself.
- Tests:

### O50 Ask about code reads its session without asking (feat/e6a)

- The owner, 25 Nov: "It should never require permission to query a code session. I only want approval when it offers a prompt into the session."
- **The cause, from the audit log:** `read_session` had been held for approval 36 times since 8 Nov, the last at 12:44 today on 2.0.635, as no-rule. Yet the rule `session-read-tools` allows it, and its conditions were met: the Companion's source sdk and the chat's session kind reach the Approver.
  - The installed `app\policy\rules.yaml` dated from 2 Nov: 58 lines against 102 in the source, with no `session-read-tools`, nor any other rule added since (the handover, test-impact, routine-draft, debugger and assumption rules among them).
  - Each update's `Codehouse-Update.exe` (`Updater.cs`, `CarryYourFiles`) carried the old policy folder over the new one, seed rules and all. So every install kept the seed rules it was first installed with, and nothing told anyone.
  - So this was not only Ask about code: every rule added to `rules.yaml` since an install never reached it.
- **The fix, on the Host's side so it works whichever version's updater ran:**
  - The package keeps a copy of its seed rules in `app\policy-seed\rules.yaml`, outside the folder an update carries.
  - At start the Host (`seed-rules.ts`) puts that copy in place when `app\policy\rules.yaml` differs. It keeps the replaced file beside it as `rules.yaml.kept-<date>`, logs it, and audits it as `seed_rules_refreshed`.
  - Your remembered decisions (`rules.local.yaml`) and your own rules (`rules.user.yaml`) are carried as before and never touched. So is a rules file named elsewhere.
  - The package's README now says `rules.yaml` comes with each version.
  - Choice made for the owner: nothing in Codehouse writes `rules.yaml`, and your own rules belong in `rules.user.yaml`, so the shipped file wins and a hand edit is kept beside it rather than lost.
- **With the shipped rules in place:**
  - Ask about code's `read_session` is allowed by `session-read-tools` in every mode, and reading files under your workspace roots by `read-workspace`.
  - `offer_to_session`, a prompt into the session, still always asks you (O34: `session-handoff`, Severe, and the Host lets no rule, grant or Max approval answer it).
  - Golden 07-mcp-unknown checks both.
- **The next update applies it.** Your copy has the 2 Nov rules until then: the first start of the new version puts the shipped ones in place.
- Checked: `npm run package:check` (the seed copy ships, identical to `policy/rules.yaml`) and `scripts/update-e2e.ts` from the installed 2.0.635 (it switches and starts). Tests: `apps/host/test/seed-rules.test.ts` (an older copy replaced and kept; left alone when it matches; made when missing; a rules file elsewhere and a folder with no seed copy untouched; your own rules untouched). Four breaks, all caught. Threat model T170.

- The owner, 25 Nov: "I want as little drift between the mac and windows version as possible. Mac features will always take the priority when it comes to menu layout, themes and style." Every place the window differs on a Mac (`isMac()`, `onMac()` and the Mac stylesheets, 40 places) was listed and sorted.
- **Brought to Windows:**

  - The tray grouped as the Mac's status menu: open and the quick prompt; then what shows and how Codehouse starts, with **Open at sign-in** added as the Mac's Open at Login (it makes or removes the Startup shortcut the installer makes); then quit.
- **Kept as each system's own:** key names (⌘ or Ctrl); where secrets are sealed (the Keychain or DPAPI) and what the memory reading means; zsh or PowerShell; Dictation or voice typing; the menu bar, the notch wing and the Mac's standard menus (Codehouse, Edit, View, Window, Help), which Windows apps do not have; Title Case or sentence case in menus; SF Pro and SF Mono or Segoe and Cascadia; the Mac's scrollbars.
- **For the Mac's next pass:** Screen-share safe mode and Start in safe mode, which the Windows tray has and the Mac's menu does not yet (docs/mac.md).
- The rule is recorded in CLAUDE.md, "Two machines", and docs/mac.md, "The same look and layout on Windows", so a look, layout or menu change on either platform is made on both. This edits Mac files (`mac.css`, `mac-standard.css`, `DisclosureMac.tsx`) from the Windows machine, as the 18 Nov rule allows; the Mac's gate runs after the merge.
- Checked: `npm run package:check` (the tray compiles and the packaged copy works) and `scripts/update-e2e.ts` from the installed 2.0.637.
- Tests:
  - `apps/web/test/mac-parity.test.tsx`: the chevron; Ctrl+comma and the sheet.

  - `look.test.ts` and `appearance.test.tsx`: always the Mac's look, with no choice offered.

  Seven breaks, all caught. Three missed at first: two had Windows line endings in a file without them, and one only hid a label. A fourth, Open at sign-in left out of the menu, needed the test to require the item's add.

### The Mac: the notification's buttons, locking, sleep, wake and power, and a folder from the Finder (feat/mac-notify-power-finder)

- **Mac: Deny and Allow once on the notification, locking, sleep, wake and power as they happen, and a folder from the Finder (owner, 25 Nov: "Lets do 2 3 and 4").** Described in `docs/mac.md` ("What it does that a browser tab does not"). No new dependency. **Decided by me, for the owner to confirm:** Allow once is offered on a notification only for a permission below High (risk under 70) that is not default to no, and asks for the Mac to be unlocked; High and Severe, questions and plans open the window. An answer from the notification is recorded on the desktop channel, as the wing beside the notch's is, so the audit keeps its channels and an older copy can still read it after an update is undone. A low battery (on battery at 20% or less) pauses Max approval sessions even with "Pause Max approval sessions on battery" off, and lets the Mac sleep; both come back on mains (stricter). Tests: notify-mac (Host and page), presence-mac, mac-events-mac, broker, power, quick. Break checks: the page allowing High, the Host offering Allow once for a default-to-no request, and the broker telling the phone twice each fail them.

### O57 Slow down fast approvals is a setting, off by default (feat/o57)

- Owner, 26 Nov: no more "You approved several requests faster than they could be read..." slow-down, as a setting switched off for him. ADR 0021, threat model T172.
- **The setting**: Settings, Safety (a new section), "Slow down fast approvals" (`slowFastApprovals`, off unless `true`, applied at once without a restart; the cards are told through health at once).
- **On**: D-021 and U4 as they were. **Off**: the Host never refuses an approval for its speed or asks for a deliberate second one (`server.ts`, "approve"), whichever way it came (the card, a row, a notification's button, the phone, Neighbourhood Watch), so no friction message is shown anywhere; and the card's one-second hold on Allow at High risk goes too (`ApprovalCard.tsx`).
- **Still recorded**: the detector keeps counting; off, each run it finds is written to the audit log as `approval_fast_run` (rule `rubber-stamp`, with the risk, how long the card showed and whether it was opened), and counting starts again.
- **Unchanged**: Severe still needs allow typed (and from CNW a signed-in session); two key presses to allow High or Severe by keyboard (D-092, a guard against stray keys, not speed); floor denies; Ask about code prompts into a session still ask; Extreme approval and every invariant.
- **Chosen**: off for every copy that never set it, as the owner asked, rather than on with the owner switching it off (a Host's settings file is not written from outside the app, and only the two owners use Codehouse); U4's hold goes with it, as the owner asked never to be made to wait; D-092's two presses stay.
- Tests: `apps/host/test/server.test.ts` (on: refused, the deliberate approval after two seconds, Severe typed allow at once; off: seven fast High approvals all through, two runs recorded, no friction); `apps/web/test/approval-card.test.tsx` (the hold on and off; Severe still asks to type allow); `apps/web/test/settings-autosave.test.tsx` (the setting saved on, and off as no setting); `settings-search.test.tsx` (the Safety section). Eight breaks, all caught.

- **The screens** (`apps/web/src/components/Phone.tsx`):
  - A tab bar at the foot in place of the sidebar: Needs you (with the count waiting), Sessions (with the count working), Ask, More. A tab keeps its place (O54's last session on each side); the tab you are on goes back to its list.

Each browser call fails in 20 seconds rather than hanging.

It opens only the demo screens on 127.0.0.1 and is never shipped.

### O52 Single sign-on for Neighbourhood Watch (feat/e6a)

- **OpenID Connect** (`apps/cnw/src/sso.ts`, `sso-routes.ts`), with **openid-client** 6.9.1:
  - the authorization code flow with PKCE (S256), state and nonce;
  - the provider found by discovery from its issuer URL;
  - the ID token's signature checked against the provider's keys (`enableNonRepudiationChecks`, since openid-client checks signatures only when asked), and its issuer, audience, expiry and nonce.
  - The issuer URL, client id and client secret are set on CNW's Server page, Single sign-on. The secret is sealed like the TOTP secrets (`Sealer`, `seal.key`), never sent back, and replaced only when a new one is typed.
- **SAML 2.0**, with **@node-saml/node-saml** 5.2.0 (passport-saml's core; its signatures are checked by xml-crypto):
  - signed assertions required (`wantAssertionsSigned`), CNW's entity ID as the audience, the IdP's issuer;
  - `InResponseTo` a request CNW made, and replays refused;
  - the IdP's metadata from a URL (https, ten seconds, kept an hour) or a file. **@xmldom/xmldom** 0.8.15 (already node-saml's own) reads only its entity ID, sign-in address and signing certificates, and refuses a DOCTYPE.
  - CNW checks no XML signature itself.
- **Who it is:** an identity matches a CNW person by the claim chosen in settings:
  - **email**: refused when the provider says it is unverified;
  - **subject**: linked once from **You** while signed in, and never one already on another account.
  - Nobody is made and no role is given unless the owner switches it on; then the role chosen, never owner.
- **Beside the rest:** passwords, codes and passkeys keep working. A second factor on the account is asked for after single sign-on too.
  - A single sign-on is not a passkey sign-in (`passkey: false`), and its session starts as not having just signed in (`Sessions.start(..., { confirmed: false })`). So a Severe allow asks for the password (and code) or a passkey again.
  - `/api/me` now says so (`severeReady`).
- **The cookies:** the session cookie stays SameSite=Strict. The sign-in's state is kept on the server under a random id, in `cnw_sso`: HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Lax, Path=/api/sso, ten minutes, used once by the callback and cleared.
  - SAML's POST from the IdP is cross-site and carries no cookie, so it signs nobody in. It checks the assertion, keeps the identity with its flow, and sends the browser on to `/api/sso/saml/finish`, a same-site request the Lax cookie comes with, which must name the same flow.
  - That one address, `/api/sso/saml/acs`, is let past CNW's origin and CSRF checks, as `/agent` is.
- **Recorded:** every sign-in (`sso-sign-in`), account made (`sso-user-made`), link (`sso-linked`), settings change (`sso-settings`) and refusal (`sso-failed`, with the reason, never a token) is in CNW's audit log and its log. Failures from an address are throttled as password failures are.
- **Kept and backed up:** settings in `sso.json`, now in the backup's files.
- The page (`public/cnw.js`):
  - the sign-in page has a button for each kind, beside the password and passkeys, and says why a single sign-on was refused;
  - **You** has Link;
  - the Server page has the settings and the three addresses to give the provider.
- Docs: `docs/neighbourhood-watch.md`, Single sign-on, with Authentik, Keycloak and Entra ID, each for OpenID Connect and SAML. Threat model T171.
- **Dependencies (why):** openid-client 6.9.1 (and its oauth4webapi 3.8.9 and jose 6.2.18), the maintained reference OpenID Connect client for Node, by jose's author. @node-saml/node-saml 5.2.0, the maintained SAML 2.0 core, so CNW writes no XML signature checking (the owner asked for a well-maintained library). @xmldom/xmldom 0.8.15, node-saml's own DOM parser, named so CNW can read the IdP's metadata. All are pinned in `apps/cnw/package.json` and the lockfile, which CNW's image installs with `npm ci`. `npm audit` found no vulnerabilities.
- On this PC, `npm install` replaced the worktree's `node_modules` junction with a partial folder of its own. The new packages were moved into the shared `node_modules`, with jose 6.2.18 nested under openid-client so nothing else's jose changed, and the junction was put back.
- Tests:
  - `apps/cnw/test/sso.test.ts` runs against an OpenID provider and a SAML IdP started by the tests, with keys and a certificate made at run time (@peculiar/x509 and xml-crypto, already installed), so no secret is kept. It covers:
    - the whole OpenID Connect sign-in, with the Lax and Strict cookies, `severeReady` false until the password is given again, and the audit;
    - a token signed with another key, for another audience, expired, or with the wrong nonce;
    - no cookie, another state, a reused flow;
    - an unverified email, nobody made, made as the chosen role and never owner;
    - linking by subject, with the password still working;
    - the sealed secret;
    - the SAML sign-in through its same-site finish, and another browser refused;
    - an unsigned assertion, another key, another audience, a replay, and an assertion carried into a new sign-in;
    - the metadata, with a DOCTYPE and no certificate refused;
    - matching.
  - `apps/cnw/test/sso-page.test.ts` covers the sign-in page's buttons and the refusal shown once.
  - All 18 CNW test files pass. Twenty breaks, all caught. Two were missed at first: a flow left unconsumed, since the replayed code was refused by the provider anyway, so the test now brings a fresh code for the same flow; and the settings left out of the backup, now tested through a real backup.
